## I. Death Brings Opportunity / 一 死亡带来机遇

===Original===
Linford Pratt, senior clerk to Eldrick & Pascoe, solicitors, of Barford, a young man who earnestly desired to get on in life, by hook or by crook, with no objection whatever to crookedness, so long as it could be performed in safety and secrecy, had once during one of his periodical visits to the town Reference Library, lighted on a maxim of that other unscrupulous person, Prince Talleyrand, which had pleased him greatly. “With time and patience,” said Talleyrand, “the mulberry leaf is turned into satin.” This seemed to Linford Pratt one of the finest and soundest pieces of wisdom which he had ever known put into words.

A mulberry leaf is a very insignificant thing, but a piece of satin is a highly marketable commodity, with money in it. Henceforth, he regarded himself as a mulberry leaf which his own wit and skill must transform into satin: at the same time he knew that there is another thing, in addition to time and patience, which is valuable to young men of his peculiar qualities, a thing also much beloved by Talleyrand—opportunity. He could find the patience, and he had the time—but it would give him great happiness if opportunity came along to help in the work. In everyday language, Linford Pratt wanted a chance—he waited the arrival of the tide in his affairs which would lead him on to fortune.

Leave him alone—he said to himself—to be sure to take it at the flood. If Pratt had only known it, as he stood in the outer office of Eldrick & Pascoe at the end of a certain winter afternoon, opportunity was slowly climbing the staircase outside—not only opportunity, but temptation, both assisted by the Devil. They came at the right moment, for Pratt was alone; the partners had gone: the other clerks had gone: the office-boy had gone: in another minute Pratt would have gone, too: he was only looking round before locking up for the night. Then these things came—combined in the person of an old man, Antony Bartle, who opened the door, pushed in a queer, wrinkled face, and asked in a quavering voice if anybody was in.

===Chinese===
林福德·普拉特（Linford Pratt）是巴福德（Barford）镇埃尔德里克—帕斯科律师事务所（Eldrick & Pascoe）的高级文书。这个年轻人一门心思想往上爬，为此可以不择手段；至于歪门邪道，他更是毫无异议——只要能做得安全隐秘就行。他每隔一阵子要去镇上的参考图书馆跑一趟，其间偶然读到另一位毫无道德原则的人物——塔列朗亲王（Prince Talleyrand）——的一句箴言，大为受用。“只要有时间和耐心，”塔列朗说，“桑叶就能变成绸缎。”在林福德·普拉特看来，这是他平生所知被诉诸文字的箴言里，最精妙、最可靠的一句。

桑叶是顶不起眼的东西，绸缎却是值钱的紧俏货，里头藏着真金白银。从此，他把自己看作一片桑叶，要靠自己的机敏和本事把它变成绸缎；与此同时他也明白，对他这种特别素质的年轻人来说，除了时间和耐心，还有一样东西必不可少——那同样是塔列朗的心头好——机遇。耐心他拿得出来，时间他有的是——可若是机遇肯来搭把手，那才叫称心快意。说白了，林福德·普拉特就盼着一个机会——他正等着自家时运的潮水涌来，好把他一路托上富贵。

且由他去吧——他心里盘算着——到时可千万要赶在潮头正满时下手。普拉特哪里知道，就在某个冬日的傍晚，当他站在埃尔德里克—帕斯科事务所的外间办公室里时，机遇正沿着外面的楼梯慢慢爬上来——来的还不光是机遇，还有诱惑，魔鬼在一旁给这两位都帮了腔。它们来得正是时候，因为普拉特正独自一人：两位合伙人走了，其他文书走了，办公室的小听差也走了，再过一分钟普拉特自己也要走了——他正准备四下看一眼，好锁门下班。就在这时，这两样东西到了——化身成一个老头子，安东尼·巴特尔（Antony Bartle）。他推开门，探进一张皱巴巴的怪脸，声音发颤地问里面还有没有人。

===Original===
“I’m in, Mr. Bartle,” answered Pratt, turning up a gas jet which he had just lowered. “Come in, sir. What can I do for you?”

Antony Bartle came in, wheezing and coughing. He was a very, very old man, feeble and bent, with little that looked alive about him but his light, alert eyes. Everybody knew him—he was one of the institutions of Barford—as well known as the Town Hall or the Parish Church. For fifty years he had kept a secondhand bookshop in Quagg Alley, the narrow passageway which connected Market Street with Beck Street. It was not by any means a common or ordinary secondhand bookshop: its proprietor styled himself an “antiquarian bookseller”; and he had a reputation in two Continents, and dealt with millionaire buyers and virtuosos in both.

Barford people sometimes marvelled at the news that Mr. Antony Bartle had given two thousand guineas for a Book of Hours, and had sold a Missal for twice that amount to some American collector; and they got a hazy notion that the old man must be well-to-do—despite his snuffiness and shabbiness, and that his queer old shop, in the window of which there was rarely anything to be seen but a few ancient tomes, and two or three rare engravings, contained much that he could turn at an hour’s notice into gold. All that was surmise—but Eldrick & Pascoe—which term included Linford Pratt—knew all about Antony Bartle, being his solicitors: his will was safely deposited in their keeping, and Pratt had been one of the attesting witnesses.

===Chinese===
“我在，巴特尔先生，”普拉特应声答道，把刚拧小的煤气灯又拧大了些。“请进，先生。有什么能为您效劳的？”

安东尼·巴特尔喘着气、咳着嗽走了进来。他是个老得不能再老的老人，孱弱伛偻，通身上下看着还有活气的，只剩一双灵动警觉的眼睛。镇上人人都认得他——他是巴福德的一处“名胜”，同市政厅、教区教堂一样家喻户晓。五十年来，他一直在夸格巷开着一爿旧书店，那是连通马克特街和贝克街的一条窄巷。这爿店可绝不是寻常的旧书铺：店主自称“古董书商”，名声远扬两大洲，欧美的百万富翁买主和大藏家都同他有生意往来。

巴福德人有时会对这样的新闻啧啧称奇：安东尼·巴特尔先生花两千几尼买了一本《时祷书》（Book of Hours），转手又把一部《弥撒书》（Missal）以双倍的价钱卖给了某位美国收藏家。于是人们隐隐约约地认定：这老头子鼻烟渍斑斑、一身寒酸，家底想必殷实得很；他那爿古怪的老铺子——橱窗里除几册古旧大部头和两三幅珍版版画之外难得摆出别的东西——里头必定藏着多少物件，只消一个钟头工夫就能点化成金。这些都不过是揣测——可埃尔德里克—帕斯科事务所——这个名号自然包括林福德·普拉特——对安东尼·巴特尔知根知底，因为这爿事务所就是他的律师：他的遗嘱安然存放在他们手里，普拉特便是见证遗嘱签署的人之一。

===Original===
The old man, having slowly walked into the outer office, leaned against a table, panting a little. Pratt hastened to open an inner door.

“Come into Mr. Eldrick’s room, Mr. Bartle,” he said. “There’s a nice easy chair there—come and sit down in it. Those stairs are a bit trying, aren’t they? I often wish we were on the ground floor.”

He lighted the gas in the senior partner’s room, and turning back, took hold of the visitor’s arm, and helped him to the easy chair. Then, having closed the doors, he sat down at Eldrick’s desk, put his fingers together and waited. Pratt knew from experience that old Antony Bartle would not have come there except on business: he knew also, having been at Eldrick & Pascoe’s for many years, that the old man would confide in him as readily as in either of his principals.

===Chinese===
老人慢吞吞地走进外间，靠在一张桌子上，微微喘着气。普拉特赶紧去开一扇内门。

“到埃尔德里克（Eldrick）先生房间里来吧，巴特尔先生，”他说，“那儿有一把舒服的安乐椅——来，坐进去歇歇。那楼梯够呛人的，是不是？我常盼着我们这办公室要是设在底层就好了。”

他点亮了高级合伙人房间里的煤气灯，回身挽住来客的胳膊，扶他坐进安乐椅。随后他掩上房门，在埃尔德里克的书桌前坐下，十指对拢，静候来意。普拉特凭经验知道，老安东尼·巴特尔若非有事，绝不会登门；他还知道——他在埃尔德里克—帕斯科事务所干了这么多年——老头子对他掏心窝子，同对两位合伙人中的任何一位没什么两样。

===Original===
“There’s a nasty fog coming on outside,” said Bartle, after a fit of coughing. “It gets on my lungs, and then it makes my heart bad. Mr. Eldrick in?”

“Gone,” replied Pratt. “All gone, Mr. Bartle—only me here.”

“You’ll do,” answered the old bookseller. “You’re as good as they are.” He leaned forward from the easy chair, and tapped the clerk’s arm with a long, claw-like finger. “I say,” he continued, with a smile that was something between a wink and a leer, and suggestive of a pleased satisfaction. “I’ve had a find!”

===Chinese===
“外头起了股讨厌的雾，”一阵咳嗽过后，巴特尔说道。“它钻我的肺，接着又闹得我心脏不好。埃尔德里克先生在吗？”

“走了，”普拉特答道，“都走了，巴特尔先生——就剩我一个。”

“你就行，”老书商答道，“你跟他们一样顶事。”他从安乐椅里欠身向前，用一根长长的、鹰爪似的手指敲敲文书的胳膊。“我跟你说，”他接着道，脸上挂着一种介于眨眼示意和睨视之间的笑，透着一股得意的满足。“我捡着宝贝了！”

===Original===
“Oh!” responded Pratt. “One of your rare books, Mr. Bartle? Got something for twopence that you’ll sell for ten guineas? You’re one of the lucky ones, you know, you are!”

“Nothing of the sort!” chuckled Bartle. “And I had to pay for my knowledge, young man, before I got it—we all have. No—but I’ve found something: not half an hour ago. Came straight here with it. Matters for lawyers, of course.”

“Yes?” said Pratt inquiringly. “And—what may it be?” He was expecting the visitor to produce something, but the old man again leaned forward, and dug his finger once more into the clerk’s sleeve.

“I say!” he whispered. “You remember John Mallathorpe and the affair of—how long is it since?”

“Two years,” answered Pratt promptly. “Of course I do. Couldn’t very well forget it, or him.”

===Chinese===
“哦！”普拉特应道。“又是您的什么珍本吧，巴特尔先生？两便士收来的东西，转手要卖十几尼？您可是有福气的人，您哪！”

“才不是那回事！”巴特尔呵呵笑道。“再说了，年轻人，我这点学问也是花钱买来的——谁都没白得。不——这回我是真发现了样东西：就在半个钟头前。我揣着它径直奔这儿来了。当然喽，是律师管的事儿。”

“是吗？”普拉特探询道。“那么——敢问是什么东西？”他正等着来客掏出什么物件，不料老头子又欠身向前，把那根手指再度戳进文书的衣袖。

“我说！”他压低嗓门。“你还记得约翰·马拉索普（John Mallathorpe），还有那桩——到如今多久了？”

“两年，”普拉特应声答道。“当然记得。那事儿想忘也忘不掉，那个人也一样。”

===Original===
He let his mind go back for the moment to an affair which had provided Barford and the neighbourhood with a nine days’ sensation. One winter morning, just two years previously, Mr. John Mallathorpe, one of the best-known manufacturers and richest men of the town, had been killed by the falling of his own mill-chimney. The condition of the chimney had been doubtful for some little time; experts had been examining it for several days: at the moment of the catastrophe, Mallathorpe himself, some of his principal managers, and a couple of professional steeplejacks, were gathered at its base, consulting on a report. The great hundred-foot structure above them had collapsed without the slightest warning: Mallathorpe, his principal manager, and his cashier, had been killed on the spot: two other bystanders had subsequently died from injuries received. No such accident had occurred in Barford, nor in the surrounding manufacturing district, for many years, and there had been much interest in it, for according to the expert’s conclusions the chimney was in no immediate danger.

Other mill-owners then began to examine their chimneys, and for many weeks Barford folk had talked of little else than the danger of living in the shadows of these great masses of masonry.

===Chinese===
一时间，他的思绪回到了那桩当年轰动巴福德全镇及四乡、热闹了好一阵子的旧事。恰好两年前的一个冬晨，约翰·马拉索普先生——全镇最有名的厂商之一、数一数二的富人——被自家工厂倒塌的烟囱砸死了。那烟囱的状况此前一阵子就悬而未决；专家们已一连查验了好几天：大祸临头的那一刻，马拉索普本人、几位主要经理人，还有两名职业高空作业工人，正聚在烟囱底下商议一份报告。他们头顶上那座百英尺高的大构筑物毫无半点征兆地坍塌下来：马拉索普、他的经理长和出纳主任当场殒命；另有两名旁观者后来也伤重不治。多年以来，巴福德乃至周边整个制造业地区都没出过这样的事故，此事自然引起了莫大的关注——尤其因为，照专家们先前的结论，那烟囱根本并无迫在眉睫的危险。

随后，其他厂主纷纷开始查验自家的烟囱，一连好几个星期，巴福德人张口闭口谈的几乎全是住在这些庞然砖石建筑阴影底下的危险。

===Original===
But there had soon been something else to talk of. It sprang out of the accident—and it was of particular interest to persons who, like Linford Pratt, were of the legal profession. John Mallathorpe, so far as anybody knew or could ascertain, had died intestate. No solicitor in the town had ever made a will for him. No solicitor elsewhere had ever made a will for him. No one had ever heard that he had made a will for himself. There was no will. Drastic search of his safes, his desks, his drawers revealed nothing—not even a memorandum. No friend of his had ever heard him mention a will. He had always been something of a queer man. He was a confirmed bachelor. The only relation he had in the world was his sister-in-law, the widow of his deceased younger brother, and her two children—a son and a daughter. And as soon as he was dead, and it was plain that he had died intestate, they put in their claim to his property.

===Chinese===
可没过多久，人们便又有了新的谈资。这谈资也是从那场事故里生出来的——对于林福德·普拉特这类吃法律饭的人，它尤其意味深长。据人们所知、所能查证，约翰·马拉索普死时没有留下遗嘱。镇上没有哪位律师替他立过遗嘱。别处也没有哪位律师替他立过遗嘱。谁也没听说过他自己动手立过遗嘱。遗嘱压根儿不存在。把他的保险柜、书桌、抽屉翻了个底朝天，一无所获——连张字条都没有。他的朋友谁也没听他提起过遗嘱。他向来是个颇为古怪的人，终身未娶，一辈子打光棍。他在这个世界上唯一的亲眷，便是他那位寡居的弟媳——他亡故的弟弟的遗孀——和她的两个孩子，一儿一女。所以他一死，等到没立遗嘱这事确凿无疑，他们便对遗产提出了主张。

===Original===
John Mallathorpe had left a handsome property. He had been making money all his life. His business was a considerable one—he employed two thousand workpeople. His average annual profit from his mills was reckoned in thousands—four or five thousands at least. And some years before his death, he had bought one of the finest estates in the neighbourhood, Normandale Grange, a beautiful old house, set amidst charming and romantic scenery in a valley, which, though within twelve miles of Barford, might have been in the heart of the Highlands. Therefore, it was no small thing that Mrs. Richard Mallathorpe and her two children laid claim to. Up to the time of John Mallathorpe’s death, they had lived in very humble fashion—lived, indeed, on an allowance from their well-to-do kinsman—for Richard Mallathorpe had been as much of a waster as his brother had been of a money-getter. And there was no withstanding their claim when it was finally decided that John Mallathorpe had died intestate—no withstanding that, at any rate, of the nephew and niece. The nephew had taken all the real estate: he and his sister had shared the personal property. And for some months they and their mother had been safely installed at Normandale Grange, and in full possession of the dead man’s wealth and business.

===Chinese===
约翰·马拉索普留下的家业相当可观。他挣了一辈子的钱。他的生意规模不小——厂里雇着两千名工人。工厂每年平均利润以千计——少说也有四五千镑。去世前几年，他还在附近置下了数得着的好产业之一——诺曼代尔庄园（Normandale Grange），一座秀美的老宅，坐落在一条山谷里，四外风光旖旎如画；那地方离巴福德虽然不过十二英里，却恍若身处苏格兰高地的腹地。所以，理查德·马拉索普太太（Mrs. Richard Mallathorpe）和她的两个孩子所主张继承的，可不是一星半点的家当。在约翰·马拉索普去世之前，他们一家过得十分寒素——实际上，就靠那位殷实亲眷按期发放的津贴度日——因为理查德·马拉索普（Richard Mallathorpe）败家的本事，同他哥哥挣家的本事不相上下。待到最终裁定约翰·马拉索普死时未立遗嘱，他们这份主张便再无人能够阻挡——无论如何，做侄子和侄女的这份主张是阻挡不了的。侄子继承了全部不动产；动产则由他同妹妹二人分享。几个月来，姐弟俩连同母亲已然稳稳当当地住进了诺曼代尔庄园，把死者的财富和产业尽数收入掌中。

===Original===
All this flashed through Linford Pratt’s mind in a few seconds—he knew all the story: he had often thought of the extraordinary good fortune of those young people. To be living on charity one week—and the next to be legal possessors of thousands a year!—oh, if only such luck would come his way!

“Of course!” he repeated, looking thoughtfully at the old bookseller. “Not the sort of thing one does forget in a hurry, Mr. Bartle. What of it?”

Antony Bartle leaned back in his easy chair and chuckled—something, some idea, seemed to be affording him amusement.

“I’m eighty years old,” he remarked. “No, I’m more, to be exact. I shall be eighty-two come February. When you’ve lived as long as that, young Mr. Pratt, you’ll know that this life is a game of topsy-turvy—to some folks, at any rate. Just so!”

===Chinese===
这一切在几秒钟里从林福德·普拉特脑中闪过——事情的原委他了然于胸：他不止一次掂量过那两个年轻人天大的造化。上个星期还在仰人鼻息，下个星期就成了每年几千镑进项的合法主人！——唉，这样的运气怎么就轮不到他头上呢！

“当然记得！”他又说了一遍，若有所思地打量着老书商。“这种事可不是说忘就忘的，巴特尔先生。可它怎么样了呢？”

安东尼·巴特尔往安乐椅背上一靠，咯咯地笑了起来——好像有什么东西、什么念头，让他觉得分外有趣。

“我八十岁了，”他说。“不对，确切说还不止。到二月我就满八十二了。等你活到我这个岁数，年轻的普拉特先生，你就会明白，人这辈子就是一场颠倒乾坤的游戏——反正对有些人来说是这样。就是这么回事！”

===Original===
“You didn’t come here to tell me that, Mr. Bartle,” said Pratt. He was an essentially practical young man who dined at half-past six every evening, having lunched on no more than bread-and-cheese and a glass of ale, and he also had his evenings well mapped out. “I know that already, sir.”

“Aye, aye, but you’ll know more of it later on,” replied Bartle. “Well—you know, too, no doubt, that the late John Mallathorpe was a bit—only a bit—of a book-collector; collected books and pamphlets relating to this district?”

“I’ve heard of it,” answered the clerk.

“He had that collection in his private room at the mill,” continued the old bookseller, “and when the new folks took hold, I persuaded them to sell it to me. There wasn’t such a lot—maybe a hundred volumes altogether—but I wanted what there was. And as they were of no interest to them, they sold ’em. That’s some months ago. I put all the books in a corner—and I never really examined them until this very afternoon. Then—by this afternoon’s post—I got a letter from a Barford man who’s now out in America. He wanted to know if I could supply him with a nice copy of Hopkinson’s *History of Barford*. I knew there was one in that Mallathorpe collection, so I got it out, and examined it. And in the pocket inside, in which there’s a map, I found—what d’ye think?”

===Chinese===
“您总不至于专为跑来跟我说这个吧，巴特尔先生，”普拉特道。他是个顶讲实际的小伙子：每天午饭无非面包加干酪、外加一杯淡啤酒，晚上六点半准时开饭，晚饭后的时光也都安排得妥妥帖帖。“这个道理我早懂了，先生。”

“是，是，不过往后你会更明白的，”巴特尔答道。“这个——你八成也知道，已故的约翰·马拉索普有点儿——就那么一点儿——收藏书的癖好；专收跟本地区有关的书册和小册子？”

“听说过，”文书答道。

“那批藏书原先搁在他厂里的私人办公室，”老书商接着说，“新主人接手产业后，我劝他们把书卖给了我。数量不算多——拢共约莫一百来卷——可里头有什么我都要。那些书他们本就毫无兴趣，便卖给了我。那是几个月前的事了。我把书全堆在一个角落里——直到今天下午之前，压根儿没正经翻检过。恰巧——今天下午的邮班——送来一封信，写信的是个如今远在美国的巴福德人。他问我能不能供他一册品相好的霍普金森（Hopkinson）的《巴福德史》（*History of Barford*）。我记得马拉索普那批藏书里就有一部，便把它取出来查验。结果，在封底内页的夹袋里——就是装地图的那个夹袋——我发现了一样东西——你猜是什么？”

===Original===
“Couldn’t say,” replied Pratt. He was still thinking of his dinner, and of an important engagement to follow it, and he had not the least idea that old Antony Bartle was going to tell him anything very important. “Letters? Banknotes? Something of that sort?”

The old bookseller leaned nearer, across the corner of the desk, until his queer, wrinkled face was almost close to Pratt’s sharp, youthful one. Again he lifted the claw-like finger: again he tapped the clerk’s arm.

“I found John Mallathorpe’s will!” he whispered. “His—will!”

Linford Pratt jumped out of his chair. For a second he stared in speechless amazement at the old man; then he plunged his hands deep into his trousers’ pockets, opened his mouth, and let out a sudden exclamation.

===Chinese===
“猜不着，”普拉特答道。他心里还惦记着晚饭，惦记着饭后的要紧约会，做梦也没想到老安东尼·巴特尔要告诉他什么了不得的大事。“信件？钞票？诸如此类的东西？”

老书商隔着书桌的一角又凑近了些，直到他那张皱巴巴的怪脸几乎贴上普拉特那张精明年轻的脸。他又举起那根鹰爪似的手指，又敲了敲文书的胳膊。

“我发现了约翰·马拉索普的遗嘱！”他悄声道。“他的——遗嘱！”

林福德·普拉特从椅子上蹦了起来。有那么一秒钟，他目瞪口呆地死盯着老头子；随即双手深深插进裤袋，张大了嘴，猛地迸出一声惊呼。

===Original===
“No!” he said. “No! John Mallathorpe’s—will? His—will!”

“Made the very day on which he died,” answered Bartle, nodding emphatically.

“Queer, wasn’t it? He might have had some—premonition, eh?”

Pratt sat down again.

“Where is it?” he asked.

===Chinese===
“不会吧！”他说。“不会吧！约翰·马拉索普的——遗嘱？他的——遗嘱！”

“就立在他死去的那个当天，”巴特尔答道，重重点着头。

“邪门儿，是不是？他兴许有了什么——预感，嗯？”

普拉特重新坐了下来。

“它在哪儿？”他问。

===Original===
“Here in my pocket,” replied the old bookseller, tapping his rusty coat. “Oh, it’s all right, I assure you. All duly made out, signed, and witnessed. Everything in order, I know!—because a long, a very long time ago, I was like you, an attorney’s clerk. I’ve drafted many a will, and witnessed many a will, in my time. I’ve read this, every word of it—it’s all right. Nothing can upset it.”

“Let’s see it,” said Pratt, eagerly.

“Well—I’ve no objection—I know you, of course,” answered Bartle, “but I’d rather show it first to Mr. Eldrick. Couldn’t you telephone up to his house and ask him to run back here?”

“Certainly,” replied Pratt. “He mayn’t be there, though. But I can try. You haven’t shown it to anybody else?”

“Neither shown it to anybody, nor mentioned it to a soul,” said Bartle. “I tell you it’s not much more than half an hour since I found it. It’s not a long document. Do you know how it is that it’s never come out?” he went on, turning eagerly to Pratt, who had risen again. “It’s easily explained. The will’s witnessed by those two men who were killed at the same time as John Mallathorpe! So, of course, there was nobody to say that it was in evidence. My notion is that he and those two men—Gaukrodger and Marshall, his manager and cashier—had signed it not long before the accident, and that Mallathorpe had popped it into the pocket of that book before going out into the yard. Eh? But see if you can get Mr. Eldrick down here, and we’ll read it together. And I say—this office seems uncommonly stuffy—can you open the window a bit or something?—I feel oppressed, like.”

===Chinese===
“就在我这口袋里，”老书商答道，拍了拍他那件锈旧的外衣。“放心，手续齐备着呢，我向你担保。该立的立了，该签的签了，见证也在场了。样样都合规矩，我心里有数！——因为很久很久以前，我也跟你一样，当过律师行的文书。我这一辈子，起草过的遗嘱、见证过的遗嘱可多了。这一份我从头到尾一字不落都读过了——没问题。什么也推翻不了它。”

“给我看看吧，”普拉特急切地说。

“嗯——我没意见——你，我自然是信得过的，”巴特尔答道，“可我更愿意先给埃尔德里克先生过目。你能不能打电话到他府上，请他跑回这儿来一趟？”

“当然可以，”普拉特答道。“不过他未必在家。我可以试试。您没给别的什么人看过吧？”

“没给任何人看过，也没跟任何人提过一个字，”巴特尔说。“跟你说吧，从我发现它到这会儿，还不到半个钟头。这份文件不长。你知道它为什么一直没露出来吗？”他接着说，热切地转向又站起身来的普拉特。“这很好解释。这份遗嘱的两名见证人，正是同约翰·马拉索普一道罹难的那两个人！所以，自然就没有人知道它的存在。依我看，他和那两个人——高克罗杰（Gaukrodger）和马歇尔（Marshall），他的经理长和出纳主任——是在事故发生前不久签的字，随后马拉索普出门去院子之前，随手把它塞进了那本书的夹袋里。嗯？这样吧，看你能不能把埃尔德里克先生请回来，咱们一块儿读读它。哎，我说——这办公室闷得邪乎——你能不能把窗户开条缝什么的？——我觉得胸口发闷，堵得慌。”

===Original===
Pratt opened a window which looked out on the street. He glanced at the old man for a moment and saw that his face, always pallid, was even paler than usual.

“You’ve been talking too much,” he said. “Rest yourself, Mr. Bartle, while I ring up Mr. Eldrick’s house. If he isn’t there, I’ll try his club—he often turns in there for an hour before going home.”

He went out by a private door to the telephone box, which stood in a lobby used by various occupants of the building. And when he had rung up Eldrick’s private house and was waiting for the answer, he asked himself what this discovery would mean to the present holders of the Mallathorpe property, and his curiosity—a strongly developed quality in him—became more and more excited. If Eldrick was not at home, if he could not get in touch with him, he would persuade old Bartle to let him see his find—he would cheerfully go late to his dinner if he could only get a peep at this strangely discovered document. Romance! Why, this indeed was romance; and it might be—what else? Old Bartle had already chuckled about topsy-turvydom: did that mean that—

===Chinese===
普拉特打开了一扇临街的窗。他朝老人瞧了一眼，只见那张素来苍白的脸，比往常更加没有血色了。

“您话讲得太多了，”他说。“歇一歇吧，巴特尔先生，我去给埃尔德里克先生府上挂个电话。他要是不在家，我再试试他的俱乐部——他回家前常去那儿消磨一个钟头。”

他从一扇专用门出去，到电话间去了；电话间设在楼里各家房客共用的门厅里。他给埃尔德里克的私宅拨了号，等着回音的当儿，心里盘算起这一发现对马拉索普家产的现任主人们意味着什么；他那好奇心——这是他身上一项格外发达的素质——越发亢奋起来。倘若埃尔德里克不在家，倘若联络不上他，他就劝老巴特尔让他先开开眼——只要能瞄上一眼这份离奇现世的文书，晚饭晚些吃他也心甘情愿。传奇！可不是么，这才是真正的传奇；而且它还可能——还有什么不是呢？老巴特尔方才不是已经为这场乾坤颠倒乐得咯咯直笑了么：那是不是意味着——

===Original===
The telephone bell rang: Eldrick had not yet reached his house. Pratt got on to the club: Eldrick had not been there. He rang off, and went back to the private room.

“Can’t get hold of him, Mr. Bartle,” he began, as he closed the door. “He’s not at home, and he’s not at the club. I say!—you might as well let me have a look at—”

Pratt suddenly stopped. There was a strange silence in the room: the old man’s wheezy breathing was no longer heard. And the clerk moved forward quickly and looked round the high back of the easy chair.  …

He knew at once what had happened—knew that old Bartle was dead before he laid a finger on the wasted hand which had dropped helplessly at his side. He had evidently died without a sound or a movement—died as quietly as he would have gone to sleep. Indeed, he looked as if he had just laid his old head against the padding of the chair and dropped asleep, and Pratt, who had seen death before, knew that he would never wake again. He waited a moment, listening in the silence. Once he touched the old man’s hand; once, he bent nearer, still listening. And then, without hesitation, and with fingers that remained as steady as if nothing had happened, he unbuttoned Antony Bartle’s coat, and drew a folded paper from the inner pocket.

===Chinese===
电话铃响了：埃尔德里克还没到家。普拉特又接通了俱乐部：埃尔德里克没去过那儿。他挂断电话，回到了里面的房间。

“找不到他，巴特尔先生，”他掩上房门，开口说道。“他不在家，也不在俱乐部。我说！——您不如干脆让我先看一看——”

普拉特突然收住了话头。房间里静得出奇：老人那呼哧呼哧的喘息声听不见了。文书快步抢上前去，绕过安乐椅高高的椅背看去……

他立刻明白出了什么事——不等手指碰上那只无力垂在身侧、枯瘦如柴的手，他就知道老巴特尔已经死了。老人显然走得不声不响、一动不动——就像安然入睡一般悄然离世。他看上去确实像是刚把那颗苍老的头靠上椅子的软垫、便沉沉睡去；见惯了死亡的普拉特知道，他再也不会醒来了。他静立了片刻，在寂静中凝神细听。他碰了碰老人的手；又一次俯身凑近，仍在细听。然后，他毫不犹豫地——手指稳定得仿佛什么都没有发生过——解开安东尼·巴特尔的外衣扣子，从内袋里抽出了一张折起的纸。

## II. In Trust / 二 信托

===Original===
As quietly and composedly as if he were discharging the most ordinary of his daily duties, Pratt unfolded the document, and went close to the solitary gas jet above Eldrick’s desk. What he held in his hand was a half-sheet of ruled foolscap paper, closely covered with writing, which he at once recognized as that of the late John Mallathorpe. He was familiar with that writing—he had often seen it. It was an old-fashioned writing—clear, distinct, with every letter well and fully formed.

===Chinese===
普拉特不动声色、从容自若，仿佛在料理自己平素最寻常的一桩日常公务。他展开那份文书，走到埃尔德里克书桌上空那盏孤零零的煤气灯跟前。他手里拿的是半张带横格线的大页纸，上面密密麻麻写满了字——他一眼就认出，那是已故约翰·马拉索普的笔迹。这笔迹他熟得很——平时没少见。那是种老派的字——清楚、分明，每个字母都写得端正饱满。

===Original===
“Made it himself!” muttered Pratt. “Um!—looks as if he wanted to keep the terms secret. Well—”

He read the will through—rapidly, but with care, murmuring the phraseology half aloud.

===Chinese===
“他自己动手立的！”普拉特喃喃道。“嗯！——瞧这光景，倒像是有意要瞒住条款。好吧——”

他把遗嘱从头到尾通读了一遍——读得飞快，却毫不马虎，嘴里半高声地念着那些条文词句。

===Original===
This is the last will of me, John Mallathorpe, of Normandale Grange, in the parish of Normandale, in the West Riding of the County of York. I appoint Martin William Charlesworth, manufacturer, of Holly Lodge, Barford, and Arthur James Wyatt, chartered accountant, of 65, Beck Street, Barford, executors and trustees of this my will. I give and devise all my estate and effects real and personal of which I may die possessed or entitled to unto the said Martin William Charlesworth and Arthur James Wyatt upon trust for the following purposes to be carried out by them under the following instructions, namely:—As soon after my death as is conveniently possible they will sell all my real estate, either by private treaty or by public auction; they shall sell all my personal property of any nature whatsoever; they shall sell my business at Mallathorpe’s mill in Barford as a going concern to any private purchaser or to any company already in existence or formed for the purpose of acquiring it; and they shall collect all debts and moneys due to me. And having sold and disposed of all my property, real and personal, and brought all the proceeds of such sales and of such collection of debts and moneys into one common fund they shall first pay all debts owing by me and all legal duties and expenses arising out of my death and this disposition of my property and shall then distribute my estate as follows, namely: to each of themselves, Martin William Charlesworth and Arthur James Wyatt, they shall pay the sum of five thousand pounds; to my sister-in-law, Ann Mallathorpe, they shall pay the sum of ten thousand pounds; to my nephew, Harper John Mallathorpe, they shall pay the sum of ten thousand pounds; to my niece, Nesta Mallathorpe, they shall pay the sum of ten thousand pounds. And as to the whole of the remaining residue they shall pay it in one sum to the Mayor and Corporation of the borough of Barford in the County of York to be applied by the said Mayor and Corporation at their own absolute discretion and in any manner which seems good to them to the establishment, furtherance and development of technical and commercial education in the said borough of Barford. Dated this sixteenth day of November, 1906.

===Chinese===
本人约翰·马拉索普，住约克郡西区诺曼代尔教区诺曼代尔庄园，谨立本遗嘱。本人委任巴福德镇霍利洛奇宅（Holly Lodge）制造商马丁·威廉·查尔斯沃思（Martin William Charlesworth），及巴福德镇贝克街65号特许会计师亚瑟·詹姆斯·怀亚特（Arthur James Wyatt），为本遗嘱之执行人兼受托人。本人将去世时拥有或有权享有之全部不动产及动产，交予上述马丁·威廉·查尔斯沃思与亚瑟·詹姆斯·怀亚特，以信托方式由其遵照下列指示、为下列目的办理，即：——本人故世后，彼等务须尽早出售本人全部不动产，或以私下议约，或以公开拍卖；凡本人动产，无论性质若何，均须出售变现；本人设于巴福德马拉索普工厂之营业，须作为营运中之企业，售予任何私人买主，或售予任何现有公司，或为收购该营业而新设之公司；凡他人应付本人之债款银钱，均须收取。待全部动产与不动产俱已售出处置，并将售卖所得及所收债款银钱汇成一宗共同基金后，彼等应首先清偿本人所欠一切债务，及因本人亡故与本次财产处分而生之一切法定捐税费用，然后按下列方式分配本人遗产，即：——彼等应向其本人，即马丁·威廉·查尔斯沃思与亚瑟·詹姆斯·怀亚特，各付五千镑；应向本人弟媳安·马拉索普（Ann Mallathorpe）付一万镑；应向本人侄子哈珀·约翰·马拉索普（Harper John Mallathorpe）付一万镑；应向本人侄女妮斯塔·马拉索普（Nesta Mallathorpe）付一万镑。至其余全部剩余遗产，彼等应一次整笔付予约克郡巴福德自治镇之镇长暨市政委员会，由上述镇长暨市政委员会自行全权酌夺、以其认为妥善之任何方式，用于在上述巴福德镇设立、推进并发展技术与商业教育。立于1906年11月16日。

===Original===
Signed by the testator in the presence of us both present at the same time who in his presence and in the presence of each other have hereunto set our names as witnesses.

John Mallathorpe

Henry Gaukrodger, 16, Florence Street,

						Baford, Mill Manager.

Charles Watson Marshall, 56, Laburnum Terrace,

						Barford, Cashier.

===Chinese===
立遗嘱人当面签署本遗嘱，我等二人同时在场，并于其面前及彼此面前，于此署名用为见证。

约翰·马拉索普

亨利·高克罗杰，弗洛伦斯街16号，

						巴福德，经理长。

查尔斯·沃森·马歇尔，拉伯纳姆台56号，

						巴福德，出纳主任。

===Original===
As the last word left his lips Pratt carefully folded up the will, slipped it into an inner pocket of his coat, and firmly buttoned the coat across his chest. Then, without as much as a glance at the dead man, he left the room, and again visited the telephone box. He was engaged in it for a few minutes. When he came out he heard steps coming up the staircase, and looking over the banisters he saw the senior partner, Eldrick, a middle-aged man. Eldrick looked up, and saw Pratt.

===Chinese===
最后一个字音才落，普拉特便仔仔细细把遗嘱折好，塞进外衣内袋，把衣襟直扣到胸口。随后，他连一眼也没朝那死人看，径自出了房间，又去了电话间，在里头忙了几分钟。出来的时候，他听见楼梯上有脚步声上来，从栏杆边往下一探，看见高级合伙人埃尔德里克——一个中年人——正上楼来。埃尔德里克抬头，也看见了普拉特。

===Original===
“I hear you’ve been ringing me up at the club, Pratt,” he said. “What is it?”

Pratt waited until Eldrick had come up to the landing. Then he pointed to the door of the private room, and shook his head.

“It’s old Mr. Bartle, sir,” he whispered. “He’s in your room there—dead!”

===Chinese===
“我听说你往俱乐部给我挂电话了，普拉特，”他说。“什么事？”

普拉特等到埃尔德里克上了楼梯口，才指了指那间专用办公室的房门，摇了摇头。

“是老巴特尔先生，先生，”他悄声道。“他在您那间屋里——死了！”

===Original===
“Dead?” exclaimed Eldrick. “Dead!”

Pratt shook his head again.

“He came up not so long after you’d gone, sir,” he said. “Everybody had gone but me—I was just going. Wanted to see you about something I don’t know what. He was very tottery when he came in—complained of the stairs and the fog. I took him into your room, to sit down in the easy chair. And—he died straight off. Just,” concluded Pratt, “just as if he was going quietly to sleep!”

===Chinese===
“死了？”埃尔德里克惊呼出声。“死了！”

普拉特又摇了摇头。

“您走后没多久他就上来了，先生，”他说。“人都走了，就剩我一个——我也正要走呢。他要见您，为着什么事我可不知道。他进来的时候，脚下已经虚得很——直抱怨那道楼梯，还有外头的雾。我扶他进您屋里，在安乐椅上坐下。接着——他就一下子去了。就像，”普拉特收尾道，“就像安安静静睡过去了似的！”

===Original===
“You’re sure he is dead?—not fainting?” asked Eldrick.

“He’s dead, sir—quite dead,” replied Pratt. “I’ve rung up Dr. Melrose—he’ll be here in a minute or two—and the Town Hall—the police—as well. Will you look at him, sir?”

Eldrick silently motioned his clerk to open the door; together they walked into the room. And Eldrick looked at his quiet figure and wan face, and knew that Pratt was right.

===Chinese===
“您拿得准他是死了？——不是晕过去了？”埃尔德里克问。

“死了，先生——死透了，”普拉特答道。“我已经给梅尔罗斯医生（Dr. Melrose）挂了电话——他一两分钟就到——市政厅——警察局——也都通知了。您去看看他吗，先生？”

埃尔德里克默默打了个手势，让文书开门；两人一同走进房间。埃尔德里克望着老人安详的身影和苍白的面容，知道普拉特说得不错。

===Original===
“Poor old chap!” he murmured, touching one of the thin hands. “He was a fine man in his time, Pratt; clever man! And he was very, very old—one of the oldest men in Barford. Well, we must wire to his grandson, Mr. Bartle Collingwood. You’ll find his address in the book. He’s the only relation the old fellow had.”

“Come in for everything, doesn’t he, sir?” asked Pratt, as he took an address book from the desk, and picked up a sheaf of telegram forms.

“Every penny!” murmured Eldrick. “Nice little fortune, too—a fine thing for a young fellow who’s just been called to the Bar. As a matter of fact, he’ll be fairly well independent, even if he never sees a brief in his life.”

“He has been called, has he, sir?” asked Pratt, laying a telegram form on Eldrick’s writing pad and handing him a pen. “I wasn’t aware of that.”

===Chinese===
“可怜的老伙计！”他轻声叹道，碰了碰那只瘦削的手。“想当年他也是个人物呢，普拉特；有本事的人！再说他实在、实在太老了——巴福德数得着的高寿。这样吧，我们得给他孙子巴特尔·科林伍德（Bartle Collingwood）先生拍电报。他的地址你在那本册子里找得到。这老头子就这么一门亲眷。”

“遗产一股脑儿全落他一个人头上，是不是，先生？”普拉特一面从书桌上拿起地址簿、抓起一沓电报纸，一面问道。

“一个子儿不落！”埃尔德里克喃喃道。“还是一笔挺可观的小家底呢——对一个刚取得出庭律师资格的年轻人，是件美事。说实在的，哪怕他一辈子接不着一桩案子，也尽可以稳稳当当过下去了。”

“他已经取得资格了，是吗，先生？”普拉特一面把一张电报纸摊在埃尔德里克的写字垫板上、递给他一支笔，一面问道。“这我倒不知道。”

===Original===
“Called this term—quite recently—at Gray’s Inn,” replied Eldrick, as he sat down. “Very promising, clever young man. Look here!—we’d better send two wires, one to his private address, and one to his chambers. They’re both in that book. It’s six o’clock, isn’t it?—he might be at his chambers yet, but he may have gone home. I’ll write both messages—you put the addresses on, and get the wire off—we must have him down here as soon as possible.”

“One address is 53x, Pump Court; the other’s 96, Cloburn Square,” remarked Pratt consulting the book. “There’s an express from King’s Cross at 8:15 which gets here midnight.”

===Chinese===
“这一期刚取得资格——就在不久前——在格雷律师学院（Gray’s Inn），”埃尔德里克坐下来答道。“很有些前途，是个聪明的年轻人。这样吧！——我们顶好拍两封电报，一封拍他私宅，一封拍他事务所。两个地址都在那本册子里。现在六点了，对不对？——他说不定还在事务所，也可能回家了。两封电文都由我来写——你把地址填上，赶紧发出去——我们必须让他尽早赶到这儿来。”

“一个地址是泵院（Pump Court）53x，另一个是克洛本广场（Cloburn Square）96号，”普拉特翻着地址簿说道。“八点一刻国王十字（King’s Cross）有一班快车，半夜十二点到这儿。”

===Original===
“Oh, it would do if he came down first thing in the morning—leave it to him,” said Eldrick. “I say, Pratt, do you think an inquest will be necessary?”

Pratt had not thought of that—he began to think. And while he was thinking, the doctor whom he had summoned came in. He looked at the dead man, asked the clerk a few questions, and was apparently satisfied. “I don’t think there’s any need for an inquest,” he said in reply to Eldrick. “I knew the old man very well—he was much feebler than he would admit. The exertion of coming up these stairs of yours, and the coughing brought on by the fog outside—that was quite enough. Of course, the death will have to be reported in the usual way, but I have no hesitation in giving a certificate. You’ve let the Town Hall people know? Well, the body had better be removed to his rooms—we must send over and tell his housekeeper. He’d no relations in the town, had he?”

===Chinese===
“哦，他明儿一早头一趟赶来也来得及——由他自己定吧，”埃尔德里克说。“我说，普拉特，你看需要验尸审理吗？”

这一层普拉特倒没想过——他开始盘算起来。他正盘算着，他请的那位医生走了进来。医生看了看死者，问了文书几句话，看样子颇为满意。“我看用不着验尸审理，”他回答埃尔德里克道。“这老头子我熟得很——他的身子骨，比他自己肯承认的虚得多。爬你们那道楼梯费的力气，再加上外头的雾呛出来的咳嗽——光这两样就尽够了。当然，死亡照例得申报，不过开具死亡证明，我是毫无顾虑的。市政厅那边你们通知过了？嗯，遗体最好抬回他住处去——得派人知会他的女管家一声。他在镇上没有亲眷，是吧？”

===Original===
“Only one in the world that he ever mentioned—his grandson—a young barrister in London,” answered Eldrick. “We’ve just been wiring to him. Here, Pratt, you take these messages now, and get them off. Then we’ll see about making all arrangements. By the by,” he added, as Pratt moved towards the door, “you don’t know what—what he came to see me about?”

“Haven’t the remotest idea, sir,” answered Pratt, readily and glibly. “He died—just as I’ve told you—before he could tell me anything.”

===Chinese===
“他这辈子提过的，天底下就这一门亲——他孙子——伦敦一个年轻的出庭律师，”埃尔德里克答道。“我们刚才正给他拍电报呢。来，普拉特，这几封电文你这就拿去发了。回头咱们再商量各项安排。对了，”普拉特朝门口走去时，他又添了一句，“你可知道他——他来找我究竟是为了什么事？”

“一点儿影子也没有，先生，”普拉特脱口而答，又快又顺溜。“他死了——正像我告诉您的那样——还来不及跟我说上半句话就死了。”

===Original===
He went downstairs, and out into the street, and away to the General Post Office, only conscious of one thing, only concerned about one thing—that he was now the sole possessor of a great secret. The opportunity which he had so often longed for had come. And as he hurried along through the gathering fog he repeated and repeated a fragment of the recent conversation between the man who was now dead, and himself—who remained very much alive.

“You haven’t shown it to anybody else?” Pratt had asked.

“Neither shown it to anybody, nor mentioned it to a soul,” Antony Bartle had answered. So, in all that great town of Barford, he, Linford Pratt, he, alone out of a quarter of a million people, knew—what? The magnitude of what he knew not only amazed but exhilarated him. There were such possibilities for himself in that knowledge. He wanted to be alone, to think out those possibilities; to reckon up what they came to. Of one thing he was already certain—they should be, must be, turned to his own advantage.

===Chinese===
他下了楼，出门上街，直奔邮政总局而去；心里只意识到一件事，只牵挂着一件事——如今，一桩天大的秘密，唯独归他一人所有。他渴慕了不知多少回的机遇，终于来了。他顶着渐浓的夜雾匆匆赶路，一遍又一遍咀嚼着方才那番对话的只言片语——对话的一方如今已做了死人，另一方——他自己——可还活得好好的。

“您没给别的什么人看过吧？”普拉特当时这样问过。

“没给任何人看过，也没跟任何人提过一个字，”安东尼·巴特尔当时这样回答。这么说来，在巴福德那座大镇上，他林福德·普拉特，他，二十五万人里独一个，知道——知道了什么？他所知晓之事的分量，不但叫他惊愕，更叫他亢奋。这份知晓里头，藏着他此身前程的多少可能！他要独自一人，把这些可能一一想透；算一算它们究竟能兑现成什么。有一件事他早已拿定主意——它们应当、也必须，尽数变成他自己的进益。

===Original===
It was past eight o’clock before Pratt was able to go home to his lodgings. His landlady, meeting him in the hall, hoped that his dinner would not be spoiled: Pratt, who relied greatly on his dinner as his one great meal of the day, replied that he fervently hoped it wasn’t, but that if it was it couldn’t be helped, this time. For once he was thinking of something else than his dinner—as for his engagement for that evening, he had already thrown it over: he wanted to give all his energies and thoughts and time to his secret. Nevertheless, it was characteristic of him that he washed, changed his clothes, ate his dinner, and even glanced over the evening newspaper before he turned to the real business which was already deep in his brain. But at last, when the maid had cleared away the dinner things, and he was alone in his sitting-room, and had lighted his pipe, and mixed himself a drop of whisky-and-water—the only indulgence in such things that he allowed himself within the twenty-four hours—he drew John Mallathorpe’s will from his pocket, and read it carefully three times. And then he began to think, closely and steadily.

===Chinese===
普拉特回到寓所时，已经过了八点。女房东在门厅里迎着他，只盼他那顿晚饭别给糟蹋了：普拉特一日三餐全指着晚饭这顿正经饭，便答道，他热切盼着没糟蹋，不过真糟蹋了，这一回也无可奈何。破天荒头一遭，他心里惦记的竟是晚饭以外的物事——至于当晚的约会，他早已丢开不管了：他要把全部精力、心思和时间，都倾在那个秘密上。话虽如此，他到底是他的那个性子：先洗漱，更衣，用晚饭，甚至把晚报也粗粗翻了一遍，才回头料理那桩早已在脑子里盘桓的正事。终于，女仆收拾了饭桌，他独自坐在起居室里，点起烟斗，给自己兑了一小杯掺水的威士忌——这是他二十四小时里唯一容许自己的这点点口腹之娱——然后从口袋里掏出约翰·马拉索普的遗嘱，仔仔细细读了三遍。接着，他开始缜密而沉着地思索起来。

===Original===
First of all, the will was a good will. Nothing could upset it. It was absolutely valid. It was not couched in the terms which a solicitor would have employed, but it clearly and plainly expressed John Mallathorpe’s intentions and meanings in respect to the disposal of his property. Nothing could be clearer. The properly appointed trustees were to realize his estate. They were to distribute it according to his specified instructions. It was all as plain as a pikestaff. Pratt, who was a good lawyer, knew what the Probate Court would say to that will if it were ever brought up before it, as he did, a quite satisfactory will. And it was validly executed. Hundreds of people, competent to do so, could swear to John Mallathorpe’s signature; hundreds to Gaukrodger’s; thousands to Marshall’s—who as cashier was always sending his signature broadcast. No, there was nothing to do but to put that into the hands of the trustees named in it, and then. …

===Chinese===
首先，这是一份立得住的遗嘱。什么都推翻不了它。它绝对有效。措辞虽不是律师惯用的那套章程，却清清楚楚、明明白白道出了约翰·马拉索普对自己财产处分的心意和盘算。再没有比这更清楚的了。正式指定的受托人须把他的产业变现，再照他明定的指示分发出去。桩桩件件，一望而知。普拉特是个懂行的，深知这份遗嘱若果真呈到遗嘱检验法庭面前，法庭会下什么断语——同他一样：一份无可挑剔的遗嘱。何况它签署手续完备有效。够格作证的人成百上千：能起誓认得约翰·马拉索普签名的，成百；认得高克罗杰的，成百；认得马歇尔的，上千——他当着出纳主任，成天把签名撒向四面八方。所以说，别无二话，只消把这份遗嘱交到其中指名的受托人手上，然后。……

===Original===
Pratt thought next of the two trustees. They were well-known men in the town. They were comparatively young men—about forty. They were men of great energy. Their chief interests were in educational matters—that, no doubt, was why John Mallathorpe had appointed them trustees. Wyatt had been plaguing the town for two years to start commercial schools: Charlesworth was a devoted champion of technical schools. Pratt knew how the hearts of both would leap, if he suddenly told them that enormous funds were at their disposal for the furtherance of their schemes. And he also knew something else—that neither Charlesworth nor Wyatt had the faintest, remotest notion or suspicion that John Mallathorpe had ever made such a will, or they would have moved heaven and earth, pulled down Normandale Grange and Mallathorpe’s Mill, in their efforts to find it.

===Chinese===
接着，普拉特想到那两位受托人。两人都是镇上的知名人士，都还算年轻——四十上下——都是精力充沛的角色。他们的主要兴致都在教育事务上——毫无疑问，约翰·马拉索普正是为这个才委任他们做受托人的。怀亚特缠着全镇闹了两年，一心要办商业学校：查尔斯沃思则是技术学校的热忱拥趸。普拉特心里有数：倘若他冷不丁告诉这两位，说有一笔巨款正听凭他们支配，好推进各自的大计，两人的心会怎样怦怦直跳。他还知道另一层——查尔斯沃思也好，怀亚特也好，对约翰·马拉索普立过这样一份遗嘱这件事，连一丝一毫的念头和疑心都不曾有过；否则，他们便是使尽移山倒海之力，把诺曼代尔庄园连同马拉索普工厂夷为平地，也要把它找出来。

===Original===
But the effect—the effect of producing the will—now? Pratt, like everybody else, had been deeply interested in the Mallathorpe affair. There was so little doubt that John Mallathorpe had died intestate, such absolute certainty that his only living relations were his deceased brother’s two children and their mother, that the necessary proceedings for putting Harper Mallathorpe and his sister Nesta in possession of the property, real and personal, had been comparatively simple and speedy. But—what was it worth? What would the two trustees have been able to hand over to the Mayor and Corporation of Barford, if the will had been found as soon as John Mallathorpe died? Pratt, from what he remembered of the bulk and calculations at the time, made a rapid estimate. As near as he could reckon, the Mayor and Corporation would have got about £300,000.

===Chinese===
可是，后果呢——此刻把这份遗嘱抖出来，后果如何？马拉索普家那桩案子，普拉特同旁人一样，一直密切关注。当初约翰·马拉索普死时未立遗嘱，这一点几乎无可置疑；他在世的至亲就只有亡故弟弟的一双儿女及其母亲，这一点更是铁板钉钉；因此，为把哈珀·马拉索普和他妹妹妮斯塔扶上全部动产与不动产主人之位而履行的必要法律程序，办得相当简便利落。可是——那份家业究竟值多少？倘若遗嘱在约翰·马拉索普一死便被发现，两位受托人本可向巴福德镇长暨市政委员会移交多大的一笔款子？凭着对当年产业规模和各项估算的记忆，普拉特飞快核了一笔账。照他的盘算，落到镇长暨市政委员会手里的，大约会有三十万镑。

===Original===
That, then—and this was what he wanted to get at—was what these young people would lose if he produced the will. Nay!—on second thoughts, it would be much more, very much more in some time; for the manufacturing business was being carried on by them, and was apparently doing as well as ever. It was really an enormous amount which they would lose—and they would get—what? Ten thousand apiece and their mother a like sum. Thirty thousand pounds in all—in comparison with hundreds of thousands. But they would have no choice in the matter. Nothing could upset that will.

===Chinese===
这么一说——这正是他要盘算到底的一笔账——他若亮出遗嘱，这两个年轻人失掉的便是这些。岂止！——再往深里一想，时日一久，这笔数目还会大得多，大得多得多；因为那爿制造生意如今正由他们兄妹经营着，看光景兴旺不减当年。他们要失掉的，实在是一笔巨款——而他们能到手的——是什么？每人一万镑，他们的母亲也是一万。统共三万镑——同几十万相比。可在这件事上，他们半点选择的余地也没有。什么都推翻不了那份遗嘱。

===Original===
He began to think of the three people whom the production of this will would dispossess. He knew little of them beyond what common gossip had related at the time of John Mallathorpe’s sudden death. They had lived in very quiet fashion, somewhere on the outskirts of the town, until this change in their fortunes. Once or twice Pratt had seen Mrs. Mallathorpe in her carriage in the Barford streets—somebody had pointed her out to him, and had observed sneeringly that folk can soon adapt themselves to circumstances, and that Mrs. Mallathorpe now gave herself all the airs of a duchess, though she had been no more than a hospital nurse before she married Richard Mallathorpe. And Pratt had also seen young Harper Mallathorpe now and then in the town—since the good fortune arrived—and had envied him: he had also thought what a strange thing it was that money went to young fellows who seemed to have no particular endowments of brain or energy. Harper was a very ordinary young man, not over intelligent in appearance, who, Pratt had heard, was often seen lounging about the one or two fashionable hotels of the place. As for the daughter, Pratt did not remember having ever set eyes on her—but he had heard that up to the time of John Mallathorpe’s death she had earned her own living as a governess, or a nurse, or something of that sort.

===Chinese===
他开始琢磨这份遗嘱一旦面世、便要被扫地出门的那三个人。除了约翰·马拉索普暴亡当时街谈巷议的那些闲话，他对这三人的底细所知寥寥。时来运转之前，他们一直住在镇子边上什么地方，日子过得极不起眼。有一两回，普拉特在巴福德街上见过坐马车的马拉索普太太——有人指给他看的，还冷言冷语地议论道，人这东西适应境遇可真快，又说马拉索普太太如今摆足了派头，俨然一位公爵夫人，殊不知她没嫁给理查德·马拉索普的时候，不过是个医院里的看护。哈珀·马拉索普——自打好运临头之后——普拉特在镇上也不时撞见，还眼热过他：他也想过，钱偏偏流向那些脑子、精力似乎都瞧不出什么过人之处的年轻小子，天底下的怪事莫过于此。哈珀是个顶顶平庸的年轻人，长相也说不上聪明；普拉特听说，常见他在本地那一两家时髦旅馆里闲逛厮混。至于那位女儿，普拉特不记得曾亲眼见过——只听说直到约翰·马拉索普去世为止，她一直靠当家庭教师还是当看护什么的，自食其力。

===Original===
He turned from thinking of these three people to thoughts about himself. Pratt often thought about himself, and always in one direction—the direction of self-advancement. He was always wanting to get on. He had nobody to help him. He had kept himself since he was seventeen. His father and mother were dead; he had no brothers or sisters—the only relations he had, uncles and aunts, lived—some in London, some in Canada. He was now twenty-eight, and earning four pounds a week. He had immense confidence in himself, but he had never seen much chance of escaping from drudgery. He had often thought of asking Eldrick & Pascoe to give him his articles—but he had a shrewd idea that his request would be refused. No—it was difficult to get out of a rut. And yet—he was a clever fellow, a good-looking fellow, a sharp, shrewd, able—and here was a chance, such a chance as scarcely ever comes to a man. He would be a fool if he did not take it, and use it to his own best and lasting advantage.

===Chinese===
他把这三个人从脑子里撇开，转而想到自己。普拉特时常想自己，而且永远朝着一个方向想——往上爬的方向。他总想着出人头地。没人帮衬他。他打十七岁起就自食其力。父母双亡；无兄无弟、无姐无妹——仅有的几门亲戚，叔叔伯伯、姑姑舅舅，住的住伦敦，住的住加拿大。他如今二十八岁，每星期挣四镑。他自信心极强，可一直看不出有多少摆脱苦差的指望。他不止一次动过念头，想求埃尔德里克—帕斯科同他签订见习契约（articles），正式收他做徒弟——但他心里透亮，这请求八成要吃闭门羹。是啊——人要跳出老辙，谈何容易。然而——他是个聪明人，个体面的俊俏后生，机灵、精明、有才干——而眼下这正是个机会，人生难逢难遇的机会。他若不抓住它，不把它变成本人长远而极致的进益，那才叫傻子。

===Original===
And so he locked up the will in a safe place, and went to bed, resolved to take a bold step towards fortune on the morrow.

===Chinese===
于是他把遗嘱锁进一个稳妥的地方，上床睡了，打定了主意：明天，就朝富贵前程迈出大胆的一步。

## III. The Shop-Boy / 三 小店伙

===Original===
When Pratt arrived at Eldrick & Pascoe’s office at his usual hour of nine next morning, he found the senior partner already there. And with him was a young man whom the clerk at once set down as Mr. Bartle Collingwood, and looked at with considerable interest and curiosity. He had often heard of Mr. Bartle Collingwood, but had never seen him. He knew that he was the only son of old Antony Bartle’s only child﻿—a daughter who had married a London man; he knew, too, that Collingwood’s parents were both dead, and that the old bookseller had left their son everything he possessed﻿—a very nice little fortune, as Eldrick had observed last night. And since last night he had known that Collingwood had just been called to the Bar, and was on the threshold of what Eldrick, who evidently knew all about it, believed to be a promising career. Well, there he was in the flesh; and Pratt, who was a born observer of men and events, took a good look at him as he stood just within the private room, talking to Eldrick.

===Chinese===
第二天早上九点，普拉特照着平日的钟点来到埃尔德里克—帕斯科的事务所，只见高级合伙人已经先到了。他身边还立着一个年轻人，文书一眼便断定这是巴特尔·科林伍德（Bartle Collingwood）先生，于是怀着不小的兴味和好奇把他打量了一番。巴特尔·科林伍德先生其人，他时常听人说起，却从未见过。他知道，此人是老安东尼·巴特尔那独生女儿的唯一儿子——那位小姐当年嫁了个伦敦人；他还知道，科林伍德的双亲俱已亡故，老书商把身外之物一股脑儿全留给了他们这个儿子——正如埃尔德里克昨晚所言，是挺可观的一小笔家私。而且从昨晚起，他又知道了科林伍德刚刚取得出庭律师资格，正跨在一条前程的门槛上——那条前程，显然知根知底、成竹在胸的埃尔德里克认定是大有指望的。好，眼下本人活生生地就在这儿；普拉特天生是个善于观察人事的主儿，便趁他站在专用办公室门里侧、正跟埃尔德里克说话的当口，把他端详了个够。

===Original===
A good-looking fellow; what most folk would call handsome; dark, clean-shaven, tall, with a certain air of reserve about his well-cut features, firm lips, and steady eyes that suggested strength and determination. He would look very well in wig and gown, decided Pratt, viewing matters from a professional standpoint; he was just the sort that clients would feel a natural confidence in, and that juries would listen to. Another of the lucky ones, too; for Pratt knew the contents of Antony Bartle’s will, and that the young man at whom he was looking had succeeded to a cool five-and-twenty thousand pounds, at least, through his grandfather’s death.

===Chinese===
一个体面的后生；换了寻常人的说法，就要叫他俊俏人物了；肤色黝黑，脸刮得干干净净，身材高挑，端正的脸相上带着几分矜持，嘴唇紧抿，目光沉稳，透出一股力量和决断。戴上假发、披上法袍一定很有架势——普拉特从行内人的立场把这事断定了；他正是那种当事人自然而然要信服的人，也是陪审团肯静听的人。而且又是个走运的；因为普拉特知道安东尼·巴特尔遗嘱的内容，知道他此刻打量的这个年轻人，光凭着祖父一死，少说也到手了足足两万五千镑。

===Original===
“Here is Pratt,” said Eldrick, glancing into the outer office as the clerk entered it. “Pratt, come in here﻿—here is Mr. Bartle Collingwood, He would like you to tell him the facts about Mr. Bartle’s death.”

===Chinese===
“普拉特来了，”埃尔德里克说——文书正走进外间办公室，他朝那边瞥了一眼。“普拉特，到这儿来——这位是巴特尔·科林伍德先生。他想请你把巴特尔先生去世的实情讲给他听听。”

===Original===
Pratt walked in﻿—armed and prepared. He was a clever hand at foreseeing things, and he had known all along that he would have to answer questions about the event of the previous night.

===Chinese===
普拉特应声走了进去——胸有成竹，有备而来。他是个预事于先的好手，早就明白昨夜那桩事少不得要经受一番盘问。

===Original===
“There’s very little to tell, sir,” he said, with a polite acknowledgment of Collingwood’s greeting. “Mr. Bartle came up here just as I was leaving﻿—everybody else had left. He wanted to see Mr. Eldrick. Why, he didn’t say. He was coughing a good deal when he came in, and he complained of the fog outside, and of the stairs. He said something﻿—just a mere mention﻿—about his heart being bad. I lighted the gas in here, and helped him into the chair. He just sat down, laid his head back, and died.”

===Chinese===
“没什么可讲的，先生，”他说着，对科林伍德的招呼客气地领了一领。“巴特尔先生上来的时候，我正要走——旁的人全走了。他要见埃尔德里克先生。为着什么事，他没说。他进来时咳嗽得厉害，抱怨外头的雾，还抱怨那道楼梯。他说了一句什么——不过是顺口一提——说自己心脏不好。我点亮这屋里的煤气灯，扶他到椅子上坐下。他刚一坐下，头往后一靠，就死了。”

===Original===
“Without saying anything further?” asked Collingwood.

===Chinese===
“后来再没说什么了？”科林伍德问。

===Original===
“Not a word more, Mr. Collingwood,” answered Pratt. “He﻿—well, it was just as if he had dropped off to sleep. Of course, at first I thought he’d fainted, but I soon saw what it was﻿—it so happens that I’ve seen a death just as sudden as that, once before﻿—my landlady’s husband died in a very similar fashion, in my presence. There was nothing I could do, Mr. Collingwood﻿—except ring up Mr. Eldrick, and the doctor, and the police.”

===Chinese===
“再没一个字，科林伍德先生，”普拉特答道。“他——唉，就跟缓缓睡过去了一样。当然，起先我只当他是晕过去了，可不多时就看出是怎么回事了——说来也巧，那样突然的死，我先前亲眼见过一回——我房东太太的丈夫就是在我在场的时候，以差不多的样子咽气的。我一点儿办法也没有，科林伍德先生——只好给埃尔德里克先生挂电话，还有医生，还有警察。”

===Original===
“Mr. Pratt made himself very useful last night in making arrangements,” remarked Eldrick, looking at Collingwood. “As it is, there is very little to do. There will be no need for any inquest; Melrose has given his certificate. So﻿—there are only the funeral arrangements. We can help you with that matter, of course. But first you’d no doubt like to go to your grandfather’s place and look through his papers? We have his will here, you know﻿—and I’ve already told you its effect.”

===Chinese===
“昨晚张罗各项事宜，普拉特先生帮了很大的忙，”埃尔德里克望着科林伍德说。“如今看来，要办的事不多。验尸审理是用不着了；梅尔罗斯已经开了证明。所以——剩下的只有丧葬的安排。这件事我们自然可以帮您料理。不过，您总想先去您祖父的铺子，把他那些文书翻检一遍吧？您知道，他的遗嘱就在我们手里——遗嘱的效力，我也已经对您说过了。”

===Original===
“I’m much obliged to you, Mr. Pratt,” said Collingwood, turning to the clerk. He turned again to Eldrick. “All right,” he went on. “I’ll go over to Quagg Alley. By the by, Mr. Pratt﻿—my grandfather didn’t tell you anything of the reason of his call here?”

===Chinese===
“多谢您了，普拉特先生，”科林伍德说着，转向文书。随后又转回埃尔德里克那边。“好的，”他接着说。“我这就到夸格巷去。对了，普拉特先生——我祖父上这儿来的缘由，他没跟您透露过一言半语吧？”

===Original===
“Not a word, sir,” replied Pratt. “Merely said he wanted Mr. Eldrick.”

===Chinese===
“一个字也没有，先生，”普拉特答道。“只说要见埃尔德里克先生。”

===Original===
“Had he any legal business in process?” asked Collingwood.

===Chinese===
“他手上有没有正在办理的法律事务？”科林伍德问。

===Original===
Eldrick and his clerk both shook their heads. No, Mr. Bartle had no business of that sort that they knew of. Nothing﻿—but there again Pratt was prepared.

===Chinese===
埃尔德里克和他的文书都摇了摇头。没有，据他们所知，巴特尔先生手头没有那一类事务。一件也没有——不过这一层，普拉特也早有准备。

===Original===
“It might have been about the lease of that property in Horsebridge Land, sir,” he said, glancing at his principal. “He did mention that, you know, when he was in here a few weeks ago.”

===Chinese===
“也许是为一处房产的租约吧，就是霍斯布里奇兰（Horsebridge Land）那处房产，先生，”他说着，朝自家东家瞟了一眼。“您知道，几个星期前他来这儿的时候，倒是提起过那个。”

===Original===
“Just so,” agreed Eldrick. “Well, you’ll let me know if we can be of use,” he went on, as Collingwood turned away. “Pratt can be at your disposal, any time.”

===Chinese===
“正是这么回事，”埃尔德里克附和道。“好，用得着我们之处，您言语一声，”科林伍德转身要走，他又接着说。“普拉特随时听候您的差遣，什么时候都行。”

===Original===
Collingwood thanked him and went off. He had travelled down from London by the earliest morning train, and leaving his portmanteau at the hotel of the Barford terminus, had gone straight to Eldrick & Pascoe’s office; accordingly this was his first visit to the shop in Quagg Alley. But he knew the shop and its surroundings well enough, though he had not been in Barford for some time; he also knew Antony Bartle’s old housekeeper, Mrs. Clough, a rough and ready Yorkshirewoman, who had looked after the old man as long as he, Collingwood, could remember. She received him as calmly as if he had merely stepped across the street to inquire after his grandfather’s health.

===Chinese===
科林伍德道了谢，动身走了。他是搭当天头一班早车从伦敦下来的，把旅行皮箱往巴福德车站的旅馆里一寄，便径直奔了埃尔德里克—帕斯科的事务所；因此，夸格巷这爿铺子，他还是头一回登门。不过铺子和左近的环境他熟得很，虽说已有一些日子没回巴福德了；安东尼·巴特尔那位老女管家克拉夫太太（Mrs. Clough），他也认得——一个粗直爽利的约克郡妇人，打他科林伍德记事的时候起，照应老人的一直是她。她迎见他时那份安然，倒好像他不过是穿过一条街来问问祖父的安。

===Original===
“I thowt ye’d be down here first thing, Mestur Collingwood,” she said, as he walked into the parlor at the back of the shop. “Of course, there’s naught to be done except to see after yer grandfather’s burying. I don’t know if ye were surprised or no when t’ lawyers tellygraphed to yer last night? I weren’t surprised to hear what had happened. I’d been expecting summat o’ that sort this last month or two.”

===Chinese===
“俺就琢磨着，你今儿一早准得头一个赶到，克林伍德先生，”他走进铺子后身的客堂时，她说。“事到如今，自然没啥可办的了，就剩下张罗你爷爷的丧事。昨儿晚上律师们给你拍电报，你受惊了没有，俺不知道？俺听得出了这么件事，可一点儿也没吃惊。前个把月俺就估摸着，早晚要出这么一桩。”

===Original===
“You mean﻿—he was failing?” asked Collingwood.

===Chinese===
“你的意思是——他一天不如一天了？”科林伍德问。

===Original===
“He were gettin’ feebler and feebler every day,” said the housekeeper. “But nobody dare say so to him, and he wouldn’t admit it hisself. He were that theer high-spirited ’at he did things same as if he were a young man. But I knew how it ’ud be in the end﻿—and so it has been﻿—I knew he’d go off all of a sudden. And of course I had all in readiness﻿—when they brought him back last night there was naught to do but lay him out. Me and Mrs. Thompson next door, did it, i’ no time. Wheer will you be for buryin’ him, Mestur Collingwood?”

===Chinese===
“他一天虚似一天、一天弱似一天，”女管家说。“可谁也不敢当他面儿说，他自己也死不肯认。他那心气儿高着呢，做起事来还只当自己是个年轻人。可俺早知道末了儿是这个收场——如今果然应了——俺就知道他会说走就走。俺自然样样都备妥了——昨儿晚上他们把他抬回来，只消装裹入殓就是。俺跟隔壁的汤普森太太（Mrs. Thompson）两个，没多大工夫就拾掇停当了。你打算把他葬在哪儿，克林伍德先生？”

===Original===
“We must think that over,” answered Collingwood.

===Chinese===
“这个我们得从长计议，”科林伍德答道。

===Original===
“Well, an’ theer’s all ready for that, too,” responded Mrs. Clough. “He’s had his grave all ready i’ the cemetery this three year﻿—I remember when he bowt it﻿—it’s under a yew-tree, and he told me ’at he’d ordered his monnyment an’ all. So yer an’ t’ lawyers’ll have no great trouble about them matters. Mestur Eldrick, he gev’ orders for t’ coffin last night.”

===Chinese===
“哦，那个也早齐备下了，”克拉夫太太应道。“他三年前就在公墓里把坟茔置办停当——他置下那块地的时候俺还记着呢——就在一棵紫杉树底下，他还跟俺说，连墓碑都一并订下了。这么着，你跟律师们办这些事，也费不了多大周折。棺材呢，埃尔德里克先生昨儿晚上就吩咐下去了。”

===Original===
Collingwood left these gruesome details﻿—highly pleasing to their narrator﻿—and went up to look at his dead grandfather. He had never seen much of him, but they had kept up a regular correspondence, and always been on terms of affection, and he was sorry that he had not been with the old man at the last. He remained looking at the queer, quiet, old face for a while; when he went down again, Mrs. Clough was talking to a sharp-looking lad, of apparently sixteen or seventeen years, who stood at the door leading into the shop, and who glanced at Collingwood with keen interest and speculation.

===Chinese===
科林伍德撇下这些阴惨惨的细情——讲述的人倒讲得津津有味——上楼去看他故去的祖父。祖孙俩素来见面不多，书信却从未断过，彼此始终亲厚；他心里很是惋惜，只怨老人临终之际自己没能在跟前。他对着那张古怪而安详的老脸端详了一阵；再下楼来，克拉夫太太正跟一个透着机灵的少年说话，看年纪约莫十六七岁，站在通往铺子的那扇门口，正拿满含兴味、暗自揣度的眼光打量科林伍德。

===Original===
“Here’s Jabey Naylor wants to know if he’s to do aught, Mestur,” said the housekeeper. “Of course, I’ve telled him ’at we can’t have the shop open till the burying’s over﻿—so I don’t know what theer is that he can do.”

===Chinese===
“这位是杰比·奈勒（Jabey Naylor），来问要不要他搭把手，先生，”女管家说。“俺自然已经跟他说了，出殡以前铺子开不得张——所以俺也不知道，还有啥能让他做的。”

===Original===
“Oh, well, let him come into the shop with me,” answered Collingwood. He motioned the lad to follow him out of the parlour. “So you were Mr. Bartle’s assistant, eh?” he asked. “Had he anybody else?”

===Chinese===
“哦，那让他随我到铺子里来吧，”科林伍德答道。他打个手势，教少年跟他出了客堂。“这么说，你就是巴特尔先生手下的伙计，嗯？”他问。“他底下还有别人吗？”

===Original===
“Nobody but me, sir,” replied the lad. “I’ve been with him a year.”

===Chinese===
“就我一个，先生，”少年答道。“我跟着他一年了。”

===Original===
“And your name’s what?” inquired Collingwood.

===Chinese===
“那么你叫什么名字？”科林伍德问。

===Original===
“Jabez Naylor, sir, but everybody call me Jabey.”

===Chinese===
“杰贝兹·奈勒（Jabez Naylor），先生，不过大家都管我叫杰比。”

===Original===
“I see﻿—Jabey for short, eh?” said Collingwood good-humouredly. He walked into the shop, followed by the boy, and closed the door. The outer door into Quagg Alley was locked: a light blind was drawn over the one window; the books and engravings on the shelves and in the presses were veiled in a half-gloom. “Well, as Mrs. Clough says, we can’t do any business for a few days, Jabey﻿—after that we must see what can be done. You shall have your wages just the same, of course, and you may look in every day to see if there’s anything you can do. You were here yesterday, of course? Were you in the shop when Mr. Bartle went out?”

===Chinese===
“明白了——杰比是叫着顺口的简称，嗯？”科林伍德和颜悦色地说。他走进铺子，少年跟在后头，他随手带上了门。通夸格巷的外门上了锁；仅有的那扇窗上放下一领浅色的遮帘；书架上、柜橱里的书籍和版画，都蒙在一层半昏的暗影里。“这么着，正如克拉夫太太所说，这几天咱们做不成生意，杰比——往后再瞧能怎么办。你的工钱照发，这个自然；你也可以每天来望望，有什么能做的就做点儿什么。昨天你当然在铺子里吧？巴特尔先生出去的时候，你在铺子里？”

===Original===
“Yes, sir,” replied the lad. “I’d been in with him all the afternoon. I was here when he went out﻿—and here when they came to say he’d died at Mr. Eldrick’s.”

===Chinese===
“在的，先生，”少年答道。“一下午我都在铺子里陪着他。他出门的时候我在——后来人家来报信，说他在埃尔德里克先生那里去世了，我也在。”

===Original===
Collingwood sat down in his grandfather’s chair, at a big table, piled high with books and papers, which stood in the middle of the floor.

===Chinese===
科林伍德在祖父的那把椅子上坐了下来——椅子摆在屋子正中一张大桌子跟前，桌上书报文牍堆得老高。

===Original===
“Did my grandfather seem at all unwell when he went out?” he asked.

===Chinese===
“我祖父出门的时候，瞧着有什么不舒服吗？”他问。

===Original===
“No, sir. He had been coughing a bit more than usual﻿—that was all. There was a fog came on about five o’clock, and he said it bothered him.”

===Chinese===
“没有，先生。就是咳嗽比平常多了几声——没别的。五点钟前后起了雾，他说雾呛得他难受。”

===Original===
“What had he been doing during the afternoon? Anything particular?”

===Chinese===
“一下午他都在做些什么？有什么特别的事没有？”

===Original===
“Nothing at all particular before half-past four or so, sir.”

===Chinese===
“四点半以前，什么特别的事也没有，先生。”

===Original===
Collingwood took a closer look at Jabez Naylor. He saw that he was an observant lad, evidently of superior intelligence﻿—a good specimen of the sharp town lad, well trained in a modern elementary school.

===Chinese===
科林伍德把杰贝兹·奈勒又细看了一眼。他看出这是个眼尖心细的少年，天资显然高人一等——是城里那种机灵孩子里的一个好样儿，在新式小学里受过良好的调教。

===Original===
“Oh?” he said. “Nothing particular before half-past four, eh? Did he do something particular after half-past four?”

===Chinese===
“哦？”他说。“四点半以前没什么特别的，嗯？那么四点半以后，他做了什么特别的事？”

===Original===
“There was a post came in just about then, sir,” answered Jabey. “There was an American letter﻿—that’s it, sir﻿—just in front of you. Mr. Bartle read it, and asked me if we’d got a good clear copy of Hopkinson’s *History of Barford*. I reminded him that there was a copy amongst the books that had been bought from Mallathorpe’s Mill some time ago.”

===Chinese===
“差不多就是那当口，一班邮差送信进来了，先生，”杰比答道。“有一封美国来的信——喏，就是它，先生——就摆在您跟前。巴特尔先生读完信，问咱们手里有没有一册霍普金森的《巴福德史》，要书页干净、书品清爽的本子。我提醒他，前些时候从马拉索普工厂买进的那批书里，正有那么一册。”

===Original===
“Books that had belonged to Mr. John Mallathorpe, who was killed?” asked Collingwood, who was fully acquainted with the chimney accident.

===Chinese===
“就是那位后来罹难的约翰·马拉索普先生旧藏的书？”科林伍德问——烟囱倒塌那桩事故的底细，他早已知之甚详。

===Original===
“Yes, sir, Mr. Bartle bought a lot of books that Mr. Mallathorpe had at the Mill﻿—local books. They’re there in that corner: they were put there when I fetched them, and he’d never looked over them since, particularly.”

===Chinese===
“是的，先生，马拉索普先生放在厂里的那批书，巴特尔先生买下了好些——都是本地书。就搁在那个角落里：我搬回来的那回放进去的，打那以后他也没好好翻检过。”

===Original===
“Well﻿—and this *History of Barford*? You reminded him of it?”

===Chinese===
“那么——这部《巴福德史》呢？你提醒了他有这么一册？”

===Original===
“I got it out for him, sir. He sat down﻿—where you’re sitting﻿—and began to examine it. He said something about it being a nice copy, and he’d get it off that night﻿—that’s it, sir: I didn’t read it, of course. And then he took some papers out of a pocket that’s inside it, and I heard him say ‘Bless my soul﻿—who’d have thought it!’ ”

===Chinese===
“我取出来给了他，先生。他坐下——就坐在您现在坐的地方——动手验看。他说这册书品相真好，当晚就要发出去——喏，就是它，先生：我当然没读过。后来他从书里头的一个夹袋里取出几页纸，我就听见他念叨了一声：‘老天爷——谁想得到！’”

===Original===
Collingwood picked up the book which the boy indicated﻿—a thick, substantially bound volume, inside one cover of which was a linen pocket, wherein were some loose maps and plans of Barford.

===Chinese===
科林伍德拿起少年指给他看的那部书——厚厚的一册，装订结实，一面封皮的内侧缝着一个亚麻布夹袋，里头装着几幅巴福德的散页地图和图样。

===Original===
“These what he took out?” he asked, holding them up.

===Chinese===
“取出来的就是这几张？”他把那几页纸举起来，问道。

===Original===
“Yes, sir, but there was another paper, with writing on it﻿—a biggish sheet of paper﻿—written all over.”

===Chinese===
“是的，先生，不过另外还有一张纸，上面写着字——挺大的一张——写得满满当当。”

===Original===
“Did you see what the writing was? Did you see any of it?”

===Chinese===
“写的是什么，你看见了吗？其中有一星半点儿，你瞧见没有？”

===Original===
“No, sir﻿—only that it was writing, I was dusting those shelves out, over there; when I heard Mr. Bartle say what he did. I just looked round, over my shoulder﻿—that was all.”

===Chinese===
“没有，先生——只晓得那上头是字。那会儿我正在那边掸书架呢，听见巴特尔先生叫出那么一声。我就回头望了一眼——就望了那么一眼。”

===Original===
“Was he reading this paper that you speak of?”

===Chinese===
“他读的，就是你说的这张纸？”

===Original===
“Yes, sir﻿—he was holding it up to the gas, reading it.”

===Chinese===
“是的，先生——他把纸举到煤气灯跟前，正读着呢。”

===Original===
“Do you know what he did with it?”

===Chinese===
“他后来把那张纸怎么了，你知道吗？”

===Original===
“Yes, sir﻿—he folded it up and put it in his pocket.”

===Chinese===
“知道，先生——他把它折叠起来，放进自己口袋了。”

===Original===
“Did he say any more﻿—make any remark?”

===Chinese===
“他还说了什么没有——发过什么议论？”

===Original===
“No, sir. He wrote a letter then.”

===Chinese===
“没有了，先生。他跟着就写了一封信。”

===Original===
“At once?”

===Chinese===
“当场就写？”

===Original===
“Yes, sir﻿—straight off. But he wasn’t more than a minute writing it. Then he sent me to post it at the pillar-box, at the end of the Alley.”

===Chinese===
“是的，先生——提笔就写。不过统共没写多大工夫，一分钟的活儿。写完他就打发我送到巷子口那座邮筒去投了。”

===Original===
“Did you read the address?”

===Chinese===
“信上的地址，你看了吗？”

===Original===
The lad turned to a book which stood with others in a rack over the chimneypiece, and tapped it with his finger.

===Chinese===
少年转过身去，朝着壁炉架上方格架上与其他簿册立在一处的一本簿子，伸指敲了敲。

===Original===
“Yes, sir﻿—because Mr. Bartle gave orders when I first came here that a register of every letter sent out was to be kept﻿—I’ve always entered them in this book.”

===Chinese===
“看了，先生——我刚来那阵子，巴特尔先生就立下规矩，说发出去的每一封信都要登记造册——我一向都记在这本簿子里。”

===Original===
“And this letter you’re talking about﻿—to whom was it addressed?”

===Chinese===
“那么你说的这封信——是写给谁的？”

===Original===
“Miss Mallathorpe, Normandale Grange, sir.”

===Chinese===
“马拉索普小姐，诺曼代尔庄园，先生。”

===Original===
“You went and posted it at once?”

===Chinese===
“你当时就去投了？”

===Original===
“That very minute, sir.”

===Chinese===
“一分钟也没耽搁，先生。”

===Original===
“Was it soon afterwards that Mr. Bartle went out?”

===Chinese===
“巴特尔先生是随后不久就出门的吗？”

===Original===
“He went out as soon as I came back, sir.”

===Chinese===
“我一回来他就走了，先生。”

===Original===
“And you never saw him again?”

===Chinese===
“往后你就再没见过他了？”

===Original===
Jabey shook his head.

===Chinese===
杰比摇了摇头。

===Original===
“Not alive, sir,” he answered. “I saw him when they brought him back.”

===Chinese===
“活的没再见过，先生，”他答道。“他们把他抬回来的时候，我见着了。”

===Original===
“How long had he been out when you heard he was dead?”

===Chinese===
“你听得他死讯的时候，他出门出去多久了？”

===Original===
“About an hour, sir﻿—just after six it was when they told Mrs. Clough and me. He went out at ten minutes past five.”

===Chinese===
“约莫一个钟头，先生——人家来告诉克拉夫太太和我，是刚过六点的事。他出门是在五点过十分。”

===Original===
Collingwood got up. He gave the lad’s shoulder a friendly squeeze.

===Chinese===
科林伍德站起身来，友好地在少年肩头捏了一把。

===Original===
“All right!” he said. “Now you seem a smart, intelligent lad﻿—don’t mention a word to anyone of what we’ve been talking about. You have not mentioned it before, I suppose? Not a word? That’s right﻿—don’t. Come in again tomorrow morning to see if I want you to be here as usual. I’m going to put a manager into this shop.”

===Chinese===
“好啦！”他说。“你这少年瞧着机灵懂事——咱们方才谈的话，对谁也不许提一个字。你先前也没对人提起过吧？一个字也没提过？那就好——不许提。明儿早上再进来一趟，瞧瞧我是不是还要你照常到铺子里来。我打算往这爿铺子里安一位经理。”

===Original===
When the boy had gone Collingwood locked up the shop from the house side, put the key in his pocket, and went into the kitchen.

===Chinese===
少年去后，科林伍德从住家这一面把铺子上锁，钥匙揣进衣袋，走进厨房。

===Original===
“Mrs. Clough,” he said. “I want to see the clothes which my grandfather was wearing when he was brought home last night. Where are they?”

===Chinese===
“克拉夫太太，”他说。“我想看看我祖父昨晚被抬回家时身上那套衣裳。放在哪儿？”

===Original===
“They’re in that little room aside of his bedchamber, Mestur Collingwood,” replied the housekeeper. “I laid ’em all there, on the clothespress, just as they were taken off of him, by Lawyer Eldrick’s orders﻿—he said they hadn’t been examined, and wasn’t to be, till you came. Nobody whatever’s touched ’em since.”

===Chinese===
“在他卧房隔壁那间小屋里，克林伍德先生，”女管家答道。“俺全摆在那屋的衣柜上了，照着从他身上褪下来时的原样摆的——这是埃尔德里克律师吩咐的——他说那些衣裳还没有查验过，你来了以前不许查验。打那以后，无论谁都没碰过一下。”

===Original===
Collingwood went upstairs and into the little room﻿—a sort of box-room opening out of that in which the old man lay. There were the clothes; he went through the pockets of every garment. He found such things as keys, a purse, loose money, a memorandum book, a bookseller’s catalogue or two, two or three letters of a business sort﻿—but there was no big folded paper, covered with writing, such as Jabey Naylor had described.

===Chinese===
科林伍德上了楼，走进那间小屋——一间从老人停灵那间屋子开门进去的杂物小间。衣裳就在那儿；他把每一件的口袋都翻检了一遍。钥匙、钱夹、零星银钱、一本备忘簿、一两册书商目录、两三封生意往来的书信——诸如此类的物件都翻着了，唯独没有那样一张折叠的大纸，写得满满的那种——杰贝兹·奈勒所形容的那张。

===Original===
The mention of that paper had excited Collingwood’s curiosity. He rapidly summed up what he had learned. His grandfather had found a paper, closely written upon, in a book which had been the property of John Mallathorpe, deceased. The discovery had surprised him, for he had given voice to an exclamation of what was evidently astonishment. He had put the paper in his pocket. Then he had written a letter﻿—to Mrs. Mallathorpe of Normandale Grange. When his shop-boy had posted that letter, he himself had gone out﻿—to his solicitor. What, asked Collingwood, was the reasonable presumption? The old man had gone to Eldrick to show him the paper which he had found.

===Chinese===
那张纸一经提起，科林伍德的好奇心便被撩拨起来了。他飞快地把已知的事实归拢了一遍。祖父在一部故人约翰·马拉索普的旧藏里，发现了一张写满字迹的纸。这个发现叫他吃惊不小——因为他分明发出了一声惊叹，听得出是错愕万分。他把那张纸放进了口袋。接着他写了一封信——写给诺曼代尔庄园的马拉索普太太。店里的伙计把信投出去之后，他自己便出了门——去见他的事务律师。那么，科林伍德自问，按情理该作何推想？老人是上埃尔德里克那儿，把他发现的那张纸拿给他过目去了。

===Original===
He lingered in the little room for a few minutes, thinking. No one but Pratt had been with Antony Bartle at the time of his seizure and sudden death. What sort of a fellow was Pratt? Was he honest? Was his word to be trusted? Had he told the precise truth about the old man’s death? He was evidently a suave, polite, obliging sort of fellow, this clerk, but it was a curious thing that if Antony Bartle had that paper, whatever it was﻿—in his pocket when he went to Eldrick’s office it should not be in his pocket still﻿—if his clothing had really remained untouched. Already suspicion was in Collingwood’s mind﻿—vague and indefinable, but there.

===Chinese===
他在小屋里又盘桓了几分钟，心里琢磨着。安东尼·巴特尔发病猝然去世的时候，陪伴在侧的只有普拉特一人。普拉特是个什么样的人？他诚实吗？他的话靠得住吗？关于老人的死，他讲的确实句句是实情吗？这位文书，看得出是个圆通客气、殷勤随和的角色，可是有一桩蹊跷事：倘若安东尼·巴特尔揣着那张纸——不管它究竟是什么——去了埃尔德里克的事务所，那张纸此刻就该仍在他口袋里——前提是那身衣裳当真无人动过。疑心已然在科林伍德心里生了根——影影绰绰，说不分明，可它确确实实在那儿。

===Original===
He was half inclined to go straight back to Eldrick & Pascoe’s and tell Eldrick what Jabey Naylor had just told him. But he reflected that while Naylor went out to post the letter, the old bookseller might have put the paper elsewhere; locked it up in his safe, perhaps. One thing, however, he, Collingwood, could do at once﻿—he could ask Mrs. Mallathorpe if the letter referred to the paper. He was fully acquainted with all the facts of the Mallathorpe history; old Bartle, knowing they would interest his grandson, had sent him the local newspaper accounts of its various episodes. It was only twelve miles to Normandale Grange﻿—a motorcar would carry him there within the hour. He glanced at his watch﻿—just ten o’clock.

===Chinese===
他一时很想径直折回埃尔德里克—帕斯科的事务所，把杰贝兹·奈勒方才这番话一五一十告诉埃尔德里克。可他转念一想，纳勒出门投信那会儿，老书商兴许已把那张纸挪到了旁的地方；说不定锁进了他的保险柜。不过有一件事，他科林伍德眼下立刻就能办——他可以去问马拉索普太太，那封信说的是不是就是那张纸。马拉索普家这段始末根由，他早已知之甚详；老巴特尔料定孙儿会对这些事感兴趣，当初本地报纸对桩桩件件的报道，都给他剪寄了过去。到诺曼代尔庄园不过十二英里——一辆汽车，一个钟头之内就能把他载到。他看了一眼表——恰好十点。

===Original===
An hour later, Collingwood found himself standing in a fine oak-panelled room, the windows of which looked out on a romantic valley whose thickly wooded sides were still bright with the red and yellow tints of autumn. A door opened﻿—he turned, expecting to see Mrs. Mallathorpe. Instead, he found himself looking at a girl, who glanced inquiringly at him, and from him to the card which he had sent in on his arrival.

===Chinese===
一个钟头之后，科林伍德发觉自己已然立在一间嵌着橡木护板的上好房间里，几扇窗外望去，是一道景致如画的山谷，谷坡上林木蓊郁，犹自焕着秋日红黄斑驳的颜色。一扇门开了——他转过身，满以为进来的是马拉索普太太。不料抬眼望去，却是一位姑娘，正带着探询的神色望他，又从他身上，望到他那张到场时递进来的名片。

## IV. The Fortunate Possessors / 四 走运的继承人

===Original===
Collingwood at once realized that he was in the presence of one of the two fortunate young people who had succeeded so suddenly—and, according to popular opinion, so unexpectedly—to John Mallathorpe’s wealth. This was evidently Miss Nesta Mallathorpe, of whom he had heard, but whom he had never seen. She, however, was looking at him as if she knew him, and she smiled a little as she acknowledged his bow.

===Chinese===
科林伍德立刻明白，眼前这一位，正是骤然间——而且照众人的说法，还是全然出乎意料地——继承了约翰·马拉索普家产的那两个走运年轻人之一。这分明就是妮斯塔·马拉索普（Nesta Mallathorpe）小姐，其人他久有耳闻，却从未见过。她呢，打量他的神情倒像早已认得他一般，还礼的时候微微一笑。

===Original===
“My mother is out in the grounds, with my brother,” she said, motioning Collingwood towards a chair. “Won’t you sit down, please?—I’ve sent for her; she will be here in a few minutes.”

===Chinese===
“母亲跟哥哥到园子里去了，”她说着，示意科林伍德落座。“请坐，好吗？——我已经打发人去请她了，她一会儿就到。”

===Original===
Collingwood sat down; Nesta Mallathorpe sat down, too, and as they looked at each other she smiled again.

===Chinese===
科林伍德坐下了；妮斯塔·马拉索普也坐了下来，两人相视之际，她又笑了一笑。

===Original===
“I have seen you before, Mr. Collingwood,” she said. “I knew it must be you when they brought up your card.”

===Chinese===
“我从前见过您，科林伍德先生，”她说。“他们把您的名片递上来时，我就知道必定是您。”

===Original===
Collingwood used his glance of polite inquiry to make a closer inspection of his hostess. He decided that Nesta Mallathorpe was not so much pretty as eminently attractive—a tall, well-developed, warm-coloured young woman, whose clear grey eyes and red lips and general bearing indicated the possession of good health and spirits. And he was quite certain that if he had ever seen her before he would not have forgotten it.

===Chinese===
科林伍德借着客气探询的一瞥，把这位女主人细细端详了一番。他断定，妮斯塔·马拉索普与其说漂亮，不如说格外动人——一个身材高挑、体态丰盈、面色红润的年轻姑娘，清澈的灰眼睛、红润的嘴唇、通身的气度，无不显出体魄康健、精神饱满。而且他十分确信，倘若从前真见过她，决计不至于忘记。

===Original===
“Where have you seen me?” he asked, smiling back at her.

===Chinese===
“您在哪儿见过我？”他问，也朝她报以微笑。

===Original===
“Have you forgotten the mock-trial—year before last?” she asked.

===Chinese===
“您忘了前年那回模拟审判了？”她问。

===Original===
Collingwood remembered what she was alluding to. He had taken part, in company with various other law students, in a mock-trial, a breach of promise case, for the benefit of a certain London hospital, to him had fallen one of the principal parts, that of counsel for the plaintiff. “When I saw your name, I remembered it at once,” she went on. “I was there—I was a probationer at St. Chad’s Hospital at that time.”

===Chinese===
科林伍德想起她所指何事了。前年，他曾同另外一些法科学生一起，参加了一场模拟审判——一桩毁弃婚约的案子，为伦敦某家医院筹款义演，他分到的正是主要角色之一，充当原告一方的出庭律师。“我一见您的名字，立刻就想起来了，”她接着说。“当时我在场——那阵子我正在圣查德医院（St. Chad’s）当见习护士。”

===Original===
“Dear me!” said Collingwood, “I should have thought our histrionic efforts would have been forgotten. I’m afraid I don’t remember much about them, except that we had a lot of fun out of the affair. So you were at St. Chad’s?” he continued, with a reminiscence of the surroundings of the institution they were talking of. “Very different to Normandale!”

===Chinese===
“哎呀！”科林伍德说，“我还当我们台上的那番卖力早叫人忘干净了呢。恐怕我自己也记不得多少了，只记得那一遭我们乐了个够。这么说，您是在圣查德医院待过？”他接着说，脑海里浮现出他们所说的那家医院的四近景象。“跟诺曼代尔可是大不相同！”

===Original===
“Yes,” she replied. “Very—very different to Normandale. But when I was at St. Chad’s, I didn’t know that I—that we should ever come to Normandale.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，”她答道。“跟诺曼代尔——太不相同了。可我在圣查德医院那会儿，并不知道我——并不知道我们有一天会住到诺曼代尔来。”

===Original===
“And now that you are here?” he asked.

===Chinese===
“那么如今到了这儿，又如何呢？”他问。

===Original===
The girl looked out through the big window on the valley which lay in front of the old house, and she shook her head a little.

===Chinese===
姑娘透过大窗，望着老宅前面横着的那道山谷，微微摇了摇头。

===Original===
“It’s very beautiful,” she answered, “but I sometimes wish I was back at St. Chad’s—with something to do. Here—there’s nothing to do but to do nothing.” Collingwood realized that this was not the complaint of the well-to-do young woman who finds time hang heavy—it was rather indicative of a desire for action.

===Chinese===
“这儿很美，”她答道，“可我有时倒情愿回到圣查德医院去——好歹有事可做。在这儿——除了无所事事，再无事可做。”科林伍德听得出，这可不是那种闲得发慌的阔小姐的抱怨——倒更透露出一股渴望有所作为的心气。

===Original===
“I understand!” he said. “I think I should feel like that. One wants—I suppose—is it action, movement, what is it?”

===Chinese===
“我明白！”他说。“我想我也会有同感。人总得要——我说不好——是行动，是活动，究竟是要什么？”

===Original===
“Better call it occupation—that’s a plain term,” she answered. “We’re both suffering from lack of occupation here, my brother and I. And it’s bad for us—especially for him.”

===Chinese===
“不如就管它叫‘有事可做’——这是个直白的字眼，”她答道。“我们兄妹俩在这儿都害着没事可做的病。这于我们有害——尤其于他。”

===Original===
Before Collingwood could think of any suitable reply to this remarkably fresh and candid statement, the door opened, and Mrs. Mallathorpe came in, followed by her son. And the visitor suddenly and immediately noticed the force and meaning of Nesta Mallathorpe’s last remark. Harper Mallathorpe, a good-looking, but not remarkably intelligent appearing young man, of about Collingwood’s own age, gave him the instant impression of being bored to death; the lacklustre eye, the aimless lounge, the hands thrust into the pockets of his Norfolk jacket as if they took refuge there from sheer idleness—all these things told their tale. Here, thought Collingwood, was a fine example of how riches can be a curse—relieved of the necessity of having to earn his daily bread by labour, Harper Mallathorpe was finding life itself laborious.

===Chinese===
这句新鲜爽利得出奇的话，科林伍德还没来得及想好一句得体的答语，门便开了，马拉索普太太走了进来，身后跟着她儿子。客人当下便领略到妮斯塔·马拉索普方才那句话的分量和意味。哈珀·马拉索普（Harper Mallathorpe）是个长相体面的青年，瞧着却不见得有多少聪明，年纪与科林伍德不相上下，给客人的第一印象便是百无聊赖到了极点：眼神黯淡无光，站相没精打采，两只手插在诺福克外套的口袋里，仿佛纯属闲得没处安放才躲进去避难似的——凡此种种，都在不言之中道了个明白。瞧这，科林伍德心里想，钱财何以能成为祸害，这儿便是个现成的好标本——挣每日面包的劳苦既已免了，哈珀·马拉索普反倒觉得活着本身就够费劲的了。

===Original===
But there was nothing of aimlessness, idleness, or lack of vigour in Mrs. Mallathorpe. She was a woman of character, energy, of brains—Collingwood saw all that at one glance. A little, neat-figured, compact sort of woman, still very good-looking, still on the right side of fifty, with quick movements and sharp glances out of a pair of shrewd eyes: this, he thought, was one of those women who will readily undertake the control and management of big affairs. He felt, as Mrs. Mallathorpe turned inquiring looks on him, that as long as she was in charge of them the Mallathorpe family fortunes would be safe.

===Chinese===
可马拉索普太太身上，没有一丝散漫、懈怠或乏力。她是个有性子、有精力、有头脑的女人——这些，科林伍德一眼便看了出来。小巧玲珑、身段利落的一个女人，风韵犹存，还不满五十，动作麻利，一双精明的眼睛目光尖利：这样的人物，他想，正是那种说担就担、担得起大事掌管调度的一类女人。当马拉索普太太转过身来、用探询的眼光打量他时，他感觉到，只要有她掌着舵，马拉索普家的家业便稳当无虞。

===Original===
“Mother,” said Nesta, handing Collingwood’s card to Mrs. Mallathorpe, “this gentleman is Mr. Bartle Collingwood. He’s—aren’t you?—yes, a barrister. He wants to see you. Why, I don’t know. I have seen Mr. Collingwood before—but he didn’t remember me. Now he’ll tell you what he wants to see you about.”

===Chinese===
“母亲，”妮斯塔说着，把科林伍德的名片递给马拉索普太太，“这位先生是巴特尔·科林伍德先生。他是——您不就是吗？——对，是位出庭律师。他要见您。为着什么事，我可不知道。这位科林伍德先生我从前见过——只是他没记住我。这会儿他该告诉您，他要见您为的是什么了。”

===Original===
“If you’ll allow me to explain why I called on you, Mrs. Mallathorpe,” said Collingwood, “I don’t suppose you ever heard of me—but you know, at any rate, the name of my grandfather, Mr. Antony Bartle, the bookseller, of Barford? My grandfather is dead—he died very suddenly last night.”

===Chinese===
“请容我说明冒昧登门的缘由吧，马拉索普太太，”科林伍德说，“我想您从没听说过我这个人——不过我祖父的名字，巴福德的书商安东尼·巴特尔先生，您总归是知道的吧？我祖父去世了——就在昨儿夜里，去得非常突然。”

===Original===
Mrs. Mallathorpe and Nesta murmured words of polite sympathy. Harper suddenly spoke—as if mere words were some relief to his obvious boredom.

===Chinese===
马拉索普太太和妮斯塔低声说了几句客套的吊慰话。哈珀突然开了口——仿佛开口说说话，多少能排遣一下他那显而易见的烦闷似的。

===Original===
“I heard that, this morning,” he said, turning to his mother. “Hopkins told me—he was in town last night. I meant to tell you.”

===Chinese===
“今儿早上我听说了，”他说着，转向母亲。“霍普金斯（Hopkins）告诉我的——他昨儿晚上在镇上来着。我本想告诉您的。”

===Original===
“Dear me!” exclaimed Mrs. Mallathorpe, glancing at some letters which stood on a rack above the mantelpiece. “Why—I had a letter from Mr. Bartle this very morning!”

===Chinese===
“哎呀！”马拉索普太太叫了出来，瞥了一眼壁炉架上方格子里立着的几封信。“可不是——今儿早上我还收到了巴特尔先生的一封信呢！”

===Original===
“It is that letter that I have come to see you about,” said Collingwood. “I only got down here from London at half-past eight this morning, and of course, I have made some inquiries about the circumstances of my grandfather’s sudden death. He died very suddenly indeed at Mr. Eldrick’s office. He had gone there on some business about which nobody knows nothing—he died before he could mention it. And according to his shop-boy, Jabey Naylor, the last thing he did was to write a letter to you. Now—I have reason for asking—would you mind telling me, Mrs. Mallathorpe, what that letter was about?” Mrs. Mallathorpe moved over to the hearth, and took an envelope from the rack. She handed it to Collingwood, indicating that he could open it. And Collingwood drew out one of old Bartle’s memorandum forms, and saw a couple of lines in the familiar crabbed handwriting:

===Chinese===
“我正是为着那封信来见您的，”科林伍德说。“今儿早上八点半我才从伦敦下来，当然，已就祖父猝然去世的情形打听了一番。他确实是猝死在埃尔德里克先生的事务所里的。他去那里办一桩什么事，谁也不知道——话没来得及出口，人就去了。而据他店里的小伙计杰比·奈勒（Jabey Naylor）说，他生前做的最后一件事，就是给您写了一封信。那么——我这样问是有缘由的——您可否告诉我，马拉索普太太，那封信写的是什么事？”马拉索普太太走到壁炉边，从格架上取下一只信封，递给科林伍德，示意他可以拆开。科林伍德抽出一张老巴特尔的便笺，只见那笔熟悉的潦草字迹写了两行：

===Original===
Mrs. Mallathorpe, Normandale Grange.

===Chinese===
马拉索普太太，诺曼代尔庄园。

===Original===
Madam—If you should drive into town tomorrow, will you kindly give me a call? I want to see you particularly.

===Chinese===
夫人——倘若您明日驱车进城，可否赏光到小店一叙？我有要事想见您。

===Original===
Respectfully, A. Bartle.

===Chinese===
谨上，A. 巴特尔。

===Original===
Collingwood handed back the letter.

===Chinese===
科林伍德把信递了回去。

===Original===
“Have you any idea to what that refers?” he asked.

===Chinese===
“这信指的是什么，您可有头绪？”他问。

===Original===
“Well, I think I have—perhaps,” answered Mrs. Mallathorpe. “Mr. Bartle persuaded us to sell him some books—local books—which my late brother-in-law had at his office in the mill. And since then he has been very anxious to buy more local books and pamphlets about this neighbourhood, and he had some which Mr. Bartle was very anxious indeed to get hold of. I suppose he wanted to see me about that.” Collingwood made no remarks for the moment. He was wondering whether or not to tell what Jabey Naylor had told him about this paper taken from the linen pocket inside the *History of Barford*. But Mrs. Mallathorpe’s ready explanation had given him a new idea, and he rose from his chair.

===Chinese===
“这个嘛，我想我知道些——兴许是吧，”马拉索普太太答道。“巴特尔先生先前劝得我们卖给他一批书——都是本地书——原是我那位已故的大伯子搁在厂里办公室的。打那以后，他一直急切地想再多收些有关这一带的本埠书刊小册子，而我们手头就有几册，是巴特尔先生千方百计巴望弄到手的。我猜他要见我，为的就是这个。”科林伍德一时没有作声。他正在盘算，要不要把杰比·奈勒说的那件事告诉她——就是从《巴福德史》封皮里侧的亚麻夹袋中取出的那张纸。可马拉索普太太这一番现成的解释给了他一个新的想法，他便从椅子上站起身来。

===Original===
“Thank you,” he said. “I suppose that’s it. You may think it odd that I wanted to know what he’d written about, but as it was certainly the last letter he wrote—”

===Chinese===
“谢谢您，”他说。“我想就是这么回事了。您或许觉得奇怪，我何必打听他信里写了些什么——可这毕竟是他生前写的最后一封信——”

===Original===
“Oh, I’m quite sure it must have been that!” exclaimed Mrs. Mallathorpe. “And as I am going into Barford this afternoon, in any case, I meant to call at Mr. Bartle’s. I’m sorry to hear of his death, poor old gentleman! But he was very old indeed, wasn’t he?”

===Chinese===
“哦，我敢肯定必是那么回事！”马拉索普太太叫道。“反正今儿下午我横竖要进巴福德去，原就打算到巴特尔先生铺子里走一趟的。听闻他去世，我心里难过，可怜的老先生！不过他实在够高寿的了，可不是吗？”

===Original===
