“Why,” continued Cobcroft, “it isn’t what you rightly would call a secret—though I don’t think anybody knows aught about it but myself! It was just this—and it may be there’s naught in it but a mere fancy o’ mine. That morning, before the accident happened, I was in and out of the private office a good deal—carrying in and out letters, and account books, and so on. Mr. John Mallathorpe’s private office, ye’ll understand, sir, opened out of our countinghouse—as it does still—the present manager, Mr. Horsfall, has it, just as it was. Well, now, on one occasion, when I went in there, to take a ledger back to the safe, Mr. Mallathorpe had his manager and cashier, Gaukrodger and Marshall in with him. Mr. Mallathorpe, he always used a stand-up desk to write at—never wrote sitting down, though he had a big desk in the middle of the room that he used to sit at to look over accounts or talk to people. Now when I went in, he and Gaukrodger and Marshall were all at this stand-up desk—in the window-place—and they were signing some papers. At least Gaukrodger had just signed a paper, and Marshall was taking the pen from him. ‘Sign there, Marshall,’ says Mr. Mallathorpe. And then he went on, ‘Now we’ll sign this other—it’s well to have these things in duplicate, in case one gets lost.’ And then—well, then, I went out, and—why, that was all.”

===Chinese===
“是这么回事，”科布克罗夫特接着说，“这事其实算不得您所说的那种秘密——不过俺琢磨着，除了俺自个儿，怕是没人知道半个字！事情就是这样——兴许这里头啥也没有，不过是俺一个人的瞎想。那天早上，出事之前，俺在私人办公室里进进出出好几趟——把信件、账本什么的抱进抱出。您要晓得，先生，约翰·马拉索普先生的私人办公室，门就开在俺们账房里——如今还是这样——现任经理霍斯福尔先生还照原样用它。唔，有那么一回，俺进去把一本总账放回保险柜，马拉索普先生正把他的经理长和出纳主任——高克罗杰和马歇尔——叫在里头。马拉索普先生写字，向来用一张立式写字台——从来不坐着写，虽说屋子正当中还有张大书桌，他看账目、同人谈话的时候也常坐在那儿。俺进去那会儿，他同高克罗杰、马歇尔都凑在那张立式写字台跟前——在窗户那一块——正签着几份文书。至少是高克罗杰刚签完一份，马歇尔正打他手里接笔。‘你签在这儿，马歇尔，’马拉索普先生说。接着他又往下说：‘咱们再把这份也签了——这种东西一式两份才稳妥，防着哪一份弄丢了。’然后——唔，然后俺就出来了，再——唉，就是这么回事。”

===Original===
“You’ve some idea in your mind about that,” said Collingwood, who had watched Cobcroft closely as he talked. “What is it?”

===Chinese===
“您心里对这事存着些想法，”科林伍德说——方才他一直紧盯着科布克罗夫特说话。“是什么？”

===Original===
Cobcroft smiled—and looked round as if to ascertain that they were alone. “Why!” he answered in a low voice. “I’ll tell you what I did wonder—some time afterwards. I dare say you’re aware—it was all in the papers—that Mr. John Mallathorpe died intestate?”

===Chinese===
科布克罗夫特笑了笑——还四下里张了张，仿佛要确认只有他们两个在场。“是这么着！”他压低嗓音答道。“俺告诉您，事后过了些时候，俺确实犯过一回嘀咕。想必您知道——报上全登过——约翰·马拉索普先生死的时候，没立下遗嘱？”

===Original===
“Yes,” asserted Collingwood. “I know that.”

===Chinese===
“是，”科林伍德断言道。“这个我知道。”

===Original===
“I’ve oft wondered,” continued Cobcroft, “if that could ha’ been his will that they were signing! But then I reflected a bit on matters. And there were two or three things that made me say naught at all—not a word. First of all, I considered it a very unlikely thing that a rich man like Mr. John Mallathorpe would make a will for himself. Second—I remembered that very soon after I’d been in his private office Marshall came out into the countinghouse and gave the office lad a lot of letters and documents to take to the post—some of ’em big envelopes—and I thought that what I’d seen signed was some agreement or other that was in one of them. And third—and most important—no will was ever found in any of Mr. John Mallathorpe’s drawers or safes or anywhere, though they turned things upside down at the office, and, I heard, at his house as well. Of course, you see, sir, supposing that to have been a will—why, the only two men who could possibly have proved it was were dead and gone! They were killed with him. And of course, the young people, the nephew and niece, they came in for everything—so there was an end of it. But—I’ve oft wondered what those papers were. One thing is certain, anyway!” concluded Cobcroft, with a grim laugh, “when those three signed ’em, they were picking up their pens for the last time!”

===Chinese===
“俺就不止一回犯过嘀咕，”科布克罗夫特接着说，“他们签的，莫不就是他的遗嘱！可后来俺把事情前后掂量了一番。有两三样缘故，叫俺干脆啥也不说——半个字也不说。头一样，俺寻思，像约翰·马拉索普先生那样的阔人，自个儿动手写遗嘱，实在不大像会有的事。第二样——俺记得，俺打他私人办公室出来没多久，马歇尔就出到账房里，交给小听差一大沓信件文书，叫他送去付邮——有几个还是大信封——俺就想着，俺瞧见签的那份，兴许是件什么契约，装在其中一封里。第三样——也是最要紧的一样——约翰·马拉索普先生的抽屉啊、保险柜啊，还有什么别的地方，压根儿没搜出过遗嘱，虽说厂里把东西翻了个底朝天，听说他家里也翻了个底朝天。您瞧，先生，当然啦，那要真是份遗嘱——嗐，天底下能证明它是遗嘱的，拢共就那两个人，可俩人全都死了没了！同他一块儿罹难了。再说，两位年轻人，侄子和侄女，顺理成章把一切都承继了——这桩事也就到此为止。可是——俺不止一回琢磨，那几份文书到底是什么。反正有一桩是笃定的！”科布克罗夫特惨然一笑，收梢道，“那三位提笔签字的时候，正是他们这辈子最后一回提笔！”

===Original===
“How long was it—after you saw the signing of those papers—that the accident occurred?” asked Collingwood.

===Chinese===
“从您瞧见签那几份文书——到出事，中间过了多久？”科林伍德问。

===Original===
“It ’ud be twelve or fifteen minutes, as near as I can recollect,” replied Cobcroft. “A few minutes after I’d left the private office, Gaukrodger came out of it, alone, and stood at the door leading into the yard, looking up at the chimney. The steeplejack was just coming down, and his mate was waiting for him at the bottom. Gaukrodger turned back to the private office and called Mr. Mallathorpe out. All three of ’em, Mallathorpe, Gaukrodger, Marshall, went out and walked across the yard to the chimney foot. They stood there talking a bit—and then—down it came!”

===Chinese===
“俺估摸着，总有十二到十五分钟吧，”科布克罗夫特答道。“俺离开私人办公室没几分钟，高克罗杰一个人从里头出来，站在通场院的那扇门口，仰着脑袋瞧那根烟囱。尖塔检修工正往下下来，他的同伴在底下候着他。高克罗杰折回私人办公室，把马拉索普先生唤了出来。他们仨——马拉索普、高克罗杰、马歇尔——一同出来，穿过场院，走到烟囱根脚下。他们站在那儿说了几句——跟着——轰，塌下来了！”

===Original===
Collingwood thought matters over. Supposing that the document which Cobcroft spoke of as being in process of execution before him were indeed duplicate copies of a will. What could have been done with them, in the few minutes which elapsed between the signing and the catastrophe to the chimney? It was scarcely likely that John Mallathorpe would have sent them away by post. If they had been deposited in his own pocket, they would have been found when his clothing was removed and examined. If they were in the private office when the three men left it—

===Chinese===
科林伍德把事情前前后后想了一遍。倘若科布克罗夫特所说的、当着他的面正在签署的那件文书，当真是一式两份的遗嘱，那么从签字到烟囱塌坍的那几分钟里，那两份东西可能有什么去处？拿去邮寄，约翰·马拉索普似不大可能。若揣在他自己衣兜里，替他解衣验看的时候就该搜出来了。倘若三个人离开私人办公室时，它们还搁在里头——

===Original===
“You’re sure the drawers, safe and so on in Mr. Mallathorpe’s room were thoroughly searched—after his death?” he asked.

===Chinese===
“您能担保，马拉索普先生屋里那些抽屉呀、保险柜呀等等，在他死后都彻底搜查过了？”他问。

===Original===
“I should think they were!” answered Cobcroft laconically. “I helped at that, myself. There wasn’t as much as an old invoice that was not well fingered and turned over. No!—I came to the conclusion that what I’d seen signed was some contract or something—sent off there and then by the lad to post.”

===Chinese===
“那还用说！”科布克罗夫特言简意赅地答道。“俺还亲自动手帮着搜来着。连一张旧发票都没漏过，张张都翻检过、倒腾过。没了！——俺后来得出结论：俺瞧见签的那份，是件什么合同之类——当场就叫那小听差拿去付邮了。”

===Original===
Collingwood made no further remark and asked no more questions. But he thought long and seriously that night, and he came to certain conclusions. First: what Cobcroft had seen signed was John Mallathorpe’s will. Second: John Mallathorpe had made it himself and had taken the unusual course of making a duplicate copy. Third: John Mallathorpe had probably slipped the copy into the *History of Barford* which was in his private office when he went out to speak to the steeplejack. Fourth: that copy had come into Linford Pratt’s hands through Antony Bartle.

===Chinese===
科林伍德没再说什么，也没再问什么。可是那天夜里，他久久地、认认真真地想了又想，得出了几条结论。第一：科布克罗夫特瞧见签署的，正是约翰·马拉索普的遗嘱。第二：约翰·马拉索普亲自动手立了这份遗嘱，还破例做成一式两份。第三：约翰·马拉索普八成在出去同尖塔检修工讲话的当口，把那份副本塞进了他私人办公室里那部《巴福德史》。第四：那份副本经过安东尼·巴特尔之手，落进了林福德·普拉特的手里。

===Original===
And now arose two big questions. What were the terms of that will? And—where was the duplicate copy? He was still putting these to himself when noon of the next day came and brought Eldrick and Byner for the promised serious consultation.

===Chinese===
于是冒出了两个大问题。那份遗嘱的条款是什么？还有——那份副本如今在哪里？他还在心里这么自问的当口，第二天正午到了，埃尔德里克同拜纳如约前来，赴那场说定了的郑重商议。

## XX. The Green Man / 二十 ‘绿人’酒馆

===Original===
Byner, in taking his firm’s advertisement for Parrawhite to the three Barford newspaper offices, had done so with a special design﻿—he wanted Pratt to see that a serious wish to discover Parrawhite was alive in more quarters than one. He knew that Pratt was almost certain to see Eldrick’s advertisement in his own name; now he wanted Pratt to see another advertisement of the same nature in another name. Already he had some suspicion that Pratt had not told Eldrick the truth about Parrawhite, and that nothing would suit him so well as that Parrawhite should never be heard of or mentioned again: now he wished Pratt to learn that Parrawhite was much wanted, and was likely to be much mentioned﻿—wherefore the supplementary advertisements with Halstead & Byner’s name attached. It was extremely unlikely that Pratt could fail to see those advertisements.

===Chinese===
拜纳把他那家事务所寻访帕拉怀特的广告送到巴福德三家报馆去登，本是有其特别的用意——他要让普拉特瞧明白，想要寻出帕拉怀特的，可不止一处一方。他知道，埃尔德里克以本人名义登的那则广告，普拉特几乎必定会看到；如今他还要叫普拉特看到另一则同样性质、却换了名头的广告。他早已有些疑心：普拉特对埃尔德里克讲的帕拉怀特的事并非实话；而对普拉特来说，天下顶称心的，莫过于帕拉怀特从此杳无音讯、再没人提起。所以眼下他偏要叫普拉特知道，帕拉怀特正被人急切寻访着，往后多半还要屡屡被人提起——于是才添了这几则署着哈尔斯特德—拜纳名字的补充广告。这几则广告要还能逃过普拉特的眼睛，那是万万不可能的事。

===Original===
There were three newspapers in Barford: one a morning journal of large circulation throughout the county; the other two, evening journals, which usually appeared in three or four editions. As Byner stipulated for large type, and a prominent position, in the personal column of each, it was scarcely within the bounds of probability that a townsman like Pratt would miss seeing the advertisement. Most likely he would see it in all three newspapers. And if he had also seen Eldrick’s similar advertisement, he would begin to think, and then﻿—

===Chinese===
巴福德有三家报纸：一家是晨报，销路遍及全郡；另外两家是晚报，通常一天要出三四版。拜纳特意讲定，各报的人事专栏里都要用大号铅字、占显眼的位置。像普拉特这样一位镇上人，要说会漏看这广告，几乎不在情理之中。多半三家报纸他都会看到。倘若他又瞧见了埃尔德里克那则相仿的广告，他就该动起脑筋来了，跟着——

===Original===
“Why, then,” mused Byner, ruminating on his design, “then we will see what he will do!”

===Chinese===
“嗐，到那时候，”拜纳一面琢磨着自己的计策，一面自忖道，“咱们就瞧瞧他要怎么行事吧！”

===Original===
Meanwhile, there was something he himself wanted to do, and on the morning following his arrival in the town, he set out to do it. Byner had been much struck by Pickard’s account of his dealings with James Parrawhite on the evening which appeared to be the very last wherein Parrawhite was ever seen. He had watched the landlord of the Green Man closely as he told his story, and had set him down for an honest, if somewhat sly and lumpish soul, who was telling a plain tale to the best of his ability. Byner believed all the details of that story﻿—he even believed that when Parrawhite told Pickard that he would find him fifty pounds that evening, or early next day, he meant to keep his word. In the circumstances﻿—as far as Byner could reckon them up from what he had gathered﻿—it would not have paid Parrawhite to do otherwise. Byner put the situation to himself in this fashion﻿—Pratt had got hold of some secret which was being, or could be made to be, highly profitable to him. Parrawhite had discovered this, and was in a position to blackmail Pratt. Therefore Parrawhite would not wish to leave Pratt’s neighbourhood﻿—so long as there was money to be got out of Pratt, Parrawhite would stick to him like a leech. But if Parrawhite was to abide peaceably in Barford, he must pay Pickard that little matter of between fifty and sixty pounds. Accordingly, in Byner’s opinion, Parrawhite had every honest intention of returning to the Green Man on the evening of the twenty-third of November after having seen Pratt. And, in Byner’s further﻿—and very seriously considered﻿—opinion, the whole problem for solution﻿—possibly involving the solution of other and more important problems﻿—was this: Did Parrawhite meet Pratt that night, and if he did what took place between them which prevented Parrawhite from returning to Pickard?

===Chinese===
这当口，他自己另有一件事要办，到镇上的第二天早晨，他便动身去办了。皮卡德讲述了自己同詹姆斯·帕拉怀特打交道的那晚经过——那一晚看来正是帕拉怀特在人前露面的最后一晚——拜纳对此印象极深。‘绿人’酒馆的店主讲这段事的时候，他一直在细细端详，把他断定成一个老实人——虽说带几分狡黠，也带几分愚拙——正竭其所能，把一段实情平平实实地讲出来。那段事的种种细节，拜纳都信——他甚至相信，帕拉怀特对皮卡德说当天晚上、至迟第二天一早准给他弄来五十镑的时候，是真心打算践约的。照拜纳从已汇集的情形来盘算，处在他那个境地里，帕拉怀特不这么做，划不来。拜纳把这局面在心里掂量成这么一副棋——普拉特攥住了一桩秘密，这秘密正在给他、或者略加摆布就能给他带来天大的好处；帕拉怀特窥破了这一层，手里正握着可以敲诈普拉特的把柄。因此帕拉怀特不会愿意离开普拉特的左近——只要还能从普拉特身上榨出钱来，帕拉怀特就会像条水蛭似的死叮着他不放。可帕拉怀特要想在巴福德太太平平住下去，就得先还上皮卡德那笔五十到六十镑之间的小小账目。照此看来，拜纳认为，帕拉怀特在十一月二十三日见过普拉特之后，原本是满心打算回‘绿人’酒馆去的。而且，照拜纳更进一层——也是他郑重掂量过的——见解，待解的全部谜团——兴许还牵连着别的一些更要紧谜团的解答——归结起来就是这一条：那天夜里，帕拉怀特究竟见没见普拉特？若是见了，两人之间出了什么事，才叫帕拉怀特没能回到皮卡德那里去？

===Original===
It was in an endeavour to get at some first stage of a solution of this problem that Byner, having breakfasted at the Central Hotel on his second day in the town, went out immediately afterwards, asked his way to Whitcliffe, and was directed to an electric tram which started from the Town Hall Square, and after running through a district of tall warehouses and squat weaving-sheds, began a long and steady climb to the heights along the town. When he left it, he found himself in a district eminently characteristic of that part of the country. The tram set him down at a crossroads on a high ridge of land. Beneath him lay Barford, its towers and spires and the gables of its tall buildings showing amongst the smoke of its many chimneys. All about him lay open ground, broken by the numerous stone quarries of which Eldrick had spoken, and at a little distance along one of the four roads at the intersection of which he stood, he saw a few houses and cottages, one of which, taller and bigger than the rest, was distinguished by a pole, planted in front of its stone porch and bearing a swinging sign whereon was rudely painted the figure of a man in Lincoln green. Byner walked on to this, entered a flagged hall, and found himself confronting Pickard, who at sight of him, motioned him into a little parlour behind the bar.

===Chinese===
正是为了先摸到这谜团解答的第一级台阶，拜纳在到镇上的第二天，于中央旅馆（Central Hotel）用过早饭，便随即出门，打听去惠特克利夫怎么走，人家指点他坐一条由市政厅广场发车的电车。电车穿过一片高货栈与低织棚连成的街区，便顺着镇缘开始一段漫长而平稳的爬坡，往高地驶去。下车之后，他发觉自己置身的那片地界，十足体现着那一带乡野独有的风貌。电车把他撂在一道高地山脊的十字路口上。脚下横着巴福德，座座塔楼、尖顶和幢幢高楼的山墙，都掩映在满城烟囱吐出的烟霭里。四下里铺开的是旷野，叫埃尔德里克提起过的那许多采石坑切得七零八落。他站着的十字路口岔出四条路，沿其中一条走不多远，便望见稀稀落落几所房舍村屋，其中一所比旁的都高大，门前石门廊前竖着一根杆子，挑着一幅摇摇晃晃的招牌，上头粗粗地画着一个身着林肯绿（Lincoln green）衣装的人像。拜纳朝这所房子走去，跨进一间石板铺地的店堂，迎面正碰上皮卡德；皮卡德一见他，便招手请他进了酒柜后头的一间小客厅。

===Original===
“Mornin’, mister,” said he. “You’ll be all right in here﻿—there’s nobody about just now, and if my missis or any o’ t’ servant lasses sees yer, they’ll tak’ yer for a brewer’s traveller, or summat o’ that sort. Come to hev a look round, like﻿—what?”

===Chinese===
“早上好，先生，”他说。“您在这儿只管放心——眼下四下没人，就算俺家里的、或是哪个月钱丫头瞧见您，也只当您是酿酒行跑外的，或是那一号人。您这回是来四下里瞧瞧的，像——嗯？”

===Original===
“I want to have a look at the place where you told us Parrawhite was to meet Pratt that night,” replied Byner. “I thought you would perhaps be kind enough to show me where it is.”

===Chinese===
“我想瞧一瞧您跟我们说过的、帕拉怀特那晚要同普拉特碰头的地方，”拜纳答道。“想请您行个好，带我认认那地方在哪儿。”

===Original===
“I will, an’ all﻿—wi’ pleasure,” said the landlord, “but ye mun hev a drop o’ summat first﻿—try a glass o’ our ale,” he went on, with true Yorkshire hospitality. “I hev some bitter beer i’ my cellar such as I’ll lay owt ye couldn’t get t’ likes on down yonder i’ Barford﻿—no, nor i’ London neyther!﻿—I’ll just draw a jug.”

===Chinese===
“俺领路，一定——乐意效劳，”店主说，“不过您先得抿上一口啥的——尝尝俺们的麦芽酒，”他接着说，拿出地道的约克郡人的好客劲儿。“俺地窖里存的苦啤酒，俺敢拿随便什么打赌，您在底下巴福德弄不着同样的货色——不，伦敦也没有！——俺这就去打一壶来。”

===Original===
Byner submitted to this evidence of friendliness, and Pickard, after disappearing into a dark archway and down some deeply worn stone steps, came back with a foaming jug, the sight of which seemed to give him great delight. He gazed admiringly at the liquor which he presently poured into two tumblers, and drew his visitor’s attention to its colour.

===Chinese===
拜纳推却不过这番好意，只得从命。皮卡德钻进一道黑洞洞的拱门、踩着几级磨得深陷的石阶下去，回来时提着一壶浮沫四溢的酒；那壶酒一入眼，仿佛就叫他满心欢喜。他把酒斟进两只大玻璃杯，一面爱赏地端详着酒液，一面引着客人去留意它的色泽。

===Original===
“Reight stuff that, mister﻿—what?” he said. “I nobbut tapped that barril two days since, and I’d been keepin’ it twelve month, so you’ve come in for it at what they call t’ opportune moment. I say!” he went on, after pledging Byner and smacking his lips over the ale. “I heard summat last night ’at might be useful to you and Lawyer Eldrick﻿—about this here Parrawhite affair.”

===Chinese===
“地道的货色，先生——可不是？”他说。“那桶酒俺前两天才开，先前俺足足存了它十二个月，您赶上的，正是人家说的那个恰好当口。俺说！”同拜纳碰过杯、就着麦芽酒咂了咂嘴之后，他接着说，“昨儿夜里俺听着件事儿，兴许对您和埃尔德里克律师有用——就是帕拉怀特这桩事。”

===Original===
“Oh!” said Byner, at once interested. “What now?”

===Chinese===
“哦！”拜纳马上来了兴致。“讲的是什么？”

===Original===
“You’ll ha’ noticed, as you come along t’ road just now, ’at there’s a deal o’ stone quarries i’ this neighbourhood?” replied Pickard. “Well, now, of course, some o’ t’ quarry men comes in here. Last night theer wor sev’ral on ’em i’ t’ bar theer, talkin’, and one on ’em wor readin’ t’ evenin’ newspaper﻿—t’ *Barford Dispatch*. An’ he read out that theer advertisement about Parrawhite﻿—wi’ your address i’ London at t’ foot on it. Well, theer wor nowt said, except summat about advertisin’ for disappeared folk, but later on, one o’ t’ men, a young man, come to me, private like. ‘I say, Pickard,’ he says, ‘between you an’ me, worrn’t t’ name o’ that man ’at used to come in here on a Sunday sometimes, Parrawhite? It runs a’ my mind,’ he says, ‘ ’at I’ve heerd you call him by that name.’ ‘Well, an’ what if it wor?’ I says. ‘Nay, nowt much,’ he says, ‘but I see fro’ t’ *Dispatch* ’at he’s wanted, and I could tell a bit about him,’ he says. ‘What could ye tell?’ says I﻿—just like that theer. ‘Why,’ he says, ‘this much﻿—one night t’ last back-end﻿—’ ”

===Chinese===
“您方才一路打这儿过，该瞧见了，这一带采石坑多得很吧？”皮卡德答道。“是这么着，当然啦，采石坑上的工人常到俺这儿来。昨儿晚上就有好几个在酒柜那边唠嗑，其中一个看着晚报——看《巴福德快报》（Barford Dispatch）。他把报上那则寻帕拉怀特的广告念了出来——末尾还印着您在伦敦的地址。那会儿倒没人多说啥，只扯了几句登广告寻人的闲话。可后来，其中有个人，是个小伙子，私下里寻到俺跟前。‘俺说，皮卡德，’他说，‘这话就你知俺知，从前礼拜天时不时上你这儿来的那个人，是不是姓帕拉怀特？俺记得，’他说，‘听见过你这么叫过他。’‘嗯，是又怎么样？’俺说。‘嗐，倒没啥，’他说，‘不过俺从《快报》上瞧出来，这人正被人寻着哩，俺能讲出他一点儿事，’他说。‘你能讲个啥？’俺说——俺就这么问的。‘是这么回事，’他说，‘去年 back-end 的一天夜里——’”

===Original===
“Stop a bit, Mr. Pickard,” interrupted Byner. “What does that mean﻿—that term ‘back-end’?”

===Chinese===
“且慢，皮卡德先生，”拜纳插话道。“那个说法是什么意思——back-end 这个词？”

===Original===
“Why, it means t’ end o’ t’ year!” answered the landlord. “What some folks call autumn, d’ye understand? ‘One night t’ last back-end,’ says this young fellow, ‘I wor hengin’ about on t’ quiet at t’ end o’ Stubbs’ Lane,’ he says: ‘T’ truth wor,’ he says, ‘I wor waitin’ for a word wi’ a young woman ’at lives i’ that terrace at t’ top o’ Stubbs’ Lane﻿—she wor goin’ to come out and meet me for half an hour or so. An,’ he says, ‘I see’d that theer feller ’at I think I’ve heerd you call Parrawhite, come out o’ Stubbs’ Lane wi’ that lawyer chap ’at lives i’ t’ Terrace﻿—Pratt. I know Pratt,’ he says, ‘ ’cause them ’at he works for﻿—Eldricks﻿—once did a bit o’ law business for me.’ ‘Where did you see ’em go to, then?’ says I. ‘I see’d ’em cross t’ road into t’ owd quarry ground,’ he says. ‘I see’d ’em plain enough, tho’ they didn’t see me﻿—I wor keepin’ snug agen ’t wall﻿—it wor a moonlit night, that,’ he says. ‘Well,’ I says, ‘an’ what now?’ ‘Why,’ he says, ‘d’yer think I could get owt o’ this reward for tellin that theer?’ So I thowt pretty sharp then, d’ye see, mister. ‘I’ll tell yer what, mi lad,’ I says. ‘Say nowt to nobody﻿—keep your tongue still﻿—and I’ll tell ye tomorrow night what ye can do﻿—I shall see a man ’at’s on that job ’tween now and then,’ I says. So theer it is,” concluded Pickard, looking hard at Byner. “D’yer think this chap’s evidence ’ud be i’ your line?”

===Chinese===
“嗐，就是一年的末了呀！”店主答道。“也就是有些人说的秋天，明白吗？‘去年 back-end 的一天夜里，’这小伙子说，‘俺正悄悄地在斯塔布斯巷（Stubbs' Lane）口子上守着，’他说，‘实情是，’他说，‘俺在等个住巷顶上那排房子里的年轻姑娘出来搭句话——她要出来同俺会那么半个钟头。后来，’他说，‘俺就瞧见那个俺想听您叫过帕拉怀特的家伙，从斯塔布斯巷里出来，同着住在那排房子里的那个律师小子——普拉特。俺认得普拉特，’他说，‘因为他东家——埃尔德里克他们——从前替俺办过一件小小的法律事。’‘那他们上哪儿去了，你瞧见没？’俺说。‘俺瞧见他们过了马路，进了老采石场那片地界，’他说。‘俺瞧得清清楚楚，他们可没瞧见俺——俺紧贴着墙根藏得严严实实——那天夜里月亮明晃晃的，’他说。‘嗯，’俺说，‘后来呢？’‘嗐，’他说，‘您瞧，俺把这桩讲出来，能不能从那笔赏钱里分着一点儿？’俺那会儿脑筋转得飞快，您明白吗，先生。‘俺告诉你，小伙子，’俺说。‘跟谁都别吱声——把舌头管牢了——明儿夜里俺再告诉你该怎么办——打现在到那会儿，俺要见上一个正办这桩差事的人，’俺说。事情就是这样，”皮卡德说着收梢，眼睛紧盯着拜纳。“您瞧这小子的证词，合不合您那条道？”

===Original===
“Decidedly I do!” replied Byner. “Where is he to be found?”

===Chinese===
“太合了！”拜纳答道。“他上哪儿寻得着？”

===Original===
“I couldn’t say wheer he lives,” answered the landlord. “But it’ll be somewhere close about; anyway, he’ll be in here tonight. Bill Thomson t’ feller’s name is﻿—decent young feller enough.”

===Chinese===
“他住哪儿，俺说不上，”店主答道。“反正就在左近什么地方；不管怎么着，今儿夜里他会到俺这儿来。那小伙子名叫比尔·汤姆森（Bill Thomson）——是个挺正派的小伙子。”

===Original===
“I must contrive to see him, certainly,” said Byner. “Well, now, can you show me this Stubbs’ Lane and the neighbourhood?”

===Chinese===
“那我说什么也得见着他，”拜纳说。“好吧，现在，您能领我看看这斯塔布斯巷和左近一带吗？”

===Original===
“Just step along t’ road a bit and I’ll join you in a few o’ minutes,” assented Pickard. “We’d best not be seen leavin t’ house together, or our folk’ll think it’s a put-up job. Walk forrard a piece.”

===Chinese===
“您顺着大路先走一截，俺过几分钟就来会您，”皮卡德应承道。“俺们顶好别叫人瞧见一块儿出这屋子，不然俺们这儿的人该当是串通好的把戏。您先朝前走一段。”

===Original===
Byner strolled along the road a little way, and leaned over a wall until Mr. Pickard, wearing his white billycock hat and accompanied by a fine fox-terrier, lounged up with his thumbs in the armholes of his waistcoat. Together they went a little further along.

===Chinese===
拜纳沿着大路溜达了一小段，倚着一道墙候着，直到皮卡德先生戴着他那顶白色圆顶毡帽、带着一条上好的猎狐㹴，两个大拇指勾着马甲的袖孔，慢悠悠地踱了上来。两人又一道往前走了一小截。

===Original===
“Now then!” said the landlord, crossing the road towards the entrance of a narrow lane which ran between two high stone walls. “This here is Stubbs’ Lane﻿—so called, I believe, ’cause an owd gentleman named similar used to hev a house here ’at’s been pulled down. Ye see, it runs up fro’ this high road towards yon terrace o’ houses. Folks hereabouts calls that terrace t’ World’s End, ’cause they’re t’ last houses afore ye get on to t’ open moorlands. Now, that night ’at Parrawhite wor aimin’ to meet Pratt, it wor i’ this very lane. Pratt, when he left t’ tramcar, t’ other side o’ my place, ’ud come up t’ road, and up this lane. And it wor at t’ top o’ t’ lane ’at Bill Thomson see’d Pratt and Parrawhite cross into what Bill called t’ owd quarry ground.”

===Chinese===
“这就到了！”店主说着，穿过马路，朝一条夹在两道高石墙之间的窄巷口走去。“这就是斯塔布斯巷——俺琢磨着，是这么叫的缘故：从前有位同样姓斯塔布斯的老先生在这儿有所宅子，如今早拆掉了。您瞧，它打这条大路往上，通到那排房子。这一带的人都管那排房子叫‘世界尽头’（the World's End），因为再往前头，上了荒原，就没有人家了。帕拉怀特要会普拉特的那天夜里，就是在这么条巷子里。普拉特下了电车——在俺这儿的另一头——就得顺着大路走上来，再走上这条巷子。正是在巷子顶上，比尔·汤姆森瞧见普拉特同帕拉怀特过了马路，进了比尔嘴里的那片老采石场地界。”

===Original===
“Can we go into that?” asked Byner.

===Chinese===
“咱们能进那片地界去瞧瞧吗？”拜纳问。

===Original===
“Nowt easier!” said Pickard. “It’s a sort of open space where t’ childer goes and plays about: they hev’n’t worked no stone theer for many a long year﻿—all t’ stone’s exhausted, like.”

===Chinese===
“再容易没有了！”皮卡德说。“那是片空场子，娃娃们都上那儿撒欢玩耍：多少年来那儿就没采过石头——石料，您晓得，早掏尽了。”

===Original===
He led Byner along the lane to its further end, pointed out the place where Thomson said he had seen Pratt and Parrawhite, and indicated the terrace of houses in which Pratt lived. Then he crossed towards the old quarries.

===Chinese===
他领着拜纳沿巷子走到巷顶头，指明汤姆森说自己瞧见普拉特和帕拉怀特的那处地方，又指了指普拉特住的那排房子。随后他朝老采石坑那边走去。

===Original===
“Don’t know what they should want to come in here for﻿—unless it wor to talk very confidential,” said Pickard. “But lor bless yer!﻿—it ’ud be quiet enough anywheer about this neighbourhood at that time o’ neet. However, this is wheer Bill Thomson says he see’d ’em come.”

===Chinese===
“俺闹不明白他们钻到这儿来要干啥——除非是要说顶机密的体己话，”皮卡德说。“嗐，老天爷！——深更半夜，这一带随便哪处都够清静的。不管怎么说，比尔·汤姆森说瞧见他们进来的，就是这儿。”

===Original===
He led the way amongst the disused quarries, and Byner, following, climbed on a mound, now grown over with grass and weed, and looked about him. To his town eyes the place was something novel. He had never seen the like of it before. Gradually he began to understand it. The stone had been torn out of the earth, sometimes in square pits, sometimes in semicircular ones, until the various veins and strata had become exhausted. Then, when men went away, Nature had stepped in to assert her rights. All over the despoiled region she had spread a new clothing of green. Turf had grown on the flooring of the quarries; ivy and bramble had covered the deep scars; bushes had sprung up; trees were already springing. And in one of the worn-out excavations some man had planted a kitchen-garden in orderly and formal rows and plots.

===Chinese===
他在一片废弃的采石坑间领头走着，拜纳跟在后头，爬上一座如今已长满荒草野藤的土丘，四下里张望。在他这双城里人的眼睛看来，这地方着实新奇，他从没见过这般景象。渐渐地，他咂摸出它的门道来。石头是从地里生生掏出来的，有时掏成方坑，有时掏成半圆的坑，直到一道道石脉、一层层岩层都掏尽了。人一走开，大自然便站出来主张她的权利了。在这片被劫掠过的地界上，她铺开了一身新绿：坑底生了草皮；常春藤和荆棘盖住了深深的疮疤；灌木丛生了出来；树木也正一棵棵冒头。在其中一处采尽的坑里，还有人开出一方菜园，一畦畦、一块块，整整齐齐，有模有样。

===Original===
“Dangerous place that there!” said Pickard suddenly. “If I’d known o’ that, I shouldn’t ha’ let my young ’uns come to play about here. They might be tummlin’ in and drownin’ theirsens! I mun tell my missis to keep ’em away!”

===Chinese===
“那地方可危险着呢！”皮卡德猛地说道。“俺要是早知道有这号事，说什么也不叫俺那几个小的上这儿来玩耍了。保不齐一个跟头栽进去，把自个儿淹了！俺得跟俺家里的说，叫她把他们看住！”

===Original===
Byner turned﻿—to find the landlord pointing at the old shaft which had gradually become filled with water. In the morning sunlight its surface glittered like a plane of burnished metal, but when the two men went nearer and gazed at it from its edge, the water was black and unfathomable to the eye.

===Chinese===
拜纳转过身——只见店主正指着那口年深日久、渐渐积满了水的老竖井。晨光里，那水面熠熠生辉，像一方磨亮的金属；可等两人走近，从井沿上俯身细看，那水却黑黢黢的，望不见底。

===Original===
“Goodish thirty feet o’ water in that there!” surmised Pickard. “It’s none safe for childer to play about﻿—theer’s nowt to protect ’em. Next time I see Mestur Shepherd I shall mak’ it my business to tell him so; he owt either to drain that watter off or put a fence around it.”

===Chinese===
“那里头少说也有三十英尺深的水哩！”皮卡德估摸道。“娃娃们在旁边玩耍，可不是闹着玩的——啥拦护的都没有。下回俺见着谢泼德先生（Mr. Shepherd），这事俺非得跟他说不可；他要么该把水放干，要么该围上一道栅栏。”

===Original===
“Is Mr. Shepherd the property-owner?” asked Byner.

===Chinese===
“这位谢泼德先生是产业的主人？”拜纳问。

===Original===
“Aye!﻿—it’s all his, this land,” answered Pickard. He pointed to a low-roofed house set amidst elms and chestnuts, some distance off across the moor. “Lives theer, does Mestur Shepherd﻿—varry well-to-do man, he is.”

===Chinese===
“可不是！——这片地全是他的，”皮卡德答道。他指了指隔着荒原不远、掩在榆树和栗树中间的一所矮檐宅子。“就住那儿，谢泼德先生——是个家道顶殷实的人。”

===Original===
“How could that water be drained off?” asked Byner with assumed carelessness.

===Chinese===
“那水怎么个放法呢？”拜纳装出随口一问的样子。

===Original===
“Easy enough!” replied Pickard. “Cut through yon ledge, and let it run into t’ far quarry there. A couple o’ men ’ud do that job in a day.”

===Chinese===
“容易得很！”皮卡德答道。“把那边那道石坎凿开，叫水流进远处那个坑里去。两个人干这活计，一天也就成了。”

===Original===
Byner made no further remark. He and Pickard strolled back to the Green Man together. And declining the landlord’s invitation to step inside and take another glass, but promising to see him again very soon, the inquiry agent walked on to the tramcar and rode down to Barford to keep his appointment with Eldrick and Collingwood at the barrister’s chambers.

===Chinese===
拜纳没再言语。他同皮卡德一道遛遛达达回了‘绿人’酒馆。店主请他进屋再喝上一杯，他婉谢了，只答应很快就再来看他；随后这位私家侦探便步行到电车站，乘车下到巴福德，赴那位出庭律师的事务室里同埃尔德里克、科林伍德的约去了。

## XXI. The Direct Charge / 二十一 直接指控

===Original===
While Byner was pursuing his investigations in the neighbourhood of the Green Man, Collingwood was out at Normandale Grange, discussing certain matters with Nesta Mallathorpe. He had not only thought long and deeply over his conversation with Cobcroft the previous evening, but had begun to think about the crucial point of the clerk’s story as soon as he spoke in the morning, and the result of his meditations was that he rose early, intercepted Cobcroft before he started for Mallathorpe’s Mill and asked his permission to retell the story to Miss Mallathorpe. Cobcroft raised no objection, and when Collingwood had been to his chambers and seen his letters, he chartered a car and rode out to Normandale where he told Nesta of what he had learned and of his own conclusions. And Nesta, having listened carefully to all he had to tell, put a direct question to him.

===Chinese===
拜纳在‘绿人’酒馆左近一路查访的当口，科林伍德则出了城，在诺曼代尔庄园同妮斯塔·马拉索普商谈几桩事情。头天傍晚同科布克罗夫特的那番谈话，他不但想了很久、想得很深；而且那位职员第二天清早刚一开口，他就开始琢磨起这故事的关键一节来了。反复思量的结果是：他一早起身，赶在科布克罗夫特动身去马拉索普工厂之前把他截住，求他应允，把这段故事转述给马拉索普小姐听。科布克罗夫特没有异议。科林伍德先去自己的事务室看过信件，便雇了一辆汽车，乘车出城到了诺曼代尔，把自己探听得来的事、连同自家的结论，一五一十讲给妮斯塔听。妮斯塔仔仔细细听罢他要讲的一切，向他提了一个直截了当的问题。

===Original===
“You think this document which Pratt told me he holds is my late uncle’s will?” she said. “What do you suppose its terms to be?”

===Chinese===
“你认为，普拉特对我说他攥在手里的那份文书，是我已故叔父的遗嘱？”她说。“你估摸那遗嘱的条款会是怎么样的？”

===Original===
“Frankly﻿—these, or something like these,” replied Collingwood. “And I get at my conclusions in this way. Your uncle died intestate﻿—consequently, everything in the shape of real estate came to your brother and everything in personal property to your brother and yourself. Now, supposing that the document which Pratt boasts of holding is the will, one fact is very certain﻿—the property, real or personal, is not disposed of in the way in which it became disposed of because of John Mallathorpe’s intestacy. He probably disposed of it in quite another fashion. Why do I think that? Because the probability is that Pratt said to your mother, ‘I have got John Mallathorpe’s will! It doesn’t leave his property to your son and daughter. Therefore, I have all of you at my mercy. Make it worth my while, or I will bring the will forward.’ Do you see that situation?”

===Chinese===
“坦白说——就是这么些条款，或者同这些差不离的条款，”科林伍德答道。“我的结论是这么推来的。你叔父死时未立遗嘱——于是，凡属不动产，尽归你哥哥；凡属动产，则由你哥哥同你两人分得。现在，姑且假定普拉特夸口握有的那份文书就是遗嘱，有一件事实是笃定无疑的——那笔产业，无论不动产还是动产，都不会照着它因约翰·马拉索普未立遗嘱而落定的那个样子去处分。他多半是照截然不同的另一种方式处分的。我为什么这么想？因为多半是普拉特对你母亲这样说过：‘约翰·马拉索普的遗嘱在我手里！遗嘱并没有把他的产业留给你的儿女。所以，你们一家老小全攥在我掌心里。出够价钱买我这一场，不然我就把遗嘱亮出来。’这个局面，你看明白了么？”

===Original===
“Then,” replied Nesta, after a moment’s reflection, “you do think that my mother was very anxious to get that document﻿—a will﻿—from Pratt?”

===Chinese===
“那么，”妮斯塔沉吟了片刻，答道，“你当真认为，我母亲急着想从普拉特手里弄到那份文书——一份遗嘱？”

===Original===
Collingwood knew what she was thinking of﻿—her mind was still uneasy about Pratt’s account of the affair of the footbridge. But﻿—the matter had to be faced.

===Chinese===
科林伍德知道她在想什么——普拉特对便桥那桩事的讲法，仍叫她心下难安。可是——这件事总得摊开来面对。

===Original===
“I think your mother would naturally be very anxious to secure such a document,” he said. “You must remember that according to Pratt’s story to you, she tried to buy it from him﻿—just as you did yourself, though you, of course, had no idea of what it was you wanted to buy.”

===Chinese===
“我想，你母亲自然而然会巴不得把这样一份文书攥到自己手里，”他说。“你得记住，照普拉特对你讲的那套，她试过出钱向他买——正像你后来自己也那样，不过你当时当然不知道，你要买的究竟是个什么东西。”

===Original===
“What I wanted to buy,” she answered readily, “was necessity from further interference! But﻿—is there no way of compelling Pratt to give up that document﻿—whatever it is? Can’t he be made to give it up?”

===Chinese===
“我要买的，”她爽快地答道，“是从此免受搅扰！可是——就没有法子逼普拉特交出那份文书么——不管它是什么？就不能叫他非交出来不可么？”

===Original===
“A way is may be being made, just now﻿—through another affair,” replied Collingwood. “At present matters are vague. One couldn’t go to Pratt and demand something at which one is, after all, only guessing. Your mother, of course, would deny that she knows what it is that Pratt holds. But﻿—there is the possibility of the duplicate to which Cobcroft referred. Now, I want to put the question straight to you﻿—supposing that duplicate will can be found﻿—and supposing﻿—to put it plainly﻿—its terms dispossess you of all your considerable property﻿—what then?”

===Chinese===
“眼下一条路子，兴许正借着另一桩事铺开来，”科林伍德答道。“目前种种还都悬乎。总不能寻到普拉特跟前，伸手去讨一件咱们毕竟只是瞎猜的东西。你母亲当然会一口否认，说不知道普拉特手里攥的是什么。可是——还有科布克罗夫特提到的那份副本的可能。现在，我要把问题直截了当摆到你面前——倘若那份遗嘱副本寻得出来——又倘若——把话挑明了——它的条款把你名下这笔可观的家业剥夺得一干二净——那时你怎么办？”

===Original===
“Do you want the exact truth?” she asked. “Well, then, I should just welcome anything that cleared up all this mystery! What is it at present, this situation, but intolerable? I know that my mother is in Pratt’s power, and likely to remain so as long as ever this goes on﻿—probably for life. She will not give me her confidence. What is more, I am certain that she is giving it to Esther Mawson﻿—who is most likely hand-in-glove with Pratt. Esther Mawson is always with her. I am almost sure that she communicates with Pratt through Esther Mawson. It is all what I say﻿—intolerable! I had rather lose every penny that has come into my hands than have this go on.”

===Chinese===
“你要听一字不差的实话么？”她问。“好吧，那我说：凡是能把这一切谜团澄清的事，我都巴不得它来！眼下这算什么处境？除了‘忍无可忍’，还有什么可说的？我知道我母亲攥在普拉特手心里，只要这局面拖下去，她就要一直被攥着——多半要被攥一辈子。她不肯对我说心里话。不但如此，我还断定她把心里话都给了埃丝特·莫森——而那女人十有八九同普拉特串通一气。埃丝特·莫森成天守着她。我几乎可以断定，她就是借着埃丝特·莫森同普拉特传递消息。这一切正如我说的——忍无可忍！宁可把落进我手里的每一个便士都丢了，也不要这局面再拖下去。”

===Original===
“Answer me a plain question,” said Collingwood. “Is your mother fond of money, position﻿—all that sort of thing?”

===Chinese===
“回答我一个直白的问题，”科林伍德说。“你母亲贪恋钱财么？贪恋地位么——诸如此类的东西？”

===Original===
“She is fond of power!” replied Nesta. “It pleased her greatly when we came into all this wealth to know that she was the virtual administrator. Even if she could only do it by collusion with Pratt, she would make a fight for all that she﻿—and I﻿—hold. It’s useless to deny that. Don’t forget,” she added, looking appealingly at Collingwood, “don’t forget that she has known what it was to be poor﻿—and if one does come into money﻿—I suppose one doesn’t want to lose it again.”

===Chinese===
“她贪恋的是权柄！”妮斯塔答道。“承继这全部家业的时候，知道自家成了实际上的主事人，她心里不知多受用。哪怕要保住这些，只有靠同普拉特勾结一途，她也要为她所握的——也是我所握的——一切死争到底。这一点，否认没有用。别忘了，”她望着科林伍德，眼神里带着恳求，添了一句，“别忘了她是尝过穷苦滋味的人——人一旦进了钱——我想，就再也不肯撒手了。”

===Original===
“Oh, it’s natural enough!” agreed Collingwood. “But﻿—if things are as I think, Pratt would be an incubus, a millstone, forever. Anyway, I came out to tell you what I’ve learned, and what I have an idea may be the truth, and above all, to get your definite opinion. You want the Pratt influence out of the way﻿—at any cost?”

===Chinese===
“哦，这再自然不过！”科林伍德附和道。“可是——倘若事情真如我所料，普拉特就是一场驱不散的梦魇、一副卸不下的磨盘，永永远远。且不说这个。我这趟出城，一为告诉你我探听到的事，二为告诉你我揣度或许是实情的东西，而顶要紧的，是要讨你一句明确的意见。你要普拉特这股势力从根拔除——不惜任何代价？”

===Original===
“At any cost!” she affirmed. “Even if I have to go back to earning my own living! Whatever pleasure in life could there be for me, knowing that at the back of all this there is that﻿—what?”

===Chinese===
“不惜任何代价！”她斩钉截铁。“哪怕从此回头，再去自己挣钱谋生！明知这一切背后蹲着那么个——什么呢？——人生还有什么乐趣可言？”

===Original===
“Pratt!” answered Collingwood. “Pratt! He’s the shadow﻿—with his deep schemes. However, as I said﻿—there may be﻿—developing at this moment﻿—another way of getting at Pratt. Gentlemen like Pratt, born schemers, invariably forget one very important factor in life﻿—the unexpected! Even the cleverest and most subtle schemer may have his delicate machinery broken to pieces by a chance bit of mere dust getting into it at an unexpected turn of the wheels. And to turn to plainer language﻿—I’m going back to Barford now to hear what another man has to say concerning certain of Pratt’s recent movements.”

===Chinese===
“普拉特！”科林伍德答道。“普拉特！他就是那道阴影子——揣着满腹的深谋密算。不过，方才说了——此刻兴许正有——另一条够得着普拉特的路子——在成形。普拉特这一流人物，天生的算计家，一辈子总免不了忘掉人生里一样顶要紧的因素——意外！哪怕再精明、再细密的算计家，他那套精巧的机器，也可能叫一粒再偶然不过的灰尘，趁着轮子一个想不到的转弯钻了进去，砸个粉碎。换句浅白话说——我这就回巴福德去，听听另一人就普拉特近日的几桩行踪有什么话要讲。”

===Original===
Eldrick was already waiting when Collingwood reached his chambers: Byner came there a few moments later. Within half an hour the barrister had told his story of Cobcroft, and the inquiry agent his of his visit to the Green Man and the quarries. And the solicitor listened quietly and attentively to both, and in the end turned to Collingwood.

===Chinese===
科林伍德回到事务室时，埃尔德里克已在候着；不多几分钟，拜纳也到了。不到半个钟头，出庭律师讲完了科布克罗夫特那段事，征信代理人也讲完了走访‘绿人’酒馆和采石场的经过。这位事务律师静静细细听罢两人所讲，末了转向科林伍德。

===Original===
“I’ll withdraw my opinion about the nature of the document which Pratt got hold of,” he said. “What he’s got is what you think﻿—John Mallathorpe’s will!”

===Chinese===
“普拉特弄到手的那份文书性质如何，我先前的意见，我收回，”他说。“他拿到手的，正跟你想的一样——是约翰·马拉索普的遗嘱！”

===Original===
“If I may venture an opinion,” remarked Byner, “that’s dead certain!”

===Chinese===
“容我冒昧说一句，”拜纳道，“这是板上钉钉的了！”

===Original===
“And now,” continued Eldrick, “we’re faced with a nice situation! Don’t either of you forget this fact. Not out of willingness on her part, but because she’s got to do it, Mrs. Mallathorpe and Pratt are partners in that affair. He’s got the will﻿—but she knows its contents. She’ll pay any price to Pratt to keep them from ever becoming known or operative. But, as I say, don’t you forget something!”

===Chinese===
“而眼下，”埃尔德里克接着说，“咱们遇上的局面可顶微妙！你们二位谁也别忘了这一条。在这桩事里，马拉索普太太同普拉特是拴在一根绳上的伙伴——不是她心甘情愿，是她不得不搭。遗嘱在他手里——内容她却知道。要叫这些内容永不见天日、永不生效，多高的价钱她都肯付给普拉特。可是——听我说——还有一样东西，你们别忘了！”

===Original===
“What?” asked Collingwood.

===Chinese===
“什么？”科林伍德问。

===Original===
Eldrick tapped the edge of the table, emphasizing his words as he spoke them.

===Chinese===
埃尔德里克叩着桌沿，一面说，一面给字句敲上分量。

===Original===
“They can destroy that will whenever they like!” he said. “And once destroyed, nothing can absolutely prove that it ever existed!”

===Chinese===
“他们两个，想毁掉那份遗嘱，随时都毁得成！”他说。“而一旦毁掉，天底下就再没有任何东西能确凿证明它存在过！”

===Original===
“The duplicate?” suggested Collingwood.

===Chinese===
“那份副本呢？”科林伍德提示道。

===Original===
“Nothing to give us the faintest idea as to its existence!” said Eldrick.

===Chinese===
“连一丝一毫能叫人想到它存在的影踪都没有！”埃尔德里克说。

===Original===
“We might advertise,” said Collingwood.

===Chinese===
“咱们可以登广告找，”科林伍德说。

===Original===
“Lots of advertising was done when John Mallathorpe died,” replied the solicitor. “No!﻿—if any person had had it in possession, it would have turned up then. It may be﻿—probably is﻿—possibly must be﻿—somewhere﻿—and may yet come to light. But﻿—there’s another way of getting at Pratt. Through this Parrawhite affair. Pratt most likely had not the least notion that he would ever hear of Parrawhite again. He is going to hear of Parrawhite again! I am convinced now that Parrawhite knew something about this, and that Pratt squared him and got him away. Aren’t you?” he asked, turning to Byner.

===Chinese===
“约翰·马拉索普去世那阵子，广告登得还少么，”律师答道。“不成的！——那文书当年若真在什么人手里，那时节早该冒出来了。它兴许在——多半在——简直一定在——什么地方——日后兴许还会重见天日。可是——够得着普拉特，还有另一条路子：走帕拉怀特这桩事。普拉特多半做梦也没想到，此生还会再听见帕拉怀特的消息。可他这就再要听见了！如今我确信，帕拉怀特当年晓得这桩事的内情，是普拉特堵了他的嘴、把他打发走的。你说是不是？”他转向拜纳问道。

===Original===
But Byner smiled quietly and shook his head.

===Chinese===
拜纳却只淡淡一笑，摇了摇头。

===Original===
“No!” he answered. “I am not, Mr. Eldrick.”

===Chinese===
“不！”他答道。“我不这么看，埃尔德里克先生。”

===Original===
“You’re not?” exclaimed Eldrick, surprised and wondering that anybody could fail to agree with him.

===Chinese===
“你不这么看？”埃尔德里克失声叫道，又惊又怪，居然有人会不同意他的见解。

===Original===
“Why not, then?”

===Chinese===
“那么，为什么？”

===Original===
“Because,” replied Byner. “I am certain that Pratt murdered Parrawhite on the night of November twenty-third last. That’s why. He didn’t square him. He didn’t get him away. He killed him!”

===Chinese===
“因为，”拜纳答道，“我确信，普拉特在去年11月23日夜里杀害了帕拉怀特。就为这个。他没有堵嘴收买，也没有打发远走。他是杀了他！”

===Original===
The effect of this straightforward pronouncement of opinion on the two men who heard it was strikingly different. Collingwood’s face at once became cold and inscrutable; his lips fixed themselves sternly; his eyes looked hard into a problematic future. But Eldrick flushed as if a direct accusation had been levelled at himself, and he turned on the inquiry agent almost impatiently.

===Chinese===
这番直来直去的论断落在两个听的人身上，反应截然不同。科林伍德的脸色霎时变得冰冷、深不可测；双唇紧紧抿住；一双眼睛冷冷地盯住吉凶未卜的前路。埃尔德里克却涨红了脸，仿佛当头挨了一箭指着自家的控词，几乎按捺不住地转过身，冲着征信代理人。

===Original===
“Murder!” he exclaimed. “Oh, come! I﻿—really, that’s rather a stiff order! I dare say Pratt’s been up to all sorts of trickery, and even deviltry﻿—but murder is quite another thing. You’re pretty ready to accuse him!”

===Chinese===
“谋杀！”他嚷道。“嗐，得啦！我——说真的，这话可未免下得太重了！普拉特耍尽种种花招、甚至干尽种种狠事，这我都信得过——可谋杀完完全全是另一码事。你这罪名，扣得倒痛快！”

===Original===
Byner moved his head in Collingwood’s direction﻿—and Eldrick turned and looked anxiously at Collingwood, who, finding the eyes of both men on him, opened his hitherto tight-shut lips.

===Chinese===
拜纳把头朝科林伍德那边一偏——埃尔德里克便转过脸去，忧心忡忡地望着科林伍德；科林伍德发觉两人的眼睛都落在自己身上，这才启开一直紧闭着的双唇。

===Original===
“I think it quite likely!” he said.

===Chinese===
“我看十有八九是这么回事！”他说。

===Original===
Byner laughed softly and looked at the solicitor.

===Chinese===
拜纳轻轻一笑，望着律师。

===Original===
“Just listen to me a minute or two, Mr. Eldrick,” he said. “I’ll sum up my own ideas on this matter, got from the various details that have been supplied to me since I came to Barford. Just consider my points one by one. Let’s take them separately﻿—and see how they fit in.

===Chinese===
“请听我说一两分钟，埃尔德里克先生，”他说。“我把自来到巴福德以后、从人家供给我的种种细节里得来的看法，在这儿归拢归拢。请把我的论点一条一条掂量。咱们一条一条拆开看——瞧瞧它们怎样环环相扣。

===Original===
- Mr. Bartle is seen by his shop-boy to take a certain paper from a book which came from the late John Mallathorpe’s office at Mallathorpe Mill. He puts that paper in his pocket.

- Immediately afterwards Mr. Bartle goes to your office. Nobody is there but Pratt﻿—as far as Pratt knows.

- Bartle dies suddenly﻿—after telling Pratt that the paper is John Mallathorpe’s will. Pratt steals the will. And the probability is that Parrawhite, unknown to Pratt, was in that office, and saw him steal it. Why is that probable? Because﻿—

- Next night Parrawhite, who is being pressed for money by Pickard, tells Pickard that he can get it out of Pratt, over whom he has a hold. What hold? We can imagine what hold. Anyway﻿—

- Parrawhite leaves Pickard to meet Pratt. He did meet Pratt﻿—in Stubbs’ Lane. He was seen to go with Pratt into the disused quarry. And there, in my opinion, Pratt killed him﻿—and disposed of his body.

- What does Pratt do next? He goes to your office first thing next morning, and removes certain moneys which you say you carelessly left in your desk the night before, and tears out certain cheque forms from your book. When Parrawhite never turns up that morning, you﻿—and Pratt﻿—conclude that he’s the thief, and that he’s run away.

- If you want some proof of the correctness of this last suggestion, you’ll find it in the fact that no use has ever been made of those blank cheques, and that﻿—in all probability﻿—the stolen banknotes have never reached the Bank of England. On that last point I’m making inquiry﻿—but my feeling is that Pratt destroyed both cheques and banknotes when he stole them.

- This man Parrawhite out of the way, Pratt has a clear field. He’s got the will. He’s already acquainted Mrs. Mallathorpe with that fact, and with the terms of the will﻿—whatever they may be. We may be sure, however, that they are of such a nature as to make her willing to agree to his demands upon her﻿—and, accidentally, to go to any lengths﻿—upon which we needn’t touch, at present﻿—towards getting possession of the will from him.

- And the present situation﻿—from Pratt’s standpoint of yesterday﻿—is this. He’s so sure of his own safety that he doesn’t mind revealing to the daughter that the mother’s in his power. Why? Because Pratt, like most men of his sort, cannot believe that self-interest isn’t paramount with everybody﻿—it’s beyond him to conceive it possible that Miss Mallathorpe would do anything that might lose her several thousands a year. He argued﻿—‘So long as I hold that will, nobody and nothing can make me give it up nor divulge its contents. But I can bind one person who benefits by it﻿—Miss Mallathorpe, and for the mother’s sake I can keep the daughter quiet!’ Well﻿—he hasn’t kept the daughter quiet! She﻿—spoke!

- And last﻿—in all such schemes as Pratt’s, the schemer invariably forgets something. Pratt forgot that there might arise what actually has arisen﻿—inquiry for Parrawhite. The search for Parrawhite is afoot﻿—and if you want to get at Pratt, it will have to be through what I firmly believe to be a fact﻿—his murder of Parrawhite and his disposal of Parrawhite’s body.”

===Chinese===
- 巴特尔先生被他的小店伙瞧见，从一本书里取出一张纸——那本书出自已故约翰·马拉索普设在马拉索普工厂的办公室。他把那张纸放进了衣袋。

- 紧跟着，巴特尔先生就上了贵所。屋里除普拉特外空无一人——至少，照普拉特所知是如此。

- 巴特尔猝然死去——死前已告诉普拉特，那张纸是约翰·马拉索普的遗嘱。普拉特偷了那份遗嘱。而多半是：帕拉怀特当时正在那间事务所里，普拉特并不知情，亲眼瞧见了他行窃。凭什么说多半如此？因为——

- 第二天夜里，正被皮卡德逼着讨钱的帕拉怀特对皮卡德说，他能从普拉特身上把钱抠出来，因为他攥着普拉特的把柄。什么把柄？咱们想得出是什么样的把柄。总之——

- 帕拉怀特撇下皮卡德，去会普拉特。他确实会着了普拉特——在斯塔布斯巷。有人瞧见他与普拉特一同进了那座废弃的采石场。而依我看，正是在那儿，普拉特杀了他——并且处理了他的尸首。

- 接下来普拉特干了什么？第二天一早，他头一件事就是到贵所去，取走了一笔款子——就是您说过、前一晚不小心撂在书桌里的那笔——又从您的支票簿上撕下几张支票。那天早上帕拉怀特始终没有露面，您——同普拉特——便断定小偷就是他，断定人已经卷逃了。

- 倘若您想要点证据，证明这末一条推断不差，证据就在这里：那些空白支票从来不曾被动用过；而且——十有八九——偷去的钞票从来不曾有一张回到过英格兰银行。这后一点我正在查——不过我的感觉是，普拉特偷到手的当时，就把支票连同钞票一并毁掉了。

- 帕拉怀特这一除掉，普拉特面前便是一片坦途。遗嘱在他手里。他已经把这一层告知了马拉索普太太，也把遗嘱的条款告知了她——不管条款究竟如何。不过咱们可以断定：那些条款的性质，足以叫她甘愿应承他向她提出的要求——而且，附带地，甘愿不择手段——这一层咱们眼下不必深究——去把那份遗嘱从他手里弄过来。

- 而眼下的局面——按普拉特截至昨日的盘算——是这样。他对自身的安全把握十足，以至于向做女儿的挑明母亲攥在他手心，也满不在乎。为什么？因为普拉特同他那一流的大多数人一样，不信天下竟有人不把私利顶在头上——马拉索普小姐会做出任何有损她每年几千镑进项的事，这在他是万万设想不到的。他是这么盘算的——‘只要遗嘱在我手里，无论什么人、无论什么东西，都不能叫我交出来，也不能叫我泄露内容。可有一位从它上头得了好处的人，我能把她拴住——马拉索普小姐；看在母亲的份上，我能叫女儿守口如瓶！’好嘛——女儿可没有守口如瓶！她——开口了！

- 末了一条——普拉特这一类的全套算计里，算计家总有一样东西必定忘掉。普拉特忘掉的，就是眼下果真冒出来的东西——对帕拉怀特的查访。寻访帕拉怀特的事已经动起来了——诸位若想够得着普拉特，就非走这条路不可，走这件我坚信属实的事实——他杀害了帕拉怀特，处理了帕拉怀特的尸首。”

===Original===
“That’s all, Mr. Eldrick,” concluded Byner who had spoken with much emphasis throughout. “It all seems very clear to me, and,” he added, with a glance at Collingwood, “I think Mr. Collingwood is inclined to agree with most of what I’ve said.”

===Chinese===
“我要说的就是这些，埃尔德里克先生，”拜纳收梢道，他这一篇话自始至终字字着力。“依我看，这一切再清楚不过。而且，”他望了科林伍德一眼，补充道，“我想我说的话，科林伍德先生多半是赞成的。”

===Original===
“Pretty nearly all﻿—if not all,” assented Collingwood. “I think you’ve put into clear language precisely what I feel. I don’t believe there’s a shadow of doubt that Pratt killed Parrawhite! And we can﻿—and must﻿—get at him in that way. What do you suggest?” he continued, turning to Byner. “You have some idea, of course?”

===Chinese===
“差不多全赞成——就算不是全部，”科林伍德应道。“你把恰恰是我心里所感的，化成了明明白白的话。普拉特杀害了帕拉怀特，这一点我不信存有一丝疑影！而这一条——咱们能——也必须——打它去够他。你有什么提议？”他转向拜纳，接着说。“你自然已有打算？”

===Original===
“First of all,” answered Byner, “we mustn’t arouse any suspicion on Pratt’s part. Let us work behind the screen. But I have an idea as to how he disposed of Parrawhite, and I’m going to follow it up this very day﻿—my first duty, you know, is towards the people who want Parrawhite, or proof of his death. I propose to﻿—”

===Chinese===
“头一条，”拜纳答道，“千万别叫普拉特起半点疑心。咱们得躲在幕布后头行事。不过，他是怎样处理帕拉怀特的，我已有了一个想法，今天当天就要循着追下去——您知道，我的头一重职守，是对得起那些寻访帕拉怀特、或是要一份他死亡凭据的委托人。我打算——”

===Original===
Just then Collingwood’s clerk came in with a telegram.

===Chinese===
正这当口，科林伍德的书记员拿着一份电报走了进来。

===Original===
“Sent on from the Central Hotel, sir,” he answered. “They said Mr. Black would be found here.”

===Chinese===
“从中央旅馆转过来的，先生，”书记员答道。“他们说，要找布莱克先生，上这儿来准能寻着。”

===Original===
“That’s mine,” said the inquiry agent. “I left word at the hotel that they were to send to your chambers if any wire came for me. Allow me.” He opened the telegram, looked it over, and waiting until the clerk had gone, turned to his companions. “Here’s a message from my partner, Mr. Halstead,” he continued. “Listen to what he wires:

===Chinese===
“那是我的，”征信代理人说。“我在旅馆里留过话，若有发给我的电报，就转到您的事务室来。让我来吧。”他拆开电报，从头看了一遍，等书记员退出去之后，才转向两位同伴。“这儿是我合伙人哈尔斯特德先生发来的电文，”他接着说。“听听他电报里怎么说：

===Original===
“ ‘Wire just received from Murgatroyd, shipping agent, Peel Row, Barford. He says Parrawhite left that town for America on November 24th last and offers further information. Let me know what to reply!’ ”

===Chinese===
“‘巴福德皮尔罗的船运代理商默加特罗伊德来电刚到：据称帕拉怀特已于去年11月24日离该城赴美，另有详情可奉告。如何回复，请示下！’”

===Original===
Byner laid the message before Eldrick and Collingwood without further comment.

===Chinese===
拜纳把电文往埃尔德里克和科林伍德面前一放，再没多说一个字。

## XXII. The Cat’spaw / 二十二 猫爪子

===Original===
On the evening of the day whereon Nesta Mallathorpe had paid him the visit which had resulted in so much plain speech on both sides, Pratt employed his leisure in a calm review of the situation. He was by no means dissatisfied, it seemed to him that everything was going very well for his purposes. He was not at all sorry that Nesta had been to see him—far from it. He regretted nothing that he had said to her. In his desperate opinion, his own position was much stronger when she left him than it was when he opened his office door to her. She now knew, said Pratt, with what a strong and resourceful man she had to deal: she would respect him, and have a better idea of him, now that she was aware of his impregnable position.

===Chinese===
妮斯塔·马拉索普登门造访、弄得双方都说了许多直话的那天晚上，普拉特趁闲工夫，把眼下的局面冷冷静静检阅了一遍。他绝非不称意——在他看来，桩桩件件都在顺着他图谋的方向走得顺顺当当。妮斯塔来见过他，他一点也不懊恼——远非如此。他对她说过的话，一句也不后悔。照他那种孤注一掷的想法，她告辞而去时，他自家的地位，比他开事务所门迎她进来那会儿，稳当得多。普拉特自道：她如今算是知道了，同她打交道的，是个何等强硬、何等足智多谋的汉子；她既已晓得他这牢不可破的地位，往后必会敬他三分，把他看得更重。

===Original===
Herein Pratt’s innate vanity and his ignorance showed themselves. He had little knowledge of modern young women, and few ideas about them; and such ideas as he possessed were usually mistaken ones. But one was that it is always necessary to keep a firm hand on women—let them see and feel your power, said Pratt. He had been secretly delighted to acquaint Nesta Mallathorpe with his power, to drive it into her that he had the whip hand of her mother, and through her mother, of Nesta herself. He had seen that Nesta was much upset and alarmed by what he told her. And though she certainly seemed to recover her spirits at the end of the interview, and even refused to shake hands with him, he cherished the notion that in the war of words he had come off a decided victor. He did not believe that Nesta would utter to any other soul one word of what had passed between them: she would be too much afraid of calling down his vengeance on her mother. What he did believe was that as time went by, and all progressed smoothly, Nesta would come to face and accept facts: she would find him honest and hardworking in his dealings with Mrs. Mallathorpe (as he fully intended to be, from purely personal and selfish motives) and she herself would begin to tolerate and then to trust him, and eventually—well, who knew what might or might not happen? What said the great Talleyrand?—**With Time and Patience, the Mulberry Leaf Is Turned Into Satin**.

===Chinese===
普拉特天生的虚荣和无知，正在这里显露了出来。他对现代的年轻女子所知寥寥，见解也寥寥；而仅有的那几条见解，又多半是错的。可有一条他笃信不疑：对女人家，什么时候都得攥紧了、拿稳了——得叫她们瞧见你的权势，也叫她们觉出你的权势，普拉特是这么主张的。能让妮斯塔·马拉索普领教领教他的权势，能把这么一桩事凿进她心里——她母亲攥在他掌心，而借着她母亲，她妮斯塔本人也攥在他掌心——他暗地里不知多受用。他看得出，他讲的那番话叫妮斯塔心慌意乱、惊惧交加。虽说谈话到末了她分明像是缓回了神气，甚至不肯同他握手，他却牢牢记着一个想头：这场唇枪舌战，他是十足的赢家。他不信妮斯塔会把两人之间的来往向外人吐露半个字：她太怕了，怕招得他把报复落到她母亲头上。他信的是另一头：日子一久，事事顺遂，妮斯塔就会正视事实、认下事实：她会发觉他同马拉索普太太打交道时诚实肯干（他也铁了心要如此——纯然出于个人的、自私的打算），她自家也会渐渐容得下他，再渐渐信得过他，到末了——唉，谁知道往后会有什么、又不会有什么呢？伟大的塔列朗怎么说的？——**只要有时间和耐心，桑叶就能变成绸缎**。

===Original===
But Pratt’s self-complacency received a shock next morning. If he had been a reader of London newspapers, it would have received a shock the day before. Pratt, however, was essentially parochial in his newspaper tastes—he never read anything but the Barford papers. And when he picked up the Barford morning journal and saw Eldrick’s advertisement for Parrawhite in a prominent place, he literally started from sheer surprise—not unmingled with alarm. It was as if he were the occupant of a strong position, only fortified, who suddenly finds a shell dropped into his outworks from a totally unexpected quarter.

===Chinese===
可是第二天早上，普拉特的顾影自得挨了一记闷棍。他若是个读伦敦报纸的人，头一天就该挨上了。不过，普拉特看报的眼界，骨子里窄不出乡镇境界——除了巴福德的报纸，他一概不看。待他拿起巴福德的晨报，一眼瞧见埃尔德里克那则寻访帕拉怀特的广告登在惹眼的位置，他当真惊得一跳——纯然出于惊愕，惊愕里还掺着惊慌。这就好比一个人据守着坚固的阵地，工事又层层加固，却忽地发觉一发炮弹从全然意想不到的方位，落进了他的外围工事。

===Original===
Parrawhite! Advertised for by Eldrick! Why? For what reason? For what purpose? With what idea? Parrawhite!—of all men in the world—Parrawhite, of whom he had never wanted to hear again! And what on earth could Eldrick want with him, or with news of him? It would be—or might be—an uncommonly awkward thing for him, Pratt, if a really exhaustive search were made for Parrawhite. For nobody knew better than himself that one little thing leads to another, and—but he forbore to follow out what might have been his train of thought. Once he was tempted to make an excuse for going round to Eldrick & Pascoe’s with the idea of fishing for information—but he refrained. Let things develop—that was a safer plan. Still, he was anxious and disturbed all day. Then, towards the end of the afternoon, he bought one of the Barford evening papers—and saw, in staring letters, the advertisement which Byner had caused to be inserted only a few hours previously. And at that, Pratt became afraid.

===Chinese===
帕拉怀特！埃尔德里克登广告寻他！为什么？什么缘由？什么目的？打的什么算盘？帕拉怀特！——普天之下偏偏是这么个人！——帕拉怀特，这个名字，他巴不得从此再不听人提起！再说，埃尔德里克找他本人、或找他的消息，究竟想做什么？倘若真有人为帕拉怀特下一番掘地三尺的搜查，对他普拉特来说，那可是——或者可能是——一桩别扭透顶的事。因为天底下没有谁比他自己更清楚：一桩小事会牵出另一桩，连环相扣，于是——可他不许自家顺着这条思路想到底。有一阵子他动了念头，想寻个借口，到埃尔德里克—帕斯科那里绕一遭，钓一点消息出来——终究还是忍住了。且由着事情自个儿发展——那才是更稳妥的章程。话虽如此，他一整天心里都七上八下、忐忑难安。随后，挨到下半晌，他买了一份巴福德的晚报——赫然在目的大字里，瞧见了拜纳不过几个钟头前才授意插进去的那则广告。这一下，普拉特害怕了。

===Original===
Parrawhite wanted!—news of Parrawhite wanted!—and in two separate quarters. Wanted by Eldrick—wanted by some London people! What in the name of the devil did it mean? At any rate, he must see to himself. One thing was certain—no search for Parrawhite must be permitted in Barford.

===Chinese===
寻帕拉怀特！——寻帕拉怀特的消息！——而且两处地方一齐在寻。埃尔德里克在寻——伦敦什么人也在寻！这究竟是见的什么鬼？不管怎么说，他总得先顾住自家。有一件事是笃定的——巴福德这地方，绝不容许任何人搜寻帕拉怀特。

===Original===
That evening, instead of going home to dinner, Pratt remained in town, and dined at a quiet restaurant. When he dined, he thought, and planned, and schemed—and after treating himself very well in the matter of food and drink, he lighted a cigar, returned to his new offices, opened a safe which he had just set up, and took from a drawer in it a hundred pounds in banknotes. With these in his pocketbook he went off to a quiet part of the town—the part in which James Parrawhite had lodged during his stay in Barford.

===Chinese===
那天傍晚，普拉特没有回家用饭，留在镇上，寻了家清静的馆子吃晚饭。吃饭的当口，他一路思量、一路筹划、一路盘算——酒饭把自己款待得足足的，点上支雪茄，回到自家的新事务所，开了那口刚置办好的保险柜，从柜里的抽屉中取出一百镑钞票。他把钞票揣进皮夹，径直去了镇上一处僻静地段——詹姆斯·帕拉怀特侨居巴福德期间落脚的那一片。

===Original===
Pratt turned into a somewhat mean and shabby street—a street of small, poor-class shops. He went forward amongst them until he came to one which, if anything, was meaner and shabbier than the others and bore over its window the name Reuben Murgatroyd—Watchmaker and Jeweller. There were few signs of jewellery in Reuben Murgatroyd’s window—some cheap clocks, some foreign-made watches of the five-shilling and seven-and-six variety, a selection of flashy rings and chains were spread on the shelves, equally cheap and flashy bangles, bracelets, and brooches lay in dust-covered trays on the sloping bench beneath them. At these things Pratt cast no more than a contemptuous glance. But he looked with interest at the upper part of the window, in which were displayed numerous gaily-coloured handbills and small posters relating to shipping—chiefly in the way of assisted passages to various parts of the globe. These set out that you could get an assisted passage to Canada for so much; to Australia for not much more—and if the bills and posters themselves did not tell you all you wanted to know, certain big letters at the foot of each invited you to apply for further information to Mr. R. Murgatroyd, agent, within. And Pratt pushed open the shop-door and walked inside.

===Chinese===
普拉特拐进一条颇为寒碜破败的街——一条净是小铺子、做穷人生意的街。他在这些铺子间一路走去，直到来到一家铺子跟前——若说同别家有什么两样，那就是更寒碜、更破败——橱窗上头一行字号：鲁本·默加特罗伊德——钟表匠兼珠宝商。鲁本·默加特罗伊德的橱窗里，几乎寻不见珠宝的影子——几座廉价座钟，几只外国造的怀表，尽是五先令、七先令六便士一档的货色；一批花里胡哨的戒指和表链摊在架子上，同样廉价花哨的镯子、手镯和胸针，落满了灰，盛在架子底下斜面柜台的托盘里。这些东西，普拉特只撇着嘴扫了一眼。橱窗上半截却叫他看得来了兴致——那儿贴满了花花绿绿的传单和小海报，全是轮船航运的告白，大半是去往天下各地的资助船票一类的招徕。上头写着：去加拿大，资助船票若干；去澳大利亚，也贵不了多少——倘若这些传单海报没能把你想知道的都告诉你，每张末脚都有几个大字，请你进店向代理人鲁·默加特罗伊德先生详询详情。普拉特推开铺门，走了进去。

===Original===
An untidily dressed, careworn, anxious-looking man came forward from a parlour at the rear of his shop. At sight of Pratt—who in the course of business had once served him with a writ—his pale face flushed, and then whitened, and Pratt hastened to assure him of his peaceful errand.

===Chinese===
一个衣冠不整、满面愁苦、一脸惶急的男人，从铺子后身的客堂里迎了出来。一见普拉特——业务往来上，此人从前给他送达过一纸令状——他那张苍白的脸腾地涨红了，随即又刷地白了；普拉特赶忙宽慰他，说自己此来并无恶意。

===Original===
“All right, Mr. Murgatroyd,” he said. “Nothing to be alarmed about—I’m out of that line, now—no papers of that sort tonight. I’ve a bit of business I can put in your hands—profitable business. Look here!—have you got a quarter of an hour to spare?”

===Chinese===
“没事，默加特罗伊德先生，”他说。“没什么可慌的——那一行我已经不干了——今晚没有那类文书。我有一桩买卖可以交到你手里——赚钱的买卖。听我说！——你能不能腾出一刻钟？”

===Original===
Murgatroyd, who looked greatly relieved to find that his visitor had neither writ nor summons for him, glanced at his parlour door.

===Chinese===
默加特罗伊德见来客手里既没令状也没传票，一副如释重负的模样，朝自家客堂的门望了一眼。

===Original===
“I was just going to put the shutters up, and sit down to a bite of supper, Mr. Pratt,” he answered. “Will you come in, sir?”

===Chinese===
“我正要上门板，坐下来吃口晚饭呢，普拉特先生，”他答道。“您里边请，先生？”

===Original===
“No—you come out with me,” said Pratt. “Come round to the Coach and Horses, and have a drink and we can talk. You’ll have a better appetite for your supper when you come back,” he added, with a wink. “I’ve a profitable job for you.”

===Chinese===
“不——你跟我出来，”普拉特说。“到‘马车与马’酒馆去，喝上一杯，咱们好说话。等你回来，晚饭吃得还香些，”他挤了挤眼，又添一句。“我有一桩赚钱的差事给你。”

===Original===
“Glad to hear it, sir,” replied Murgatroyd. “I can do with aught of that sort, I assure you!” He went into the parlour, said a word or two to some person within, and came out again. “Not much business doing at present, Mr. Pratt,” he said, as he and his visitor turned into the street. “Gets slacker than ever.”

===Chinese===
“听您这么说真高兴，先生，”默加特罗伊德答道。“这号事，来多少我都接得住，我向您担保！”他进了客堂，跟里头的什么人交代了一两句，又出来了。“眼下生意不大做得起来，普拉特先生，”他和来客拐上大街时说道。“越发冷清了。”

===Original===
“Then you’ll do with a slice of good luck,” remarked Pratt. “It just happens that I can put a bit in your way.”

===Chinese===
“那你正缺着一份好运呢，”普拉特道。“赶巧，我手里就有一份，可以送到你跟前。”

===Original===
He led Murgatroyd to the end of the street, where stood a corner tavern, into a side-door of which Pratt turned as if he were well acquainted with the geography of the place. Walking down a narrow passage he conducted his companion into a small parlour, at that moment untenanted, pointed him to a seat in the corner, and rang the bell. Five minutes later, having provided Murgatroyd with rum and water and a cigar, he turned on him with a direct question.

===Chinese===
他把默加特罗伊德领到街尾，那儿立着一家把角的酒馆；普拉特拐进它一道边门，那熟门熟路的架势，仿佛这地方的地形他早摸得烂熟。顺着一条窄过道走下去，他把同伴引进一间小客厅——此时空无一人——指给他墙角一个座儿，摇了铃。五分钟后，他给默加特罗伊德张罗了掺水的朗姆酒和一支雪茄，便转过身来，单刀直入地抛出一个问题。

===Original===
“Look here!” he said in a low voice. “Would a hundred pounds be any use to you?”

===Chinese===
“听我说！”他压低嗓音道。“一百镑，对你有用没有？”

===Original===
Murgatroyd’s cheeks flushed.

===Chinese===
默加特罗伊德的两颊涨红了。

===Original===
“It ’ud be a fortune!” he answered with fervour. “A hundred pound! Lor’ bless you, Mr. Pratt, it’s many a year since I saw a hundred pound—of my own—all in one lump!”

===Chinese===
“那可就是一笔家当了！”他热切地答道。“一百镑！老天爷保佑您，普拉特先生，我自个儿的一百镑——囫囵一整笔的一百镑——我有多少年没见着喽！”

===Original===
Pratt pulled out his roll of banknotes, fluttered it in his companion’s face, laid it on the table, and set an ashtray on it.

===Chinese===
普拉特掏出那一卷钞票，在同伴脸前刷地一抖，搁在桌上，又把一只烟灰缸压了上去。

===Original===
“There’s a hundred pounds there!” he said, “It’s yours to pick up—if you’ll do a little job for me. Easy job, too!—you’ll never earn a hundred pounds so easy in your life!”

===Chinese===
“这儿是一百镑！”他说。“你只管伸手拿去——只要你替我办一桩小小的差事。还是桩省力的差事！——你这辈子再没有比这更容易挣一百镑的时候了！”

===Original===
Murgatroyd pricked up his ears. According to his ideas, money easily come by was seldom honestly earned. He stirred uncomfortably in his seat.

===Chinese===
默加特罗伊德竖起了耳朵。照他的想法，容易到手的钱，难得有几文是干净挣来的。他在座位上不安地挪动。

===Original===
“So long as it’s a straight job,” he muttered. “I don’t want—”

===Chinese===
“只要是一桩正路差事就成，”他咕哝道。“我可不想——”

===Original===
“Straight enough—as straight as it’s easy,” answered Pratt. “It may seem a bit mysterious, but there’s reasons for that. I give you my word it’s all right—all a mere bit of diplomacy—and that nobody’ll ever know you’re in it—that is, beyond a certain stage—and that there’s no danger to you.”

===Chinese===
“正路得很——有多省力就有多正路，”普拉特答道。“瞧着兴许有点神神秘秘，可这里头自有缘故。我拿我的话给你担保，包管没事——不过是耍一点儿外交手腕——保管谁也不会知道你掺和在里头——就是说，过了某一关之后——也保管你没有半点风险。”

===Original===
“What is it?” asked Murgatroyd, still uneasy and doubtful.

===Chinese===
“到底是什么差事？”默加特罗伊德问，心里还是七上八下、将信将疑。

===Original===
Pratt pulled the evening paper out of his pocket and showed Murgatroyd the advertisement signed Halstead & Byner.

===Chinese===
普拉特从口袋里掏出那份晚报，把署名哈尔斯特德—拜纳的那则广告指给默加特罗伊德看。

===Original===
“You see that?” he said. “Information wanted about Parrawhite. Do you remember Parrawhite? He once served you with some papers in that affair in which we were against you.”

===Chinese===
“瞧见没有？”他说。“寻访帕拉怀特的消息。你记得帕拉怀特这个人么？从前那桩官司，咱们是对家，他给你送达过几份文书。”

===Original===
“I remember him,” answered Murgatroyd. “I’ve seen him in here now and again. So he’s wanted, is he? I didn’t know he’d left the town.”

===Chinese===
“我记得他，”默加特罗伊德答道。“从前他也时不时到我铺子里来。这么说，有人在找他，是吧？他都离了镇，我倒不晓得。”

===Original===
“Left last November,” said Pratt. “And—there are folks—influential folks, as you can guess, seeing that they can throw a hundred pounds away!—who don’t want any inquiries made for him in Barford. They don’t mind—those folks—how many inquiries and searches are made for him anywhere else, but—not here!”

===Chinese===
“去年十一月走的，”普拉特说。“而且——有那么几位——有头有脸的人物，这你猜得着，瞧瞧，一百镑他们都撒得出去！——不愿有人在巴福德打听他。那几位倒不介意——别处随人家怎么打听、怎么搜寻都成——只是——不能在这儿！”

===Original===
“Well?” asked Murgatroyd anxiously.

===Chinese===
“怎么讲？”默加特罗伊德急切地问。

===Original===
“This is it,” replied Pratt. “You do a bit now and then as agent for some of these shipping lines. You book passages for emigrants—and for other people, going to New Zealand or Canada or Timbuktu—never mind where. Now then—couldn’t you remember—I’m sure you could—that you booked a passage for Parrawhite to America last November? Come! It’s an easy matter to remember is that—for a hundred pounds.”

===Chinese===
“是这么回事，”普拉特答道。“你时不常替这几家轮船公司做做代理。你给移民订船票——也给别样人订，去新西兰的、去加拿大的、去廷巴克图的——管它去哪儿。那么——你就想不起来——我保准你想得起来——去年十一月，你给帕拉怀特订过一张去美国的船票？来吧！这桩事好记得很——为了一百镑。”

===Original===
Murgatroyd’s thin fingers trembled a little as he picked up his glass. “What do you want me to do—exactly?” he asked.

===Chinese===
默加特罗伊德端起酒杯，瘦削的手指微微发颤。“您要我做的——一是一、二是二地说——究竟是什么？”他问。

===Original===
“This!” said Pratt. “I want you, tomorrow morning, early, to send a telegram to these people, Halstead & Byner, St. Martin’s Chambers, London, just saying that James Parrawhite left Barford for America on November 24th last, and that you can give further information if necessary.”

===Chinese===
“这个！”普拉特说。“我要你明天一大早，给这几位——哈尔斯特德—拜纳，伦敦圣马丁事务室——拍一份电报，只说：詹姆斯·帕拉怀特已于去年11月24日离开巴福德赴美，如属必要，你可提供进一步详情。”

===Original===
“And what if it is necessary?” inquired Murgatroyd.

===Chinese===
“要是人家真要详情呢？”默加特罗伊德问。

===Original===
“Then—in answer to any letter or telegram of inquiry—you’ll just say that you knew Parrawhite by sight as a clerk at Eldrick & Pascoe’s in this town, that on November 23rd he told you that he was going to emigrate to America, that next day you booked him his passage, for which he paid you whatever it was, and that he thereupon set off for Liverpool. See?”

===Chinese===
“那你就——在回复任何来信来电的查问时——只管这么说：你同帕拉怀特面熟，晓得他是本镇埃尔德里克—帕斯科事务所的书记员；11月23日那天，他对你说他打算移民美国；第二天你给他订了船票，船费若干，他如数付给了你；随后他便动身去了利物浦。明白？”

===Original===
“It’s all lies, you know,” muttered Murgatroyd.

===Chinese===
“这一套，你是知道的，句句是谎话，”默加特罗伊德咕哝道。

===Original===
“Nobody can find ’em out, anyway,” replied Pratt. “That’s the one important thing to consider. You’re safe! And if you’re cursed with a conscience and it’s tender—well, that’ll make a good plaister for it!”

===Chinese===
“反正没一个人戳穿得了，”普拉特答道。“顶要紧的，就是掂量这一条。你稳当着呢！再说，你若天生的背着个良心、那良心又娇嫩——得，这一叠正好给它贴一帖好膏药！”

===Original===
He pointed to the little wad of banknotes—and the man sitting at his side followed the pointing finger with hungry eyes. Murgatroyd wanted money badly. His business, always poor, was becoming worse: his shipping agency rarely produced any result: his rent was in arrears: he owed money to his neighbour-tradesmen: he had a wife and young children. To such a man, a hundred pounds meant relief, comfort, the lifting of pressure.

===Chinese===
他指了指那一小叠钞票——坐在旁边的那个人眼巴巴地随着他指的方向望去。默加特罗伊德太缺钱了。他那买卖，向来清淡，如今越发不成气候：船运代理难得有什么进项：房租拖欠着：街坊同行那里也欠着账：家里还有老婆和一串年幼的儿女。对这样一个人，一百镑意味着喘息、意味着安逸、意味着压在身上的重担就此卸脱。

===Original===
“You’re sure there’s naught wrong in it, Mr. Pratt,” he asked abruptly and assiduously. “It ’ud be a bad job for my family if anything happened to me, you know.”

===Chinese===
“您拿得准这里头没岔子么，普拉特先生？”他冷不丁地、又急切又执着地追问了一句。“您知道，我要是出了什么事，我一家老小可就惨了。”

===Original===
“There’s naught that will happen,” answered Pratt confidently. “Who on earth can contradict you? Who knows what people you sell passages to—but yourself?”

===Chinese===
“什么事也不会有，”普拉特满有把握地答道。“普天之下谁能驳倒你？你卖船票给些什么人——除了你自家，还有谁晓得？”

===Original===
“There’s the folks themselves,” replied Murgatroyd. “Suppose Parrawhite turns up?”

===Chinese===
“总有那些买船票的人自家，”默加特罗伊德答道。“万一帕拉怀特露面了呢？”

===Original===
“He won’t!” exclaimed Pratt.

===Chinese===
“他露不了面！”普拉特喝道。

===Original===
“You know where he is?” suggested Murgatroyd.

===Chinese===
“您知道他在哪儿？”默加特罗伊德试探道。

===Original===
“Not exactly,” said Pratt. “But—he’s left this country for another—further off than America. That’s certain! And—the folks I referred to don’t want any inquiry about him here.”

===Chinese===
“说不准，”普拉特说。“不过——他已经离了本国，去了另一国——比美国远得多的地方。这是笃定的！还有——我方才说的那几位，不愿有人在这儿打听他。”

===Original===
“If I am asked questions—later—am I to say he booked in his own name?” inquired Murgatroyd.

===Chinese===
“往后人家要是盘问我——我该不该说，他用的是自家名字订的船票？”默加特罗伊德问。

===Original===
“No—name of Parsons,” responded Pratt. “Here, I’ll write down for you exactly what I want you to say in the telegram to Halstead & Byner, and I’ll make a few memoranda for you—to post you up in case they write for further information.”

===Chinese===
“不——用的是帕森斯这名字，”普拉特应道。“来，我把要你拍给哈尔斯特德—拜纳的电文一字一句写给你，再给你写几条备忘——万一他们来信索要详情，也好叫你把话备齐。”

===Original===
“I haven’t said that I’ll do it,” remarked Murgatroyd. “I don’t like the looks of it. It’s all a pack of lies.”

===Chinese===
“我可还没说我肯干呢，”默加特罗伊德道。“我不喜欢这事的模样。通篇就是一包谎。”

===Original===
Pratt paid no heed to this moral reflection. He found some loose paper in his pocket and scribbled on it for a while. Then, as if accidentally, he moved the ashtray, and the banknotes beneath it, all new, gave forth a crisp, rustling sound.

===Chinese===
这番关于良心的议论，普拉特全不理会。他从口袋里寻出几张散纸，涂写了一阵。随后，像是无心似的，他把烟灰缸挪了挪——底下压着的那一叠钞票张张簇新，发出一声脆生生的窸窣。

===Original===
“Here you are!” said Pratt, pushing notes and memoranda towards his companion. “Take the brass, man!—you don’t get a job like that every day.”

===Chinese===
“都在这儿了！”普拉特说着，把钞票连同备忘一齐推到同伴跟前。“把铜钿收下吧，老兄！——这样的差事，可不是天天碰得上的。”

===Original===
And Murgatroyd put the money in his pocket, and presently went home, persuading himself that everything would be all right.

===Chinese===
默加特罗伊德把钱揣进口袋，不多时便回家去了，一路劝着自家：一切都会太平无事的。

## XXIII. Smooth Face and Anxious Brain / 二十三 光滑的脸与焦灼的心

===Original===
Byner watched Eldrick and Collingwood inquisitively as they bent over Halstead’s telegram. He was not surprised when Collingwood merely nodded in silence—nor when Eldrick turned excitedly in his own direction.

===Chinese===
拜纳盯着俯身细看哈尔斯特德那份电报的埃尔德里克和科林伍德，眼里满是探询。科林伍德只默默点了点头，他并不意外；埃尔德里克兴奋地转过身来朝着他，他也不意外。

===Original===
“There!—what did I tell you?” he exclaimed. “There’s been no murder! The man left the town. Probably, Pratt helped him off. Couldn’t have better proof than that wire!”

===Chinese===
“瞧！——我先前怎么说来着？”埃尔德里克嚷道。“根本就没有什么凶杀案！那人自己离了镇。多半还是普拉特送他上的路。再没有比那份电报更过硬的证据了！”

===Original===
“What do you take that wire to prove, then, Mr. Eldrick?” asked Byner.

===Chinese===
“那么，您认为那份电报证明了什么，埃尔德里克先生？”拜纳问。

===Original===
“Take it to prove!” answered Eldrick. “Why, that Parrawhite booked a passage to America with this man Murgatroyd, last November. Clear enough, that!”

===Chinese===
“证明了什么！”埃尔德里克答道。“嗐，还不就是说，去年十一月，帕拉怀特在默加特罗伊德这个家伙手里订了一张去美国的船票。再明白不过了，这不！”

===Original===
“What do you take it to prove, Mr. Collingwood?” continued the inquiry agent, as he turned to the barrister with a smile.

===Chinese===
“那您又认为它证明了什么，科林伍德先生？”征信代理人转向那位出庭律师，含笑接着问道。

===Original===
“Before I take it for anything,” replied Collingwood, “I want to know who Murgatroyd is.”

===Chinese===
“在我承认它证明任何事之前，”科林伍德答道，“我得先弄明白，默加特罗伊德是个什么人。”

===Original===
Byner looked at Eldrick and laughed.

===Chinese===
拜纳望着埃尔德里克，笑了。

===Original===
“Precisely!” he said. “Who is Murgatroyd? Perhaps Mr. Eldrick knows.”

===Chinese===
“正是！”他说。“默加特罗伊德是什么人？这一点，埃尔德里克先生兴许知道。”

===Original===
“I do just know that he’s a man who carries on a small watch and clock business in a poorish part of the town, and that he has some sort of a shipping agency,” answered Eldrick. “But—do you mean to imply that whatever message it is that he’s sent to your partner in London this morning has not been sent in good faith?”

===Chinese===
“我只晓得，他在镇上一块不大殷实的地段开着一家小小的钟表铺子，另外还做着一点船运代理的营生，”埃尔德里克答道。“不过——您莫不是想说，他今天上午发给您伦敦合伙人的那封电文，不是出于诚意发的？”

===Original===
“I don’t imply anything,” answered Byner. “All I say is—before I attach any value to his message I, like Collingwood, want to know something about the sender. He may have been put up to sending it. He may be in collusion with somebody. Now, Mr. Eldrick, you can come in here—strongly! I don’t want to be seen in this affair—yet. Will you go and see Murgatroyd? Tell him his wire to Halstead & Byner in London has been communicated to you here. Ask him for further particulars—and then drop in on me at my hotel and tell me what you’ve learnt. I’ll be found in the smoking-room there any time after two-thirty onward.”

===Chinese===
“我什么也不暗示，”拜纳答道。“我只说一句——在他的电文能作数之前，我跟科林伍德一样，得先摸一摸发报人的底细。他可能是受了人撺掇才发的。他可能同什么人串通一气。这样，埃尔德里克先生，这一关您可以出大力！我还不想在这桩事里露面——眼下还不想。您肯去见一见默加特罗伊德么？告诉他，他发给伦敦哈尔斯特德—拜纳的那份电报，已经转到您手里了。向他再讨些详情——然后到我下榻的旅馆来一趟，把您听来的讲给我。两点半往后，什么时候去，都能在旅馆的吸烟室里寻着我。”

===Original===
Eldrick’s intense curiosity in what was rapidly becoming a fascinating mystery to him, led him to accept this embassy. And a little before three o’clock he walked into the smoking-room at the Central Hotel and discovered Byner in a comfortable corner.

===Chinese===
这桩案子在埃尔德里克眼里，正迅速变成一个叫人着迷的谜团，他好奇得按捺不住，便应承下了这趟差使。三点差些不到，他走进中央旅馆的吸烟室，在一处舒舒服服的角落里寻见了拜纳。

===Original===
“I’ve seen Murgatroyd,” he whispered, as he took an adjacent chair. “Decent honest enough man—very poor, I should say. He tells a plain enough story. Parrawhite, whom he knew as one of our clerks, told him, last November 23rd—”

===Chinese===
“我见过默加特罗伊德了，”他拉过旁边一把椅子坐下，压低声音说。“人是够正派老实的——瞧着也真穷。他讲的事情倒明白。帕拉怀特——他认得，从前是我们所里的书记员——去年11月23日对他说——”

===Original===
“He was exact about dates, then, was he?” interrupted Byner.

===Chinese===
“这么说，日子他记得一清二楚，是吧？”拜纳插话道。

===Original===
“He mentioned them readily enough,” replied the solicitor. “But to go on—Parrawhite mentioned to him, November 23rd last, that he wanted to go to America at once, Murgatroyd told him about bookings. Parrawhite called very early next morning, paid for his passage under the name of Parsons, and went off—en route for Liverpool, of course. So—there you are!”

===Chinese===
“日子他张口就来，”这位事务律师答道。“接着往下讲——去年11月23日，帕拉怀特向他提起，说自己想即刻动身去美国，默加特罗伊德给他讲了订票的章程。第二天一清早，帕拉怀特就上了门，用帕森斯的名字付清了船钱，随后便动身了——照自然是取道奔利物浦去的。所以——就是这么回事！”

===Original===
“That’s all Murgatroyd could tell?” inquired Byner.

===Chinese===
“默加特罗伊德能讲的，就这些？”拜纳问。

===Original===
“That’s all he knows,” answered Eldrick.

===Chinese===
“他晓得的，就是这些，”埃尔德里克答道。

===Original===
“You say Murgatroyd knew Parrawhite as one of your clerks?” asked Byner after a moment’s thought.

===Chinese===
“您方才说，默加特罗伊德认得帕拉怀特，认得他是你们所里的书记员？”拜纳想了一会儿，问道。

===Original===
“We had some process in hand against this man last autumn,” replied Eldrick. “I dare say Parrawhite served him with papers.”

===Chinese===
“去年秋天，我们手上正有一桩官司告这个人，”埃尔德里克答道。“我看八成是帕拉怀特给他送达的文书。”

===Original===
“Would he—Murgatroyd—be likely to know Pratt?” continued Byner.

===Chinese===
“那么他——默加特罗伊德——会不会也认得普拉特？”拜纳接着问。

===Original===
“He might—in the same connection,” admitted Eldrick.

===Chinese===
“兴许认得——也是打那桩官司里认得的，”埃尔德里克承认道。

===Original===
Byner smoked in silence for a while.

===Chinese===
拜纳默不作声地抽了一阵烟。

===Original===
“Do you know what I think, Mr. Eldrick?” he said at last. “I think Pratt put up Murgatroyd to sending that telegram to us in London this morning.”

===Chinese===
“您知道我在想什么吗，埃尔德里克先生？”他终于开了口。“我看，今儿早上默加特罗伊德发去伦敦的那份电报，就是普拉特撺掇他发的。”

===Original===
“You do!” exclaimed Eldrick.

===Chinese===
“您真这么看！”埃尔德里克失声道。

===Original===
“Surely! And now,” continued the inquiry agent, “if you will, you can do more—much more—without appearing to do anything. Pratt’s office is only a few minutes away. Can you drop in there, making some excuse, and while there, mention, more or less casually, that Parrawhite, or information about him, is wanted; that you and a certain Halstead & Byner are advertising for him; that you’ve just seen Murgatroyd in respect of a communication which he wired to Halstead’s this morning, and that—most important of all—a fortune of twenty thousand pounds is awaiting Parrawhite! Don’t forget the last bit of news.”

===Chinese===
“那还用说！这下，”征信代理人接着道，“您若愿意，还能再多办一些——办成大事——却半点不露办事的痕迹。普拉特的事务所离这儿不过几分钟的路。您能不能寻个由头，到那里去一趟，趁人在那儿，像随口闲谈似的提上一句：说如今正有人寻访帕拉怀特，正寻访他的消息；说您同伦敦一家哈尔斯特德—拜纳正登着广告找他；说您刚见过默加特罗伊德，为的是他今早拍给哈尔斯特德那头的一份电文；还有——顶顶要紧的一条——一笔两万英镑的家当，正等着帕拉怀特去领！末了一条消息，千万别忘了说。”

===Original===
“Why that particularly?” asked Eldrick.

===Chinese===
“为什么单要提这一条？”埃尔德里克问。

===Original===
“Because,” answered Byner solemnly, “I want Pratt to know that the search for Parrawhite is going to be a thorough one!”

===Chinese===
“因为，”拜纳一本正经地答道，“我要叫普拉特知道，这一回找帕拉怀特，是要掘地三尺找的！”

===Original===
Eldrick went off on his second mission, promising to return in due course. Within a few minutes he was in Pratt’s office, talking over some unimportant matter of business which he had invented as he went along. It was not until he was on the point of departure that he referred to the real reason of his visit.

===Chinese===
埃尔德里克动身去办第二桩差使，答应随后就回来交差。不出几分钟，他已经坐在普拉特的事务所里，商谈着一桩无足轻重的业务——那是他一路走着现编出来的。直到就要起身告辞，他才把此行的真正来意挑明。

===Original===
“Did you notice that Parrawhite is being advertised for?” he asked, suddenly turning on his old clerk.

===Chinese===
“您留意到没有，如今正有人登广告寻访帕拉怀特？”他问，话锋猛地转向这位从前的老书记员。

===Original===
Pratt was ready for this—had been ready ever since Eldrick walked in. He affected a fine surprise.

===Chinese===
普拉特对这一着早有准备——打埃尔德里克进门那一刻起就备好了。他装出一副大为惊讶的模样。

===Original===
“Parrawhite!” he exclaimed. “Why—who’s advertising for him?”

===Chinese===
“帕拉怀特！”他失声道。“咦——是谁登广告寻他？”

===Original===
“Don’t you see the newspapers?” asked Eldrick, pointing to some which lay about the room. “It’s in there—there’s an advertisement of mine, and one of Halstead & Byner’s, of London.”

===Chinese===
“您没瞧见报纸？”埃尔德里克问，指了指屋里四处摊着的几份报纸。“就登在上头——有我登的一则，还有伦敦哈尔斯特德—拜纳登的一则。”

===Original===
Pratt picked up a Barford paper and looked at the advertisements with a clever affectation of having never seen them before.

===Chinese===
普拉特拿起一份巴福德的报纸，把那些广告细细瞧了一遍，那份乖巧的做作，仿佛生平从没见过它们似的。

===Original===
“I haven’t had much time for newspaper reading this last day or two,” he remarked. “Advertisements for him—from two quarters!”

===Chinese===
“这一两天我没什么工夫看报，”他说。“登广告寻他——还是两处一齐登！”

===Original===
“Acting together—acting together, you know!” replied Eldrick. “It’s those people who really want him—Halstead & Byner, inquiry agents, working for a firm of City solicitors. I’m only local agent—as it were.”

===Chinese===
“两家是合起来办的——合起来办的，您知道！”埃尔德里克答道。“真正要找他的是那几位——哈尔斯特德—拜纳，征信代理，替伦敦城一家律师事务所办事。我不过做个本地代理——姑且这么讲吧。”

===Original===
“Had any response, Mr. Eldrick?” asked Pratt, throwing aside the paper. “Anyone come forward?”

===Chinese===
“有回音没有，埃尔德里克先生？”普拉特问，随手把报纸撂在一边。“有人出头了么？”

===Original===
“Yes,” answered Eldrick, watching Pratt narrowly without seeming to do so. “This morning, a man named Murgatroyd, in Peel Row, who does a bit of shipping agency, wired to Halstead & Byner to say that he booked Parrawhite to New York last November. Of course, they at once communicated with me, and I’ve just been to see Murgatroyd. He’s that man—watchmaker—we had some proceedings against last year.”

===Chinese===
“有，”埃尔德里克答道，眼睛盯着普拉特瞧，瞧得极细，面上却浑似无意。“今天上午，皮尔罗有个叫默加特罗伊德的——做点船运代理营生的——给哈尔斯特德—拜纳发了电报，说他去年11月给帕拉怀特订过一张去纽约的船票。他们自然立刻知会了我，我刚从默加特罗伊德那儿回来。就是那个人——那个钟表匠——去年我们跟他打过一场官司的。”

===Original===
“Oh, that man!” said Pratt. “Thought the name was familiar. I remember him. And what does he say?”

===Chinese===
“哦，那个人！”普拉特说。“我就说这名字耳熟。我记得他。他怎么说？”

===Original===
“Just about as much as—and little more than—he said in his wire to London,” replied Eldrick. “Booked Parrawhite to America November 24th last, and believes he left for Liverpool there and then.”

===Chinese===
“说的同他发去伦敦的电报不相上下——也就多那么一丝，”埃尔德里克答道。“说去年11月24日给帕拉怀特订了去美国的船票，又说在他想来，帕拉怀特当天就动身去利物浦了。”

===Original===
“Ah!” remarked Pratt, “That explains it, then?”

===Chinese===
“啊！”普拉特道。“那这事就说得通了？”

===Original===
“Explains—what?” asked Eldrick.

===Chinese===
“说得通——什么？”埃尔德里克问。

===Original===
Pratt gave his old employer a look—confidential and significant.

===Chinese===
普拉特朝老东家递过去一个眼色——又机密、又有分量。

===Original===
“Explains why he took that money out of your desk,” he said. “You remember—forty odd pounds. He’d use some of that for his passage-money. America eh? Now—I suppose he’s vanished for good, then—it’s not very likely he’ll ever be heard of from across there.”

===Chinese===
“说得通他为什么从您书桌里拿走那笔钱，”他说。“您总记得——四十多镑。他总要挪出一部分来付船钱的。美国，嗯？这下——我看他是就此销声匿迹了——大西洋那一头再传回他的音信，怕是不大容易。”

===Original===
Eldrick laughed—meaningly, of set purpose.

===Chinese===
埃尔德里克笑了——笑得意味深长，是有心要这么笑的。

===Original===
“We don’t know that he’s gone there,” he observed. “He mightn’t get beyond Liverpool, you know. Anyhow, we’re going to make a very good search for him here in Barford, first. We’ve nothing but Murgatroyd’s word for his having set out for Liverpool.”

===Chinese===
“他去了那边没有，我们还不知道，”他说。“他兴许连利物浦都没出呢，您想。不管怎样，我们先要在巴福德本地，为他下一番好好的搜寻。说他动身去了利物浦，眼下也不过是默加特罗伊德的一面之词。”

===Original===
“What’s he wanted for?” asked Pratt as unconcernedly as possible. “Been up to something?”

===Chinese===
“找他做什么？”普拉特用尽量不当回事的口气问。“干了什么见不得人的事？”

===Original===
“No,” answered Eldrick, as he turned on his heel. “A relation has left him twenty thousand pounds. That’s what he’s wanted for—and why he must be found—or his death proved.”

===Chinese===
“不是，”埃尔德里克答道，说着转身就走。“一位亲眷给他留下了两万英镑。找他为的就是这个——所以才非要找到他不可——不然，就得证实他死了。”

===Original===
He gave Pratt another quick glance and went off—to return to the hotel and Byner, to whom he at once gave a faithful account of what had just taken place.

===Chinese===
他又向普拉特飞快地瞥了一眼，径自去了——回到旅馆见拜纳，把这边方才经过的一切，原原本本对他讲了一遍。

===Original===
“And he didn’t turn a hair,” he remarked. “Cool as a cucumber, all through! If your theory is correct, Pratt’s a cleverer hand than I ever took him for—and I’ve always said he was clever.”

===Chinese===
“他连眉毛都没动一根，”他说。“从头到尾，镇定自若！倘若您的推断不差，普拉特这把手，可比我一向估量的还高明——而我从来都说他高明。”

===Original===
“Didn’t show anything when you mentioned Murgatroyd?” asked Byner.

===Chinese===
“您提起默加特罗伊德的时候，他没露出点什么？”拜纳问。

===Original===
“Not a shred of a thing!” replied Eldrick.

===Chinese===
“一丝一毫也没有！”埃尔德里克答道。

===Original===
“Nor when you spoke of the twenty thousand pounds?”

===Chinese===
“那您说起两万英镑的时候呢？”

===Original===
“No more than what you might call polite and interested surprise!”

===Chinese===
“无非是您所谓客客气气、略带兴味的惊讶罢了！”

===Original===
Byner laughed, threw away the end of a cigar, and rose out of his lounging posture.

===Chinese===
拜纳笑了，扔掉雪茄烟蒂，从懒洋洋倚着的姿势里直起身来。

===Original===
“Now, Mr. Eldrick,” he said, leaning close to the solicitor, “between ourselves, do you know what I’m going to do—next—which means at once?”

===Chinese===
“这样，埃尔德里克先生，”他把身子凑近这位事务律师，说道，“咱们私下讲句话——您可知道我下一步要做什么？下一步，也就是说，立刻就做？”

===Original===
“No,” replied Eldrick.

===Chinese===
“不知道，”埃尔德里克答道。

===Original===
“The police!” whispered Byner. “That’s my next move. Just now! Within a few minutes. So—will you give me a couple of notes—one to the principal man here—chief constable, or police superintendent, or whatever he is; and another to the best detective there is here—in your opinion. They’ll save me a lot of trouble.”

===Chinese===
“找警察！”拜纳压低嗓音道。“这就是我的下一步棋。现在就去！几分钟之内。所以——您给我写两封引荐信——一封给本地的头号人物——警察局长也好，警务警司也好，不管他顶着什么衔；另一封给本地最好的探员——照您的眼力。有这两封信，能省我许多周折。”

===Original===
