“Of course—if you wish it,” answered Eldrick. “But you don’t mean to say you’re going to have Pratt arrested—on what you know up to now?”

===Chinese===
“当然可以——只要您用得着，”埃尔德里克答道。“不过，您总不至于说，就要凭您眼下知道的这些，叫人把普拉特逮捕起来吧？”

===Original===
“Not at all!” replied Byner. “Much too soon! All I want is—detective help of the strictly professional kind. No—we’ll give Mr. Pratt a little more rope yet—for another four-and-twenty-hours, say. But—it’ll come! Now, who is the best local detective—a quiet, steady fellow who knows how to do his work unobtrusively?”

===Chinese===
“完全没有的事！”拜纳答道。“还早得很呢！我要的只是——地地道道行家手笔的侦探帮手。不——普拉特先生那条绳，咱们再给他放一截——就再放它二十四小时吧。可是——总有收网的一天！对了，本地最好的侦探是谁——要那种安安静静、稳稳当当、办事不显山不露水的脚色？”

===Original===
“Prydale’s the man!” said Eldrick. “Detective-Sergeant Prydale—I’ve had reason to employ him, more than once. I’ll give you a note to him, and one to Superintendent Waterson.”

===Chinese===
“普赖代尔（Prydale）正是这样的人！”埃尔德里克说。“侦缉警长普赖代尔——我因公务用过他，不止一回。我给您一封给他的引荐信，再给一封给沃特森（Waterson）警司的。”

===Original===
He went over to a writing-table and scribbled a few lines on half-sheets of notepaper which he enclosed in envelopes and handed to Byner.

===Chinese===
他走到写字台前，在几张半页信笺上草草写了几行，装进信封，递给了拜纳。

===Original===
“I don’t know what line you’re taking,” he said, “nor where it’s going to end—exactly. But I do know this—Pratt never turned a hair when I let out all that to him.”

===Chinese===
“您走的是哪条路数，我不清楚，”他说，“这条路数究竟走到哪儿收场——也说不准。可有一件事我清楚——我把那一大套话都抖搂了出来，普拉特连眉毛都没动一根。”

===Original===
But if Eldrick went away from his old clerk’s fine new offices thinking that Pratt was quite unperturbed and unmoved by the news he had just acquired, he was utterly mistaken. Pratt was very much perturbed, deeply moved, not a little frightened. He had so schooled himself to keep a straight and ever blank expression of countenance in any sudden change of events that he had shown nothing to Eldrick—but he was none the less upset by the solicitor’s last announcement. Twenty thousand pounds was lying to be picked up by Parrawhite—or by Parrawhite’s next-of-kin! What an unhappy turn of fortune! For the next-of-kin would never rest until either Parrawhite came to light, or it was satisfactorily established that he was dead—and if search begun to be made in Barford, where might not that search end? Unmoved?—cool?—if Eldrick had turned back, he would have found that Pratt had suddenly given way to a fit of nerves.

===Chinese===
可是，埃尔德里克从这位老书记员气派的新事务所里走开时，若当真以为普拉特对刚刚得来的消息毫不动心、处之泰然，那他就大错特错了。普拉特心乱如麻，深受震动，还吓得不轻。他素来把自己操练出一副本领：任凭局面陡然生变，脸上始终平平整整、纹丝不露——所以在埃尔德里克面前，他什么也没显出来——可这位事务律师临别的那句通告，搅得他心神大乱。两万英镑正摆在那里，只等帕拉怀特来捡——或是等帕拉怀特的最近血亲来捡！时运来了何等倒霉的一个转弯！因为那位最近血亲是绝不肯撒手的——不把帕拉怀特寻出来，或者不确确凿凿查明他已死了，便一日不得安生——而搜寻一旦在巴福德开了头，天晓得会搜到哪里才算完？不动心？——镇定？——埃尔德里克若是折回来看一眼，就会发觉普拉特已经撑不住劲，发作起一阵心惊胆战了。

===Original===
But that soon passed, and Pratt began to think. He left his office early, and betook himself to his favourite gymnasium. Exercise did him good—he thought a lot while he was exercising. And once more, instead of going home to dinner, he dined in town, and he sat late over his dinner in a snug corner of the restaurant, and he thought and planned and schemed—and after twilight had fallen on Barford, he went out and made his way to Peel Row. He must see Murgatroyd again—at once.

===Chinese===
好在这一阵很快就过去了，普拉特开始动起脑筋。他早早离开事务所，去了自家心爱的那家体操房。运动于他有益——他一面运动，一面想了许多。他又一次没有回家用饭，在镇上吃了晚饭，在餐馆一处安适的角落里坐到很晚，思量着、筹划着、盘算着——待到暮色降满巴福德，他出了门，一路奔皮尔罗去。他必须再见默加特罗伊德一面——立刻就见。

===Original===
Halfway along Peel Row, Pratt stopped, suddenly—and with sudden fear. Out of a side street emerged a man, a quiet ordinary-looking man whom he knew very well indeed—Detective-Sergeant Prydale. He was accompanied by a smart-looking, much younger man, whom Pratt remembered to have seen in Beck Street that afternoon—a stranger to him and to Barford. And as he watched, these two covered the narrow roadway, and walked into Murgatroyd’s shop.

===Chinese===
走到皮尔罗中段，普拉特猛地站住了——猛地里生出惧意。一条横街上走出一个人来，一个神情安静、相貌寻常的男人——这个人他实在熟得很——侦缉警长普赖代尔。陪着他的是个模样精悍、年轻得多的人——普拉特想起来，当天下午在贝克街见过此人——对他、对巴福德，这都是张生面孔。他眼睁睁望着这两个人穿过窄窄的街面，走进了默加特罗伊德的铺子。

## XXIV. The Better Half / 二十四 更好的那一半

===Original===
Under the warming influence of two glasses of rum and water, and lulled by Pratt’s assurance that all would be well, Murgatroyd had carried home his hundred pounds with pretty much the same feeling which permeates a man who, having been within measurable distance of drowning, suddenly finds a substantial piece of timber drifting his way, and takes a firm grip on it. After all, a hundred pounds was a hundred pounds. He would be able to pay his rent, and his rates, and give something to the grocer and the butcher and the baker and the milkman; the children should have some much-needed new clothes and boots—when all this was done, there would be a nice balance left over. And it was Pratt’s affair, when all was said and done, and if any trouble arose, why, Pratt would have to settle it. So he ate his supper with the better appetite which Pratt had prophesied, and he slept more satisfactorily than usual, and next morning he went to the nearest telegraph office and sent off the stipulated telegram to Halstead & Byner in London, and hoped that there was the end of the matter as far as he was concerned. And then, shortly after noon, in walked Mr. Eldrick, one of the tribe which Murgatroyd dreaded, having had various dealings with solicitors, in the way of writs and summonses, and began to ask questions.

===Chinese===
两杯掺水的朗姆酒下肚，周身暖融融的，又有普拉特那番万事都会太平的保票安着心，默加特罗伊德把那一百英镑揣回了家，心里那股滋味，同溺水之人眼看就要没顶、忽然望见一截厚实木料朝自己漂来、一把死死抓住的，几乎一模一样。毕竟，一百英镑就是一百英镑。房租付得上了，捐税缴得上了，还能给杂货商、肉贩、面包师、送奶人各家还上一点；孩子们也该添置早就急需的新衣裳新靴子——这些都打发完，还能剩下挺好的一笔余头。归根结底，说来说去，这是普拉特的事；真出了乱子，嗐，也得普拉特去收拾。于是他吃晚饭时胃口果然好了些，正应了普拉特的预言；夜里也睡得比往常踏实；第二天一早，他到最近的电报局，把讲定的那份电报发往伦敦的哈尔斯特德—拜纳，只指望这事到自己这里便算到了头。谁知晌午刚过，埃尔德里克先生便踱了进来——此人正属默加特罗伊德素来怵的那一族类：他同事务律师打过各式各样的交道，领教的无非是令状、传票那一套——进门便问起话来。

===Original===
Murgatroyd emerged from that ordeal very satisfactorily. Eldrick’s questions were few, elementary, and easily answered. There were no signs of suspicion about him, and Murgatroyd breathed more freely when he was gone. It seemed to him that the solicitor’s visit would certainly wind things up—for him. Eldrick asked all that could be asked, as far as he could see, and he had replied: now, he would probably be bothered no more. His spirits had assumed quite a cheerful tone by evening—but they received a rude shock when, summoned from his little workshop to the front premises, he found himself confronting one man whom he certainly knew to be a detective, and another who might be one. Do what he would he could not conceal some agitation, and Detective-Sergeant Prydale, a shrewdly observant man, noticed it—and affected not to.

===Chinese===
这场盘问，默加特罗伊德顺顺当当地应付了过去。埃尔德里克问得少，问得浅，轻易就答上了。对方身上看不出起疑的苗头，人一走，默加特罗伊德呼吸也顺畅了几分。在他看来，这位事务律师这一趟，必定能把事情收了场——替他收场。照他瞧，埃尔德里克该问的都问到了，他也都答了：这会儿起，多半不会再有人来搅扰。到傍晚，他的心绪已经相当轻快——不料猛地挨了一记闷棍：他从小工作间被唤到前面店堂，一抬眼，面前立着两个人，一个他分明认得是侦探，另一个瞧着也像。任凭他怎么撑持，也掩不住几分慌乱；侦缉警长普赖代尔是个眼观六路的精明人，早瞧在眼里——却只装作没瞧见。

===Original===
“Evening, Mr. Murgatroyd,” he said cheerily. “We’ve come to see if you can give us a bit of information. You’ve had Mr. Eldrick, the lawyer, here today on the same business. You know—this affair of an old clerk of his—Parrawhite?”

===Chinese===
“晚上好，默加特罗伊德先生，”他兴致颇高地招呼道。“我们来瞧瞧，您能不能给我们提供一点儿情况。今天白天，律师埃尔德里克先生为同一桩事到您这儿来过。您知道的——就是他手下那位老书记员的闲事——帕拉怀特？”

===Original===
“I told Mr. Eldrick all I know,” muttered Murgatroyd.

===Chinese===
“我把知道的都对埃尔德里克先生讲了，”默加特罗伊德咕哝道。

===Original===
“Very likely,” replied Prydale, “but there’s a few questions this gentleman and myself would like to ask. Can we come in?”

===Chinese===
“多半是，”普赖代尔答道，“不过有几句问话，这位先生和我都还想问一问。能进去坐坐么？”

===Original===
Murgatroyd fetched his wife to mind the shop, and took the callers into the parlour which she had unwillingly vacated. He knew Prydale by sight and reputation; about Byner he wondered. Finally he set him down as a detective from London—and was all the more afraid of him.

===Chinese===
默加特罗伊德唤出妻子来看店，把来客让进她不情不愿腾出来的客堂。普赖代尔这个人，他见过面、也听过名声；拜纳的来头，他捉摸不透。末了他断定，这是个伦敦来的侦探——于是越发怕他。

===Original===
“What do you want to know?” he asked, when the three men were alone. “I don’t think there’s anything that I didn’t tell Mr. Eldrick.”

===Chinese===
“你们还想打听什么？”三人独处时，他开口问道。“我看没有什么事，是我没对埃尔德里克先生讲过的。”

===Original===
“Oh, there’s a great deal that Mr. Eldrick didn’t ask,” said Prydale. “Mr. Eldrick sort of just skirted round things, like. We want to know a bit more. This Parrawhite’s got to be found, d’ye see, Mr. Murgatroyd, and as you seem to be the last man who had aught to do with him in Barford, why, naturally, we come to you. Now, to start with, you say he came to you about getting a passage to America? Just so—now, when would that be?”

===Chinese===
“嗐，埃尔德里克先生没问到的地方多着呢，”普赖代尔说。“埃尔德里克先生嘛，可以说只是绕着事儿的外圈儿蹭了蹭。我们想再多知道一点儿。这个帕拉怀特，非寻着不可，您明白么，默加特罗伊德先生？而您呢，瞧着就是在巴福德城里同他打过交道的最后一个人，喏，我们自然就奔您来了。那么，先打头里说起——您说，他来寻您，为的是订一张去美国的船票？正是——那么，那是什么时候的事儿？”

===Original===
“Day before he did get it,” answered Murgatroyd, rapidly thinking over the memoranda which Pratt had jotted down for his benefit.

===Chinese===
“是他拿到票的前一天，”默加特罗伊德答道，脑子里飞快地过着普拉特为帮他把那几条要点草草记下的备忘。

===Original===
“That,” said Prydale, “would be on the 23rd?”

===Chinese===
“那么，”普赖代尔说，“那就是23号了？”

===Original===
“Yes,” replied Murgatroyd, “23rd November, of course.”

===Chinese===
“是，”默加特罗伊德答道，“当然是11月23号。”

===Original===
“What time, now, on the 23rd?” asked the detective.

===Chinese===
“那么，23号那天是几点钟？”侦探问。

===Original===
“Time?” said Murgatroyd. “Oh—in the evening.”

===Chinese===
“几点钟？”默加特罗伊德说。“哦——傍晚。”

===Original===
“Bit vague,” remarked Prydale. “What time in the evening?”

===Chinese===
“含糊了点儿，”普赖代尔说。“傍晚几点？”

===Original===
“As near as I can recollect,” replied Murgatroyd, “it ’ud be just about half-past eight. I was thinking of closing.”

===Chinese===
“凭我记得的，”默加特罗伊德答道，“那会儿总在八点半上下。我正寻思着打烊呢。”

===Original===
“Ah!” said Prydale, with a glance at Byner, who had already told him of Parrawhite’s presence at the Green Man on the other side of the town, a good two miles away, at the hour which Murgatroyd mentioned. “Ah!—he was here in your shop at half-past eight on the evening of November 23rd last? Asking about a ticket to America?”

===Chinese===
“啊！”普赖代尔说，眼风朝拜纳扫了一下——帕拉怀特当晚在镇子另一头、足足两英里外的‘绿人’酒馆露过面，这事拜纳早已告诉他了，时辰恰是默加特罗伊德所说的那一刻。“啊！——去年11月23日晚上八点半，他在您这铺子里？打听去美国的船票？”

===Original===
“New York,” muttered Murgatroyd.

===Chinese===
“纽约，”默加特罗伊德咕哝道。

===Original===
“And he came next morning and bought one?” asked the detective.

===Chinese===
“第二天一早他就来把票买了？”侦探问。

===Original===
“I told Mr. Eldrick that,” said Murgatroyd, a little sullenly.

===Chinese===
“这个我对埃尔德里克先生讲过了，”默加特罗伊德说，有点没好气。

===Original===
“How much did it cost?” inquired Byner.

===Chinese===
“票钱多少？”拜纳问。

===Original===
“Eight pound ten,” replied Murgatroyd. “Usual price.”

===Chinese===
“八英镑十先令，”默加特罗伊德答道。“平常的价钱。”

===Original===
“What did he pay for it in?” continued Prydale.

===Chinese===
“他拿什么付的钱？”普赖代尔接着问。

===Original===
“He gave me a ten-pound note and I gave him thirty shillings change,” answered Murgatroyd.

===Chinese===
“他给了我一张十英镑的钞票，我找了他三十先令，”默加特罗伊德答道。

===Original===
“Just so,” assented Prydale. “Now what line might that be by?”

===Chinese===
“正是，”普赖代尔应道。“那么，那是走的哪一家轮船公司的航线？”

===Original===
Murgatroyd was becoming uneasy under all these questions, and his uneasiness was deepened by the way in which both his visitors watched him. He was a man who would have been a bad witness in any case—nervous, ill at ease, suspicious, inclined to boggle—and in this instance he was being forced to invent answers.

===Chinese===
这一连串问题问下来，默加特罗伊德渐渐坐立不安，而两位来客盯着他瞧的那副神气，更叫他心里发毛。搁在哪种情形下，他都不是个合格的证人——心虚、局促、多疑，遇事爱支支吾吾——何况这一回，他是被人逼着现编答案。

===Original===
“It was—oh, the Royal Atlantic!” he answered at last. “I’ve an agency for them.”

===Chinese===
“那是——哦，皇家大西洋（Royal Atlantic）！”他终于答道。“我代理他们家的船票。”

===Original===
“So I noticed from the bills and placards in your window,” observed the detective. “And of course you issue these tickets on their paper—I’ve seen ’em before. You fill up particulars on a form and a counterfoil, don’t you? And you send a copy of those particulars to the Royal Atlantic offices at Liverpool?”

===Chinese===
“从您橱窗里那些招贴和广告单上，我就瞧出这一层了，”侦探说。“这种票，您当然是开在他们公司的票纸上——我从前见过。您把各项详情填在一张票据和一份存根上，是不是？再把那些详情誊一份，寄给利物浦的皇家大西洋办事处？”

===Original===
Murgatroyd nodded silently—this was much more than he bargained for, and he did not know how much further it was going. And Prydale gave him a sudden searching look.

===Chinese===
默加特罗伊德默默点头——这可比他料想的多出太多了，他也不知道还要往深里去多远。普赖代尔突然朝他投来一道锐利的目光。

===Original===
“Can you show us the counterfoil in this instance?” he asked.

===Chinese===
“这一笔的存根，能给我们看看么？”他问。

===Original===
Murgatroyd flushed. But he managed to get out a fairly quick reply. “No, I can’t,” he answered, “I sent that book back at the end of the year.”

===Chinese===
默加特罗伊德脸红了。但他还算利索地挤出了答话。“不，不能，”他答道，“那本存根簿，我年底就退回去了。”

===Original===
“Oh, well—they’ll have it at Liverpool,” observed Prydale. “We can get at it there. Of course, they’ll have your record of the entire transaction. He’d be down on their passenger list—under the name of Parsons, I think, Mr. Murgatroyd?”

===Chinese===
“哦，那——利物浦那边会有，”普赖代尔说。“我们到那儿能查着。整笔交易的手续，他们自然都留有您的底档。旅客名单上准登着他——用的名字，我想是帕森斯吧，默加特罗伊德先生？”

===Original===
“He gave me that name,” said Murgatroyd.

===Chinese===
“他报给我的就是这个名字，”默加特罗伊德说。

===Original===
Prydale gave Byner a look and both rose.

===Chinese===
普赖代尔朝拜纳递了个眼色，两人一同起身。

===Original===
“I think that’s about all,” said the detective. “Of course, our next inquiry will be at Liverpool—at the Royal Atlantic. Thank you, Mr. Murgatroyd—much obliged.”

===Chinese===
“我看差不多就这些了，”侦探说。“下一步，我们自然要去利物浦查——查皇家大西洋。谢谢您，默加特罗伊德先生——多谢多谢。”

===Original===
Before the watchmaker could collect himself sufficiently to say or ask more, Prydale and his companion had walked out of the shop and gone away. And then Murgatroyd realized that he was in for—but he did not know what he was in for. What he did know was that if Prydale went or sent over to Liverpool the whole thing would burst like a bubble. For the Royal Atlantic people would tell the detectives at once that no passenger named Parsons had sailed under their auspices on November 24th last, and that he, Murgatroyd, had been telling a pack of lies.

===Chinese===
钟表匠还没来得及定下神再多说一句、多问一句，普赖代尔和他的同伴已经出了店门走远了。到这时默加特罗伊德才醒悟，自己惹上事了——可惹上的是什么事，他自己也不知道。他只知道一件事：普赖代尔若是亲自去利物浦，或是打发人去，整个事儿就会像泡儿似的啪一声破掉。因为皇家大西洋那班人立刻就会告诉侦探们，去年11月24日，根本没有叫帕森斯的旅客乘他们公司的船出海，而他默加特罗伊德，从头到尾说的是一堆谎话。

===Original===
Mrs. Murgatroyd, a sharp-featured woman whose wits had been sharpened by a ten years’ daily acquaintance with poverty, came out of the shop into the parlour and looked searchingly at her husband.

===Chinese===
默加特罗伊德太太是个眉眼尖削的女人，十年间日日与穷苦厮磨，心思也磨得尖利。她从店堂走进客堂，细细端详着丈夫。

===Original===
“What did them fellows want?” she demanded. “I knew one of ’em—Prydale, the detective. Now what’s up, Reuben? More trouble?”

===Chinese===
“那两个家伙来干什么？”她质问道。“其中一个我认得——普赖代尔，干侦探的。说吧，又出什么事了，鲁本？又招麻烦了？”

===Original===
Murgatroyd hesitated a moment. Then he told his wife the whole story concealing nothing.

===Chinese===
默加特罗伊德迟疑了片刻。随后他把事情原原本本讲给了妻子，什么也没瞒。

===Original===
“If they go to the Royal Atlantic, it’ll all come out,” he groaned. “I couldn’t make any excuse or explanation—anyhow! What’s to be done?”

===Chinese===
“他们要是去了皇家大西洋，什么都要露馅，”他哼哼道。“我编不出任何借口，也圆不上——横竖不行！这可怎么办？”

===Original===
“You should ha’ had naught to do wi’ that Pratt!” exclaimed Mrs. Murgatroyd. “A scoundrelly fellow, to come and tempt poor folk to do his dirty work! Where’s the money?”

===Chinese===
“你就压根儿不该沾那个普拉特的边儿！”默加特罗伊德太太嚷道。“一个恶棍，跑来坑害穷人替他干脏活儿！钱呢？”

===Original===
“Locked up!” answered Murgatroyd. “I haven’t touched a penny of it. I thought I’d wait a bit and see if aught happened. But he assured me it was all right, and you know as well as I do that a hundred pound doesn’t come our way every day. We want money!”

===Chinese===
“锁起来了！”默加特罗伊德答道。“一个子儿我也没动。我原想等等瞧，看会不会出事。可他一口保我没事，再说一百英镑不是天天飘到咱们门上来的，这你跟我一样明白。咱们缺的就是钱！”

===Original===
“Not at that price!” said his wife. “You can pay too much for money, my lad! I wish you’d told me what that Pratt was after—he should have heard a bit o’ my tongue! If I’d only known—”

===Chinese===
“这种价钱的钱，不缺！”他妻子说。“为了钱，人是会出价太高的，我的小子！你要是早告诉我那普拉特安的什么心就好了——也该让他领教领教老娘嘴上的厉害！我要是早知道——”

===Original===
Just then the shop door opened, and Pratt walked in. He at once saw Murgatroyd and his wife standing between shop and parlour, and realized at a glance that his secret in this instance was his no longer.

===Chinese===
正在这时，店门开了，普拉特走了进来。他一眼看见默加特罗伊德夫妇站在店堂和客堂之间，顿时明白：这一桩里的秘密，已经不再是他一个人的了。

===Original===
“Well?” he said, walking up to the watchmaker. “You’ve had Prydale here—and you’d Eldrick this morning. Of course, you knew what to say to both?”

===Chinese===
“怎么样？”他说着，走到钟表匠跟前。“普赖代尔来过了——今儿早上埃尔德里克也来过。两个人的话，你自然都知道该怎么答了？”

===Original===
“I wish we’d never had you here last night, young man!” exclaimed Mrs. Murgatroyd fiercely. “What right have you to come here, making trouble for folk that’s got plenty already? But at any rate, ours was honest trouble. Yours is like to land my husband in dishonesty—if it hasn’t done so already! And if my husband had only spoken to me—”

===Chinese===
“昨儿晚上真不该让你进这个门，小伙子！”默加特罗伊德太太狠狠地嚷道。“你有什么权利上这儿来，给本来就麻烦缠身的人家再添乱？可好歹，咱家的麻烦是正经人家的麻烦。你这个，保准要把我男人拽进昧良心的勾当里去——只怕已经拽进去了！我男人要是早跟我说一句——”

===Original===
“Just let your husband speak a bit now,” interrupted Pratt, almost insolently. “It’s you that’s making all the trouble or noise, anyhow! There’s naught to fuss about, missis. What’s upset you, Murgatroyd?”

===Chinese===
“这会儿就让你男人自己说吧，”普拉特打断她，口气近乎放肆。“眼下这通乱子、这通吵嚷，横竖都是你闹出来的！没什么可咋呼的，太太。什么事把你搅得心神不宁，默加特罗伊德？”

===Original===
“They’re going to the Royal Atlantic people,” muttered the watchmaker. “Of course, it’ll all come out, then. They know that I never booked any Parsons—nor anybody else for that matter—last November. You should ha’ thought o’ that!”

===Chinese===
“他们要去找皇家大西洋那班人了，”钟表匠咕哝道。“到那时，当然，什么都要露出来了。他们那边清楚，去年十一月我根本没给什么帕森斯开过票——说起来，那阵子连旁的客人也没有一个。这一层你原该想到的！”

===Original===
Pratt realized that the man was right. He had never thought of that—never anticipated that inquiry would go beyond Murgatroyd. But his keen wits at once set to work.

===Chinese===
普拉特明白，这人说得对。这一点他从来没有想过——从没料到查问会查到默加特罗伊德以外去。但他那副精明的头脑立刻开动起来。

===Original===
“What’s the system?” he asked quickly. “Tell me—what’s done when you book anybody like that? Come on!—explain, quick!”

===Chinese===
“章程是怎么个走法？”他急忙问。“告诉我——给客人订那样一张票，都要经过哪些手续？快说！——讲清楚，越快越好！”

===Original===
Murgatroyd turned to a drawer and pulled out a book and some papers. “It’s simple enough,” he said. “I’ve this book of forms, d’ye see? I fill up this form—sort of ticket or pass for the passenger, and hand it to him—it’s a receipt as well, to him. Then I enter the same particulars on that counterfoil. Then I fill up one of these papers, giving just the same particulars, and post it at once to the Company with the passage money, less my commission. When one of these books is finished, I return the counterfoils to Liverpool—they check ’em. Prydale’s up to all that. He asked to see the counterfoil in this case. I had to say I hadn’t got it—I’d sent it to the Company. Of course, he’ll find out that I didn’t.”

===Chinese===
默加特罗伊德转身拉开一只抽屉，取出一本簿子和几张单子。“简单得很，”他说。“我手头有这么一本票据簿，明白么？我填这一张——就算给旅客的票、通行凭照什么的，交到他手里——在他也是一张收条。然后我把同样的详情登录在那份存根上。再填好这几张纸里的一张，详情一模一样，立刻连同船钱寄给公司——船钱里扣下我的佣金。一本簿子用完了，我就把存根退回利物浦——他们要核对的。普赖代尔对这些章程全在行。他要看这一笔的存根。我只好说我手头没有——寄给公司了。他当然会查出来，我根本没寄。”

===Original===
“Lies!” said Mrs. Murgatroyd, vindictively. “And they didn’t start wi’ us neither!”

===Chinese===
“撒谎！”默加特罗伊德太太咬牙切齿地说。“而且这谎也不是打我们家起的头！”

===Original===
“Who was that other man with Prydale?” asked Pratt.

===Chinese===
“跟普赖代尔一起的那个人是谁？”普拉特问。

===Original===
“London detective, I should say,” answered the watchmaker. “And judging by the way he watched me, a sharp ’un, too!”

===Chinese===
“我看是伦敦来的侦探，”钟表匠答道。“瞧他盯着我瞧的那副样子，还是个厉害角色哩！”

===Original===
“What impression did you get—altogether?” demanded Pratt.

===Chinese===
“总的掂量下来，你得了个什么印象？”普拉特追问。

===Original===
“Why!—that they’re going to sift this affair—whatever it is—right down to the bottom!” exclaimed Murgatroyd. “They’re either going to find Parrawhite or get to know what became of him. That’s my impression. And what am I going to do, now! This’ll lose me what bit of business I’ve done with yon shipping firm.”

===Chinese===
“嗐！——他们要把这桩事——不管它到底是什么事——一筛子筛到底！”默加特罗伊德嚷道。“他们不把帕拉怀特寻出来，就要打听出他的下落。我就是这个印象。可我如今怎么办呢！这一来，我跟那家轮船公司做下的这一点儿生意，也要给搅黄了。”

===Original===
“Nothing of the sort!” answered Pratt scornfully. “Don’t be a fool! You’re all right. You listen to me. You write—straight off—to the Royal Atlantic. Tell ’em you had some inquiry made about a man named Parsons, who booked a passage with you for New York last November. Say that on looking up your books you found that you unaccountably forgot to send them the forms for him and his passage money. Make out a form for that date, and crumple it up—as if it had been left lying in a drawer. Enclose the money in it—here, I’ll give you ten pounds to cover it,” he went on, drawing a banknote from his purse. “Get it off at once—you’ve time now—plenty—to catch the night-mail at the General. And then, d’ye see, you’re all right. It’s only a case then—as far as you’re concerned—of forgetfulness. What’s that?—we all forget something in business, now and then. They’ll overlook that—when they get the money.”

===Chinese===
“哪儿至于！”普拉特轻蔑地答道。“别犯傻！你没事的。听我说。你写信——立刻就写——给皇家大西洋。告诉他们，去年十一月里，有个名叫帕森斯的人在你这儿打过问讯，还在你手上订了一张去纽约的船票。就说你翻了翻簿子，发觉自己不知怎么竟忘了把他的票据连同船钱寄给他们。你照那个日子填一张票据出来，把它揉搓皱了——装出一副一直搁在抽屉里的样子。把钱包卷在里头——给，我给你十英镑，正好抵上，”他说着，从皮夹子里抽出一张钞票。“这就寄出去——眼下来得及——宽裕得很——赶得上邮政总局的夜班收寄。到那时，你明白么，你就没事了。往后就只是一桩——于你而言——忘性的事儿。怎么啦？——做生意的人，谁还不时忘个一星半点。他们不会计较的——钱一到手，就更不计较了。”

===Original===
“Aye, but you’re forgetting something now!” remarked Murgatroyd. “You’re forgetting this—no such passenger ever went! They’ll know that by their passenger lists.”

===Chinese===
“嗳，可这会儿你自己倒忘了一桩事！”默加特罗伊德说。“你忘了这个——压根儿就没这么个旅客上过船！他们一翻旅客名单就知道。”

===Original===
“What the devil has that to do with it?” snarled Pratt impatiently. “What the devil do we care whether any such passenger went or not? All that you’re concerned about is to prove that you issued a ticket to Parrawhite, under the name of Parsons. What’s it matter to you where Parrawhite, alias Parsons, went, when he’d once left your shop? You naturally thought he’d go straight to the Lancashire and Yorkshire Station, on his way to Liverpool and New York! But, for aught you know, he may have fallen down a drain pipe in the next street! Don’t you see, man? There’s nothing, there’s nobody, not all the detectives in London and Barford, can prove that you didn’t issue a ticket to Parrawhite on that date? It isn’t up to you to prove that you did!—it’s up to them to prove that you didn’t! And—they can’t. It’s impossible. You get that letter off—at once—to Liverpool, with that money inside it, and you’re as safe as houses—and your hundred pounds as well. Get it done! And if those chaps come asking any more questions, tell ’em you’re not going to answer a single one! Mind you!—do what I tell you, and you’re safe!”

===Chinese===
“那跟这桩事有个鬼的相干？”普拉特不耐烦地咬牙喝道。“那客人去没去成，鬼才管呢！你只要顾一头：证明你给帕拉怀特出过一张票，用的是帕森斯的名字。帕拉怀特——化名帕森斯——既然出了你的店门，他去了哪儿，与你何干？你自然只当他径直奔兰开夏—约克郡车站，取道利物浦、纽约去了！可天晓得，没准儿他栽进了隔壁街的水管道里去了呢！你还不明白么，伙计？没有什么东西、没有什么人——伦敦巴福德全部侦探捆在一起——能证明你那天没给帕拉怀特出过票？要证明出过票，轮不着你！——要证明你没出过票，那才是他们的事！可他们——证明不了。根本不可能。你把那封信寄出去——立刻——寄到利物浦，钱封在里头，你就稳稳当当万无一失——你那一百英镑也稳稳当当。去办吧！那两个家伙要是再来问三问四，就告诉他们，你一个字也不打算回！记牢！——照我说的办，你就平安无事！”

===Original===
With that Pratt walked out of the shop and went off towards the centre of the town, inwardly raging and disturbed. It was very evident that these people meant to find Parrawhite, alive or dead; evident, too, that they had called in the aid of the Barford police. And in spite of all his assurances to the watchmaker and his suggestion for the next move, Pratt was far from easy about the whole matter. He would have been easier if he had known who Prydale’s companion was—probably he was, as Murgatroyd had suggested, a London detective who might have been making inquiries in the town for some time and knew much more than he, Pratt, could surmise. That was the devil of the whole thing!—in Pratt’s opinion. Adept himself in working underground, he feared people who adopted the same tactics. What was this stranger chap after? What did he know? What was he doing? Had he let Eldrick know anything? Was there a web of detectives already being spun around himself? Was that silly, unfortunate affair with Parrawhite being slowly brought to light—to wreck him on the very beginning of what he meant to be a brilliant career? He cursed Parrawhite again and again as he left Peel Row behind him.

===Chinese===
说罢普拉特便出了店门，朝镇中心去了，心里又恼又乱。这些人分明打定了主意，要把帕拉怀特寻出来，活要见人、死要见尸；同样分明的是，他们已经搬来了巴福德警方的帮手。尽管他给钟表匠吃下了那许多定心丸，又指点好了下一步棋，可对整桩事，普拉特心里远谈不上安生。他若知道普赖代尔那个同伴是谁，心里倒能松快些——多半正如默加特罗伊德所猜，是个伦敦来的侦探，兴许已在镇上暗地里查访了些时日，晓得的远远多过他普拉特所能揣度的。这才是整桩事里最邪门的要命处！——照普拉特的想法。他自己惯于在暗地里做功夫，也就最怵那些使同样手段的人。这个生面孔图的是什么？他知道些什么？他在干什么？他给埃尔德里克透没透风？一张侦探的网，是不是已经在他周身织开了？同帕拉怀特的那桩糊涂晦气事，是不是正在一点点地见光——要在锦绣前程才起头的当口，把他整个毁掉？他把皮尔罗抛在身后，一路把帕拉怀特咒了一遍又一遍。

===Original===
The events of the day had made Pratt cautious as well as anxious. He decided to keep away from his lodgings that night, and when he reached the centre of the town he took a room at a quiet hotel. He was up early next morning; he had breakfasted by eight o’clock; by half-past eight he was at his office. And in his letter-box he found one letter—a thickish package which had not come by post, but had been dropped in by hand, and was merely addressed to Mr. Pratt.

===Chinese===
这一天发生的事，让普拉特既心头发慌，也添了几分谨慎。他决计当晚避开自己的寓所，一到镇中心，便在一家清静的旅馆里开了个房间。第二天他起得早；八点钟前用完了早饭；八点半便到了事务所。信箱里，他发现了一封信——一个厚墩墩的邮件，不是邮寄来的，是有人亲手投进去的，封皮上只写着“普拉特先生”收。

===Original===
Pratt tore that package open with a conviction of imminent disaster. He pulled out a sheet of cheap notepaper—and a wad of banknotes. His face worked curiously as he read a few lines, scrawled in illiterate, female handwriting.

===Chinese===
普拉特撕开那件包裹，心里已经认定大祸就在眼前。他抽出一页廉价信笺——和一沓钞票。信上寥寥几行，出自一个不通文墨的女人之手，字迹歪歪扭扭；他读着，脸上的肌肉怪模怪样地抽动起来。

===Original===
Mr. Pratt—My husband and me don’t want any more to do with either you or your money which it is enclosed. Been honest up to now though poor, and intending to remain so our purpose is to make a clean breast of everything to the police first thing tomorrow morning for which you have nobody but yourself to blame for wickedness in tempting poor people to do wrong.

Yours, Mrs. Murgatroyd.

===Chinese===
普拉特先生——俺当家的和俺，往后不想再跟你、也不想再跟你那笔钱——就是封在里头的那个——有任何相干。俺们穷是穷，一直老实到现在，也打算就这么老实下去，俺们的打算就是明儿一早头一件事，去警察那儿把一切来个和盘托出，这你怨不得旁人，全怨你自己使坏，勾穷人家去干亏心事。

你的，默加特罗伊德太太。

## XXV. Dry Sherry / 二十五 干雪利

===Original===
Pratt wasted no time in cursing Mrs. Murgatroyd. There would be plenty of opportunity for such relief to his feelings later on. Just then he had other matters to occupy him—fully. He tore the indignant letter to shreds; he hastily thrust the banknotes into one pocket and drew his keys from another. Within five minutes he had taken from his safe a sealed packet, which he placed in an inside pocket of his coat, and had left his office—for the last time, as he knew very well. That part of the game was up—and it was necessary to be smart in entering on another phase of it.

===Chinese===
普拉特没闲工夫去咒骂默加特罗伊德太太。往后有的是机会出这口恶气。眼下单是别的事，就够他全副心神应付了。他把那封怒气冲冲的信撕了个粉碎；匆匆把钞票塞进一只衣袋，又从另一只衣袋里掏出钥匙。不出五分钟，他从保险柜里取出一个封好的纸包，揣进外套内袋，随即离开了事务所——他心里透亮，这一走，就再也不会回来了。这一局的棋已经下死——必须手脚麻利，赶紧转入下一局。

===Original===
Since Eldrick’s visit of the previous day, Pratt had been prepared for all eventuality. He had made ready for flight. And he was not going empty-handed. He had a considerable amount of Mrs. Mallathorpe’s money in his possession; by obtaining her signature to one or two documents he could easily obtain much more in London, at an hour’s notice. Those documents were all ready, and in the sealed packet which he had just taken from the safe; in it, too, were some other documents—John Mallathorpe’s will; the letter which Mrs. Mallathorpe had written to him on the evening previous to her son’s fatal accident; and the power of attorney which Pratt had obtained from her at his first interview after that occurrence. All was ready—and now there was nothing to do but to get to Normandale Grange, see Mrs. Mallathorpe, and—vanish. He had planned it all out, carefully, when he perceived the first danger signals, and knew that his other plans and schemes were doomed to failure. Half an hour at Normandale Grange—a journey to London—a couple of hours in the City—and then the next train to the Continent, on his way to regions much further off. Here, things had turned out badly, unexpectedly badly—but he would carry away considerable, easily transported wealth, to a new career in a new country.

===Chinese===
打前一天埃尔德里克来过之后，普拉特就已做好万全准备，以防一切变故。逃走的行装他早备妥了。而且他不打算两手空空地走。他手里已握着马拉索普太太相当一笔钱；只消拿到她在一两份文书上的签名，便可以在伦敦临时只给一个钟头的知会，也轻而易举地再提取出多得多的钱。那几份文书都已备好，就装在他刚从保险柜里取出的那个纸包里；包里还有另外几份文件——约翰·马拉索普的遗嘱；马拉索普太太在她儿子罹祸前夜写给他的那封信；还有出事之后普拉特头一回见她时从她手里拿到的授权委托书。万事俱备——如今只消赶到诺曼代尔庄园，见上马拉索普太太一面，然后——消失。早在瞧出第一批危险信号、明白自己别的种种盘算注定要泡汤的时候，他就把这一切细细谋划停当了。在诺曼代尔庄园待上半个钟头——去一趟伦敦——在伦敦城里花上两个钟头——然后搭下一班火车远赴欧陆，奔向遥远得多的地方。在这里，事情砸了，砸得出乎意料地狠——但他要卷走一笔可观而又便于携带的财富，去新的国度开辟新的前程。

===Original===
Pratt began his flight in methodical fashion. He locked up his office, and left the building by a back entrance which took him into a network of courts and alleys at the rear of the business part of Barford. He made his way in and out of these places until he reached a bicycle-dealer’s shop in an obscure street, whereat he had left a machine of his own on the previous evening under the excuse of having it thoroughly cleaned and oiled. It was all ready for him on his arrival, and he presently mounted it and rode away through the outskirts of the town, carefully choosing the less frequented streets and roads. He rode on until he was clear of Barford: until, in fact, he was some miles from it, and had reached a village which was certainly not on the way to Normandale. And then, at the post-office he dismounted, and going inside, wrote out and dispatched a telegram. It was a brief message containing but three words—“One as usual”—and it was addressed Esther Mawson, The Grange, Normandale. This done, he remounted his bicycle, rode out of the village, and turned across country in quite a different direction. It was not yet ten o’clock—he had three hours to spare before the time came for keeping the appointment which he had just made.

===Chinese===
普拉特有条不紊地开始逃亡。他锁好事务所，从一道后门出了楼——后门通着巴福德商业区背后一片纵横交错的小院窄巷。他在这些地方穿进穿出，一直走到一条僻静街上的一家自行车铺——头天晚上，他借口要把车彻底擦洗上油，把自己的一辆车寄放在了那里。他一到，车已收拾得妥妥帖帖，他随即跨上去，穿过镇郊骑行而出，一路上留心拣行人稀少的街巷道路。他一路骑到出了巴福德地界：确切地说，骑出去好几英里，到了一个村子——那村子分明不在去诺曼代尔的路上。到了那儿，他在邮局门前下了车，进店写了份电报发出去。电文很短，只有三个词——“照旧”（One as usual）——收报人是诺曼代尔庄园的埃丝特·莫森。办完这事，他重新跨上车，骑出村子，折向野外一条截然不同的方向。这时还不到十点钟——离他刚约定下的会面时辰，还有整整三个钟头的富余。

===Original===
At an early stage of his operations, Pratt had found that even the cleverest of schemers cannot work unaided. It had been absolutely necessary to have some tool close at hand to Normandale Grange and its inhabitants; to have some person there upon whom he could depend for news. He had found that person, that tool, in Esther Mawson, who, as Mrs. Mallathorpe’s maid, had opportunities which he at once recognized as being likely to be of the greatest value to him. The circumstances of Harper Mallathorpe’s death had thrown Pratt and the maid together, and he had quickly discovered that she was to be bought, and would do anything for money. He had soon come to an understanding with her; soon bargained with her, and made her a willing accomplice in certain of his schemes, without letting her know their full meaning and extent: all, indeed, that she had learned from Pratt was that he had some considerable hold on her mistress.

===Chinese===
在布局的早期，普拉特便发觉，再精明的谋划家也不能孤身成事。他必须在诺曼代尔庄园和庄园诸人的近旁安上一件顺手的工具；必须在那里安插一个能替他递消息的人。这个人、这件工具，他在埃丝特·莫森身上找到了——她身为马拉索普太太的女仆，种种便利，普拉特一眼便看出大可为他所用。哈珀·马拉索普之死的那番情由，把普拉特和这女仆牵到了一处，他很快便摸清：这女人是钱能买通的，为了钱什么都肯干。不久他就同她达成了默契；不久就同她讲定了价钱，把她变成了自己某些计谋里一个甘心情愿的同伙——只是没让她知道那些计谋的全部用意和底细：说起来，她从普拉特那里打听到的全部，无非是他对她家太太捏着不小的一条把柄。

===Original===
But it is dangerous work to play with edged tools, and if Pratt had only known it, he was running great risks in using Esther Mawson as a semi-accomplice. Esther Mawson was in constant touch with her mistress, and Mrs. Mallathorpe, afraid of her daughter, and not greatly in sympathy with her, badly needed a confidante. Little by little the mistress began to confide in the maid, and before long Esther Mawson knew the secret—and thenceforward she played a double game. Pratt found her useful in arranging meetings with Mrs. Mallathorpe unknown to Nesta, and he believed her to be devoted to him. But the truth was that Esther Mawson had only one object of devotion—herself—and she was waiting and watching for an opportunity to benefit that object—at Pratt’s expense.

===Chinese===
然而玩弄利器，终是险事——普拉特倘若知道，便会明白，拿埃丝特·莫森当半个同伙使唤，是冒着天大的风险。埃丝特·莫森同她太太朝夕相处；而马拉索普太太呢，惧着女儿，同女儿又谈不上多么投契，正迫切需要一个能说贴心话的人。一点一点地，太太开始向女仆吐露心事，没过多久，埃丝特·莫森就知道了那个秘密——打那以后，她玩起了两面手法。普拉特觉得她大有用处，能替他瞒着妮斯塔安排同马拉索普太太的会面，还只当她一心向着自己。可实情是，埃丝特·莫森心里只供奉着一个主儿——她自己——她正守着、望着，等一个能让这位主儿得好处的机会——代价么，由普拉特来出。

===Original===
Pratt knew nothing of this as he slowly made his way to Normandale that morning. Having plenty of time he went by devious and lonely roads and by-lanes. Eventually he came to the boundary of Normandale Park at a point far away from the Grange. There he dismounted, hid his bicycle in a coppice wherein he had often left it before, and went on towards the house through the woods and plantations. He knew every yard of the ground he traversed, and was skilled in taking cover if he saw any sign of woodman or gamekeeper. And in the end, just as one o’clock chimed from the clock over the stables, he came to a quiet spot in the shrubberies behind the Grange, and found Esther Mawson waiting for him in an old summerhouse in which they had met on previous and similar occasions.

===Chinese===
那天早晨，普拉特慢慢悠悠地取道往诺曼代尔去，这些底细他一概不知。时间宽裕，他便拣僻静冷清的大路小径兜着圈子走。末了，他来到诺曼代尔园子边界上一处离庄园老远的地方。他在那里下了车，把自行车藏进一片矮树林——从前他也常把车撂在那儿——然后穿过树林和人工栽植的林带，朝大宅走去。脚下这一路，他一草一木都熟；但凡望见看林人或猎场看守的影子，他有的是躲闪的本事。最后，马厩上方的钟刚敲过一点，他到了庄园后面灌木林里一个僻静去处，只见埃丝特·莫森正在一座旧凉亭里等他——从前几回同样的幽会，都是在那里。

===Original===
Esther Mawson immediately realized that something unusual was in the air. Clever as Pratt was at concealing his feelings, she was cleverer in seeing small signs, and she saw that this was no ordinary visit.

===Chinese===
埃丝特·莫森立刻觉出风色不对。普拉特固然擅长掩饰，她却在察言观色上更胜一筹——她看出，这一回登门，不同往常。

===Original===
“Anything wrong?” she asked at once.

===Chinese===
“出什么事了？”她劈头就问。

===Original===
“Bit of bother—nothing much—it’ll blow over,” answered Pratt, who knew that a certain amount of candour was necessary in dealing with this woman. “But—I shall have to be away for a bit—week or two, perhaps.”

===Chinese===
“有点儿麻烦——不碍事——过阵子就过去了，”普拉特答道——他明白，跟这个女人打交道，话里须掺上几分实话。“只是——我得出门避一阵子——兴许一两个礼拜。”

===Original===
“You want to see her?” inquired Esther.

===Chinese===
“你要见她？”埃丝特问。

===Original===
“Of course! I’ve some papers for her to sign,” replied Pratt. “How do things stand? Coast clear?”

===Chinese===
“当然！我有几份文书要她签，”普拉特答道。“眼下家里怎么样？没有碍事的人吧？”

===Original===
“Miss Mallathorpe’s going into Barford after lunch,” answered Esther. “She’ll be driving in about half-past two. I can manage it then. How long shall you want to be with her?”

===Chinese===
“马拉索普小姐吃过午饭要进巴福德去，”埃丝特答道。“两点半上下赶车动身。那会儿我就能安排。你要同她待多久？”

===Original===
“Oh, a quarter of an hour’ll do,” said Pratt. “Ten minutes, if it comes to that.”

===Chinese===
“哦，一刻钟尽够了，”普拉特说。“真要赶，十分钟也成。”

===Original===
“And after that?” asked Esther.

===Chinese===
“往后呢？”埃丝特问。

===Original===
“Then I want to get a train at Scaleby,” replied Pratt, mentioning a railway junction which lay ten miles across country in another direction. “So make it as soon after two-thirty as you can.”

===Chinese===
“然后我得赶斯凯尔比（Scaleby）的车，”普拉特答道。斯凯尔比是个铁路联轨站，隔着旷野在另一个方向，足有十英里路程。“所以你尽量把会面排在两点半以后越早越好。”

===Original===
“You can see her as soon as Miss Mallathorpe’s gone,” said Esther. “You’d better come into the house—I’ve got the key of the turret door, and all’s clear—the servants are all at dinner.”

===Chinese===
“马拉索普小姐一走，你就能见她，”埃丝特说。“你最好进宅子里来——塔楼的门钥匙在我手上，一路没有碍事的人——仆人们这会儿都在吃饭。”

===Original===
“I could do with something myself,” observed Pratt, who, in his anxiety, had only made a light breakfast that morning. “Can it be managed?”

===Chinese===
“我自己也想垫垫肚子，”普拉特说——他心里发急，那天早晨只胡乱吃了一点儿。“能办到么？”

===Original===
“I’ll manage it,” she answered. “Come on—now.”

===Chinese===
“我自有办法，”她答道。“走吧——就现在。”

===Original===
Behind the summerhouse in which they had met a narrow path led through the shrubberies to an old part of the Grange which was never used, and was, in fact, partly ruinous. Esther Mawson led the way along this until she and Pratt came to a turret in the grey walls, in the lower story of which a massive oaken door, heavily clamped with iron, gave entrance to a winding stair, locked it from inside when she and Pratt had entered, and preceded her companion up the stair, and across one or two empty and dust-covered chambers to a small room in which a few pieces of ancient furniture were slowly dropping to decay. Pratt had taken refuge in this room before, and he sat down in one of the old chairs and mopped his forehead.

===Chinese===
他们碰面的那座凉亭后面，一条窄径穿过灌木林，通到庄园里一片从不住人的旧屋——那里其实已半属倾颓。埃丝特·莫森领着路，一路走到灰色墙垣间一座塔楼跟前。塔楼底层有一扇厚实的橡木门，门上钉着粗重的铁箍，门内便是一道盘旋的楼梯；两人进去后，她从里面把门锁上，领先同伴拾级而上，穿过一两间积尘遍布的空屋，来到一间小屋——屋里几件古旧的家具正慢慢地朽坏。普拉特从前也曾躲进这间屋子避过风，这会儿他在一把旧椅子上坐下，抹了抹额头。

===Original===
“I want something to drink, above everything,” he remarked. “What can you get?”

===Chinese===
“我顶要紧的是弄点儿喝的，”他说。“你能弄到什么？”

===Original===
“Nothing but wine,” answered Esther Mawson. “As much as you like of that, because I’ve a stock that’s kept up in Mrs. Mallathorpe’s room. I couldn’t get any ale without going to the butler. I can get wine and sandwiches without anybody knowing.”

===Chinese===
“除了酒，旁的没有，”埃丝特·莫森答道。“酒要多少有多少——马拉索普太太房里长年存着一箱。啤酒我不去找管家就取不着。酒和三明治，我能拿来不叫任何人知道。”

===Original===
“That’ll do,” said Pratt. “What sort of wine?”

===Chinese===
“那就行，”普拉特说。“什么酒？”

===Original===
“Port, sherry, claret,” she replied. “Whichever you like.”

===Chinese===
“波特、雪利、红葡萄酒，”她答道。“随你挑。”

===Original===
“Sherry, then,” answered Pratt. “Bring a bottle if you can get it—I want a good drink.”

===Chinese===
“那就雪利，”普拉特答道。“拿得动就拿一瓶来——我要好好喝一顿。”

===Original===
The woman went away—through the disused part of the old house into the modern portion. She went straight to a certain store closet and took from it a bottle of old dry sherry which had been brought there from a bin in the cellars—it was part of a quantity of fine wine laid down by John Mallathorpe, years before, and its original owner would have been disgusted to think that it should ever be used for the mere purpose of quenching thirst. But Esther Mawson had another purpose in view, with respect to that bottle. Carrying it to her own sitting-room, she carefully cut off the thick mass of sealing-wax at its neck, drew the cork, and poured a little of the wine away. And that done, she unlocked a small box which stood on a corner of her dressing table, and took from it a glass phial, half full of a colourless liquid. With steady hands and sure fingers, she dropped some of that liquid into the wine, carefully counting the drops. Then she restored the phial to its hiding-place and re-locked the box—after which, taking up a spoon which lay on her table, she poured out a little of the sherry and smelled and tasted it. No smell—other than that which ought to be there; no taste—other than was proper. Pratt would suspect nothing even if he drunk the whole bottle.

===Chinese===
那女人去了——穿过老宅废弃的那一片，走进新宅那一边。她径直来到一个贮物间，从里面取出一瓶陈年干雪利——那瓶酒是从酒窖的一格酒架上挪上来的——原是约翰·马拉索普多年前窖下的一批好酒的一部分；这位昔日的主人若是想到这酒竟有朝一日只落得供人解渴，怕不气得皱眉。可对这瓶酒，埃丝特·莫森另有一番用意。她把酒端进自己的起居室，细心割净瓶颈上厚厚一层封蜡，拔出软木塞，先倒掉一点儿酒。做完这些，她打开梳妆台一角上一只小盒子的锁，从里头取出一支玻璃小药瓶，瓶里盛着半瓶无色的液体。她手稳指准，往酒里滴进几滴那液体，一滴一滴，细心数着。随后她把药瓶放回藏处，重新锁好盒子——接着拿起桌上一把小匙，斟出少许雪利，闻了闻，尝了尝。没有异味——除了本该有的酒香；没有异样——除了本该有的酒味。哪怕普拉特把整瓶喝干，也起不了半点疑心。

===Original===
Esther Mawson had anticipated Pratt’s desires in the way of refreshment, and she now went to a cupboard and took from it a plate of sandwiches, carefully swathed in a napkin. Carrying these in one hand, and the bottle of sherry and a glass in the other, she stole quietly back to the disused part of the house, and set her provender before its expectant consumer. Pratt poured out a glassful of the sherry, and drank it eagerly.

===Chinese===
埃丝特·莫森早料到了普拉特在吃食上的想头，这时她走到食橱前，取出一盘用餐巾仔细裹好的三明治。她一手端盘，一手拿着雪利酒瓶和一只玻璃杯，蹑手蹑脚地折回老宅废弃的那一片屋子，把吃食摆在了那位翘首以待的食客面前。普拉特斟了满满一杯雪利，急切切地一饮而尽。

===Original===
“Good stuff that!” he remarked, smacking his lips. “Some of old John Mallathorpe’s—no doubt.”

===Chinese===
“好酒！”他咂着嘴说。“老约翰·马拉索普窖下的——错不了。”

===Original===
“It was here when we came, anyhow,” replied Esther. “Well—I shall have to go. You’ll be all right until I come back.”

===Chinese===
“反正咱们来的时候这酒就在这儿，”埃丝特答道。“好了——我得走了。你自管待着，等我回来。”

===Original===
“What time do you think it’ll be?” asked Pratt. “Make it as soon as the coast’s clear—I want to be off.”

===Chinese===
“你估摸着会是几点？”普拉特问。“人一走净就赶紧安排——我要动身。”

===Original===
“As soon as ever she’s gone,” agreed Esther. “I heard her order the carriage for half-past two.”

===Chinese===
“她一走就成，”埃丝特应道。“我听见她吩咐两点半套车。”

===Original===
“And no fear of anybody else being about?” asked Pratt. “That butler man, for instance? Or servants?”

===Chinese===
“不至于再撞见别的人吧？”普拉特问。“比方说，那个管家老头儿？还有仆人们？”

===Original===
“I’ll see to it,” replied Esther reassuringly. “I’ll lock this door and take the key until I come back—make yourself comfortable.”

===Chinese===
“这事交给我，”埃丝特宽慰道。“我把这扇门锁上，钥匙带走，回来再开——你自管宽坐。”

===Original===
She locked Pratt in the old room and went off, and the willing prisoner ate his sandwiches and drank his sherry, and looked out of a mullioned window on the wide stretches of park and coppice and the breezy moorlands beyond. He indulged in some reflections—not wholly devoid of sentiment. He had cherished dreams of becoming the virtual owner of Normandale. Always confident in his own powers, he had believed that with time and patience he could have persuaded Nesta Mallathorpe to marry him—why not? Now—all owing to that cursed and unfortunate contretemps with Parrawhite, that seemed utterly impossible—all he could do now was to save himself—and to take as much as he could get. More than once that morning, as he made his way across country, he had remembered Parrawhite’s advice to take cash and be done with it—perhaps, he reflected, it might have been better. Still—when he presently began his final retreat, he would carry away with him a lot of the Mallathorpe money.

===Chinese===
她把普拉特锁进那间老屋，自去了。这位心甘情愿的囚徒吃着三明治，喝着雪利，从一扇直棂格窗里望出去，园子里开阔的草地和矮树林一望无际，再远处便是风声飒飒的荒原。他不禁胡思乱想了一阵——倒也不全是算计，还夹着几分感慨。他曾做着美梦，要当诺曼代尔实际上的主人。他一向自负手段，一向相信只消假以时日和耐性，准能说动妮斯塔·马拉索普嫁给他——凭什么不能？可如今——全坏在同帕拉怀特那桩该死的、倒霉的意外纠葛上，这一切眼见是万万不能了——如今他能做的，只有保住他自己——外加能捞多少捞多少。那天早晨穿野赶路时，他不只一回想起帕拉怀特那句话：拿了现钱，一了百了——如今想来，兴许那样倒好。不过——待会儿踏上最后的退路时，他终归要把马拉索普家的一大笔钱卷走。

===Original===
But before long Pratt indulged in no more reflections—sentiment or practical. He had eaten all his sandwiches; he had drunk three-quarters of the bottle of sherry. And suddenly he felt unusually drowsy, and he laid his head back in his big chair, and fell soundly asleep.

===Chinese===
可是没过多久，普拉特便什么都想不动了——感慨也好，算计也罢。三明治吃完了；那瓶雪利，他也喝去了四分之三。蓦地，一阵反常的困意袭来，他往大椅子背上一仰头，便沉沉睡去了。

## XXVI. The Telephone Message / 二十六 电话报信

===Original===
If Pratt had only known what was going on in the old quarries at Whitcliffe, about the very time that he was riding slowly out to Barford on his bicycle, he would not only have accelerated his pace, but would have taken good care to have chosen another route: he would also have made haste to exchange bicycle for railway train as quickly as possible, and to have got himself far away before anybody could begin looking for him in his usual haunts, or at places wherein there was a possibility of his being found. But Pratt knew nothing of what Byner had done. He was conscious of Byner’s visit to the Green Man. He did not know what Pickard had been told by Bill Thomson. He was unaware of anything which Pickard had told to Byner. If he had known that Byner, guided by Pickard, had been to the old quarries, had fixed his inquiring eye on the shaft which was filled to its brim with water, and had got certain ideas from the mere sight of it, Pratt would have hastened to put hundreds of miles between himself and Barford as quickly as possible. But all that Pratt knew was that there was a possibility of suspicion—which might materialize eventually, but not immediately.

===Chinese===
普拉特若是知道，就在他慢悠悠骑着自行车离开巴福德的那阵子，惠特克利夫（Whitcliffe）的老采石场里正进行着什么，那他非但要加快脚程，还必定仔仔细细另拣一条路线不可；更要急急慌忙把自行车换成火车，越快越好，赶在任何人想到去他惯常出没的地方、或是那些有可能寻见他的处所找他之前，先把自己送到远远的地方去。可普拉特对拜纳干下的事一无所知。他只晓得拜纳去过‘绿人’酒馆。皮卡德从比尔·汤姆森那儿听了些什么，他不知道。皮卡德又向拜纳讲了些什么，他更无从知晓。他若知道拜纳由皮卡德领着到过老采石场，把那双刨根问底的眼睛盯在灌得齐了井口的那口竖井上，又单凭望见那一眼便生出了某些想头，普拉特准会火速在自己和巴福德之间隔出几百英里去。可普拉特知道的，无非是事情有被起疑的可能——这疑心或许终归会坐实，却不会是眼面前的事。

===Original===
On the previous evening, Pratt—had he but known it—made a great mistake. Instead of going into Murgatroyd’s shop after he had watched Byner and Prydale away from it—he should have followed those two astute and crafty persons, and have ascertained something of their movements. Had he done so, he would certainly not have troubled to return to Peel Row, nor to remain in Barford an hour longer than was absolutely necessary. For Pratt was sharp-witted enough when it came to a question of putting one and two together, and if he had tracked Prydale and the unknown man who was with him to a certain house whereto they repaired as soon as they quitted Murgatroyd’s shop, he would have drawn an inference from the mere fact of their visit which would have thrown him into a cold sweat of fear. But Pratt, after all, was only one man, one brain, one body, and could not be in two places, nor go in two ways, at the same time. He took his own way—ignorant of his destruction.

===Chinese===
头天傍晚，普拉特——他若是知道才好——犯下了个大错。他望着拜纳和普赖代尔走出默加特罗伊德的铺子，本该跟定那两个精明狡黠的人物，摸清几分他们的动向才是，他却偏偏进了铺子。他若是照那样做了，断不至于再费事折回皮尔罗去，更断不会在巴福德多待上一个钟头——比万不得已多待一个钟头。因为论把一和二加在一起的机灵劲儿，普拉特的脑子是足够使的；他若是一路尾随普赖代尔和那个同他一道的陌生汉子，跟到两人一离开默加特罗伊德的铺子便径直奔去的那所宅子，那么单凭他们登门这一桩事实，他便能推出一个结论——准叫他吓得一身冷汗。可普拉特说到底只是孤身一人，一副脑筋，一具身子，不能同时身在两处，也不能同时兵分两路。他走了他自己的路——对大祸临头浑然不觉。

===Original===
Byner also took a way of his own. As soon as he and Prydale left Murgatroyd’s shop, they chartered the first cab they met with, and ordered its driver to go to Whitcliffe Moor.

===Chinese===
拜纳也走了一条他自己的路。他同普赖代尔一离开默加特罗伊德的铺子，便雇下迎面碰上的头一辆马车，吩咐车夫赶往惠特克利夫荒原。

===Original===
“It’s the quickest thing to do—if my theory’s correct,” observed Byner, as they drove along, “Of course, it is all theory—mere theory! But I’ve grounds for it. The place—the time—mere lonely situation—that scrap iron lying about, which would be so useful in weighting a dead body!—I tell you, I shall be surprised if we don’t find Parrawhite at the bottom of that water!”

===Chinese===
“这是眼下最快捷的一着——倘若我的推想没错，”马车一路走着，拜纳说道，“当然喽，这全是推想——不过是推想罢了！可我有根据。那地方——那时间——四下荒无人烟——还有散在那儿的废铁，拿来给尸身压秤可太顺手了！——我跟你说，要是那潭水底下捞不着帕拉怀特，我才要奇怪呢！”

===Original===
“I shouldn’t wonder,” agreed Prydale. “One thing’s very certain, as we shall prove before we’re through with it—Pratt’s put that poor devil Murgatroyd up to this passage-to-America business. And a bit clumsily, too—fancy Murgatroyd being no better posted up than to tell me that Parrawhite called on him at a certain hour that night!”

===Chinese===
“我也不会奇怪，”普赖代尔附和道。“有一件事是千真万确的，等咱们把这案子办到底自会证实——那套‘帕拉怀特去了美国’的把戏，是普拉特撺掇可怜虫默加特罗伊德干的。而且干得还挺笨——你想想，默加特罗伊德消息竟那样不灵通，居然对我说帕拉怀特那天夜里某个钟点登门拜访过他！”

===Original===
“But you’ve got to remember that Pratt didn’t know of Parrawhite’s affairs with Pickard, nor that he was at the Green Man at that hour,” rejoined Byner. “My belief is that Pratt thinks himself safe—that he fancies he’s provided for all contingencies. If things turn out as I think they will, I believe we shall find Pratt calmly seated at his desk tomorrow morning.”

===Chinese===
“可你得记住，普拉特并不知道帕拉怀特同皮卡德之间的那些纠葛，也不知道他那个钟点正在‘绿人’酒馆，”拜纳回道。“依我看，普拉特自以为稳当——自以为万无一失，诸般意外都打点周全了。倘若事情照我料想的发展，我敢说，明天早晨准能在办公桌前寻见普拉特，他还稳稳当当坐着呢。”

===Original===
“Well—if things do turn out as you expect, we’ll lose no time in seeking him there!” observed Prydale dryly. “We’d better arrange to get the job done first thing.”

===Chinese===
“好啊——事情真要照你料想的那样，咱们一刻也不耽搁，立马就去那儿寻他！”普赖代尔干巴巴地说。“这档子事顶好赶头一件办妥。”

===Original===
“This Mr. Shepherd’ll make no objection, I suppose?” asked Byner.

===Chinese===
“这位谢泼德先生该不会有异议吧？”拜纳问。

===Original===
“Objection! Lor’ bless you—he’ll love it!” exclaimed Prydale. “It’ll be a bit of welcome diversion to a man like him that’s naught to do. He’ll object none, not he!”

===Chinese===
“异议！老天爷保佑——他喜欢还来不及呢！”普赖代尔叫道。“像他那样闲得没事可做的人，这可是求之不得的消遣。他绝不会不乐意，才不会呢！”

===Original===
Shepherd, a retired quarry-owner, who lived in a picturesque old stone house in the middle of Whitcliffe Moor, with nothing to occupy his attention but the growing of roses and vegetables, and an occasional glance at the local newspapers, listened to Prydale’s request with gradually rising curiosity. Byner had at once seen that this call was welcome to this bluff and hearty Yorkshireman, who, without any question as to their business, had immediately welcomed them to his hearth and pressed liquor and cigars on them: he sized up Shepherd as a man to whom any sort of break in the placid course of retired life was a delightful event.

===Chinese===
谢泼德是个退了休的采石场主，住在惠特克利夫荒原正中一所别有景致的老石头宅子里，平日里无非栽栽玫瑰、种种菜蔬，偶尔翻一翻本地报纸，再没有别的营生来分他的心。他听普赖代尔说明来意，好奇心一阵高过一阵。拜纳一眼便看出，这一趟登门正对了这位直爽热诚的约克郡人的脾胃——人家连他们的来意都没问一声，就把两人迎到炉火边，又是劝酒又是敬雪茄。他把谢泼德掂量了个通透：在这种人看来，安恬如水的退隐日子里但凡起一点波澜，都是桩叫人痛快的事。

===Original===
“A dead man i’ that old shaft i’ one o’ my worked out quarries!” he exclaimed. “Ye don’t mean to say so! An’ how long d’yer think he might ha’ been there, now, Prydale?”

===Chinese===
“俺那些采尽了的老石场里，那口老竖井里头竟有个死人！”他叫了起来。“你可不是说着玩的吧！那你倒说说，普赖代尔，你觉得他在那里头兴许待多久了？”

===Original===
“Some months, Mr. Shepherd,” replied the detective.

===Chinese===
“总有几个月了，谢泼德先生，”侦探答道。

===Original===
“Why, then it’s high time he were taken out,” said Shepherd. “When might you be thinkin’ o’ doin’ t’ job, like?”

===Chinese===
“哎呀，那可就该赶紧把他捞上来了，”谢泼德说。“这活计你们寻思着啥时候动手，嗯？”

===Original===
“As soon as possible,” said Prydale. “Tomorrow morning, early, if that’s convenient to you.”

===Chinese===
“越快越好，”普赖代尔说。“就明天一早，要是您方便的话。”

===Original===
“I’ll tell you what I’ll do,” observed the retired quarry-owner. “You leave t’ job to me. I’ll get two or three men first thing tomorrow morning, and we’ll do it reight. You be up there by half-past eight o’clock, and we’ll soon satisfy you as to whether there’s owt i’ t’ shape of a dead man or not i’ t’ pit. You hev’ grounds for believin’ ’at theer is—what?”

===Chinese===
“俺跟你们说俺咋办，”这位退了休的采石场主说。“这活计你们只管交给俺。明儿一早，俺头一件事就去约上两三个人手，保准把事办得妥妥帖帖。你们八点半钟上那儿去，井里头到底有没有个死人模样的物件，俺们管保很快叫你们弄个明白。你们是有凭有据，才信那里头有个死人——对不？”

===Original===
“Strong grounds!” replied the detective, “and equally strong ones for believing the man came there by foul play, too.”

===Chinese===
“凭据硬得很！”侦探答道，“而且另有同样硬的凭据，叫人相信那人还是遭了毒手才到那儿去的。”

===Original===
“Say no more!” said Shepherd. “T’ mystery shall be cleared up. Deary me! An’ to think ’at I’ve walked past yon theer pit many a dozen times within this last few o’ months, and nivver dreamed ’at theer wor owt in it but watter! Howivver, gentlemen, ye can put yer minds at ease—we’ll investigate the circumstances, as the sayin’ goes, before noon tomorrow.”

===Chinese===
“不必多说了！”谢泼德说。“这个谜团非解开不可。哎呀我的天！想想俺这几个月里，打那口井旁边走过总有几十遭了，做梦也不曾想到那里头除了水还有别的物件！不过呢，二位只管把心放宽——俗话说的，查一查情形嘛，明天晌午以前管保有个结果。”

===Original===
“One other matter,” remarked Prydale. “We want things kept quiet. We don’t want all the folk of the neighbourhood round about, you know.”

===Chinese===
“还有一桩事，”普赖代尔说。“我们要事情办得隐秘。你明白，可不能叫左近四邻的人都围拢来看热闹。”

===Original===
“Leave it to me,” answered Shepherd. “There’ll be me, and these men, and yourselves—and a pair of grapplin’ irons. We’ll do it quiet and comfortable—and we’ll do it reight.”

===Chinese===
“这事包在俺身上，”谢泼德答道。“到时候有俺，有人手，有你们二位——再添上一副打捞挠钩。俺们悄没声儿、安安稳稳地把事办了——保准办得妥妥帖帖。”

===Original===
“Odd character!” remarked Byner, when he and Prydale went away.

===Chinese===
“怪人一个！”他和普赖代尔离去之后，拜纳说道。

===Original===
“Useful man—for a job of that sort,” said the detective laconically. “Now then—are we going to let anybody else know what we’re after—Mr. Eldrick or Mr. Collingwood, for instance? Do you want them, or either of them, to be present?”

===Chinese===
“管用的人——办这种事正合适，”侦探言简意赅地说。“那么——咱们要不要让别人知道咱们在忙什么——比方说，埃尔德里克先生，或者科林伍德先生？你要他们两位、或者随便哪一位到场么？”

===Original===
“No!” answered Byner, after a moment’s reflection. “Let us see what results. We can let them know, soon enough, if we’ve anything to tell. But—what about Pratt?”

===Chinese===
“不！”拜纳沉吟了片刻，答道。“先看看有什么结果。真有了可报的信儿，再知会他们不迟。不过——普拉特那边怎么办？”

===Original===
“Keeping an eye on him—you mean?” said Prydale. “You said just now that in your opinion we should find him at his desk.”

===Chinese===
“你是说——盯着他点儿？”普赖代尔说。“你方才还说，照你的意思，咱们准能在办公桌前寻见他的。”

===Original===
“Just so—but that’s no reason why he shouldn’t be looked after tomorrow morning,” answered Byner.

===Chinese===
“正是——可这并不妨碍明天早晨照样派人看住他，”拜纳答道。

===Original===
“All right—I’ll put a man on to shadow him, from the time he leaves his lodgings until—until we want him,” said the detective. “That is—if we do want him.”

===Chinese===
“行——我派个人钉他的梢，从他出寓所那一刻起，直到——直到咱们用得着他，”侦探说。“也就是说——如果真用得着他的话。”

===Original===
“It will be one of the biggest surprises I ever had in my life if we don’t!” asserted Byner. “I never felt more certain of anything than I do of finding Parrawhite’s body in that pit!”

===Chinese===
“要是用不着，那可就是我这辈子遇到的头一号奇事了！”拜纳断言道。“我这辈子对什么事都不曾这么有把握——帕拉怀特的尸首，准在那口井里！”

===Original===
It was this certainty which made Byner appear extraordinarily cool and collected, when next day, about noon, he walked into Eldrick’s private room, where Collingwood was at that moment asking the solicitor what was being done. The certainty was now established, and it seemed to Byner that it would have been a queer thing if he had not always had it. He closed the door and gave the two men an informing glance.

===Chinese===
正是这份把握，使拜纳在第二天晌午前后走进埃尔德里克的私人办事房时，显得格外冷静沉着——其时科林伍德正向老律师问起，眼下事情办得怎么样了。此刻把握已成定局，在拜纳想来，倒仿佛自己向来就一清二楚，先前若不如此反倒成怪事了。他关上门，朝那两位递了个有话要说的眼色。

===Original===
“Parrawhite’s body has been found,” he said quietly.

===Chinese===
“帕拉怀特的尸首找到了，”他轻声说。

===Original===
Eldrick started in his chair, and Collingwood looked a sharp inquiry.

===Chinese===
埃尔德里克在椅子里猛地一震，科林伍德投来一道锐利的探问目光。

===Original===
“Little doubt about his having been murdered, just as I conjectured,” continued Byner. “And his murderer had pretty cleverly weighted his body with scrap iron, before dropping it into a pit full of water, where it might have remained for a long time undiscovered. However—that’s settled!”

===Chinese===
“几乎毫无疑问，他是被人谋害的，正如我推测的那样，”拜纳接着说。“凶手在把尸身丢进一口满水的井里之前，用废铁给它压了秤，干得相当利落——要不然，尸首兴许还要很久才被人发觉。不过——这一桩算是定局了！”

===Original===
Eldrick got out the first question.

===Chinese===
埃尔德里克抢先问出了头一句。

===Original===
“Pratt?”

===Chinese===
“普拉特呢？”

===Original===
“Prydale’s after him,” answered Byner. “I expect we shall hear something in a few minutes—if he’s in town. But I confess I’m a bit doubtful and anxious now, on that score. Because, when Prydale and I got down from Whitcliffe half an hour ago—where the body’s now lying, at the Green Man, awaiting the inquest—we found Murgatroyd hanging about the police station. He’d come to make a clean breast of it—about Pratt. And it unfortunately turns out that Pratt saw Prydale and me go to Murgatroyd’s shop last night, and afterwards went in there himself, and of course pumped Murgatroyd dry as to why we’d been.”

===Chinese===
“普赖代尔正在追他，”拜纳答道。“要是他人还在城里，估计几分钟之内咱们就能有信儿。不过这一层，我承认我这会儿有点儿没底、有点儿发急。因为半个钟头前，普赖代尔和我从惠特克利夫下来——尸首如今就停放在那儿的‘绿人’酒馆，等候验尸审讯——我们发现默加特罗伊德在警察局跟前转来转去。他是来和盘托出实情的——关于普拉特。而不巧的是，偏偏查明昨天夜里普拉特瞧见普赖代尔同我进了默加特罗伊德的铺子，过后他自己也进去了，自然把咱们为何登门，从默加特罗伊德嘴里掏了个干干净净。”

===Original===
“Why unfortunately?” asked Collingwood.

===Chinese===
“为什么不巧？”科林伍德问。

===Original===
“Because that would warn Pratt that something was afoot,” said Byner. “And—he may have disappeared during the night. He—”

===Chinese===
“因为那会向普拉特示警：事情有动静了，”拜纳说。“那么——他兴许夜里就已经跑掉了。他——”

===Original===
But just then Prydale came in, shaking his head.

===Chinese===
恰在此时，普赖代尔走了进来，连连摇头。

===Original===
“I’m afraid he’s off!” he announced. “I’d a man watching for him outside his lodgings from an early hour this morning, but he never came out, and finally my man made an excuse and asked for him there, and then he heard that he’d never been home last night. And his office is closed.”

===Chinese===
“恐怕他是跑掉了！”他通报道。“今天一大早，我就派了个人守在他寓所外头，可他始终没出来，末了我那人寻了个由头进屋去求见，这才听说他昨儿夜里压根儿没回过家。事务所也关着门。”

===Original===
“What steps are you taking?” asked Byner.

===Chinese===
“你正在采取什么步骤？”拜纳问。

===Original===
“I’ve got men all over the place already,” replied Prydale. “But—if he got off in the night, as I’m afraid he did, we shan’t find him in Barford. It’s a most unlucky thing that he saw us go to Murgatroyd’s last evening! That, of course, would set him off: he’d know things were reaching a crisis.”

===Chinese===
“我的人手已经撒遍各处了，”普赖代尔答道。“可是——他若是夜里脱了身，我怕正是这样——那在巴福德就寻不着他了。昨天傍晚他撞见咱们去默加特罗伊德那儿，真是倒霉透顶！那一来他自然要走：他会明白，事情到了摊牌的关口了。”

===Original===
Eldrick and Collingwood had arranged to lunch together that day, and they presently went off, asking the detective to keep them informed of events. But up to half-past three o’clock they heard no more—then, as they were returning along the street Byner came running up to them.

===Chinese===
那天埃尔德里克和科林伍德原约好一同吃午饭，这时便动身走了，请侦探随时把动静知会他们。可直到三点半，他们再没听到什么信儿——随后，两人正沿着大街往回走，拜纳一路小跑赶了上来。

===Original===
“Prydale’s just had a telephone message from the butler at Normandale!” he exclaimed. “Pratt is there!—and something extraordinary is going on: the butler wants the police. We’re off at once—there’s Prydale in a motor, waiting for me. Will you follow?”

===Chinese===
“普赖代尔刚接到诺曼代尔那边的管家打来的电话！”他喊道。“普拉特在那儿！——而且正发生着一桩非同寻常的事：管家要请警察去。我们立刻动身——普赖代尔在一辆汽车里等着我呢。你们跟不跟来？”

===Original===
He darted away again, and Eldrick looking round for a car, suddenly recognized the Mallathorpe livery.

===Chinese===
他又一溜烟跑掉了。埃尔德里克正四下张望想找辆车，蓦地认出了马拉索普家的仆从号衣。

===Original===
“Great Scott!” he said. “There’s Miss Mallathorpe—just driving in. Better tell her!”

===Chinese===
“老天爷！”他说。“那不是马拉索普小姐么——正赶着车进镇来。顶好告诉她一声！”

===Original===
A moment later, he and Collingwood had joined Nesta in her carriage, and the horses’ heads were turned in the direction towards which Byner and Prydale were already hastening.

===Chinese===
片刻之后，他和科林伍德已经坐进妮斯塔的马车里，车头调转，朝着拜纳和普赖代尔已然奔去的方向驶去。

## XXVII. Restored to Energy / 二十七 元气复还

===Original===
Esther Mawson, leaving Pratt to enjoy his sherry and sandwiches at his leisure, went away through the house, out into the gardens, and across the shrubbery to the stables. The coachman and grooms were at dinner—with the exception of one man who lived in a cottage at the entrance to the stable-yard. This was the very man she wanted to see, and she found him in the saddle-room, and beckoned him to its door.

===Chinese===
埃丝特·莫森撂下普拉特，由他自自在在地享用雪利酒和三明治，自己穿宅过屋出了园子，越过灌木林，朝马厩走去。车夫和马夫们都在用饭——只有一个伙计例外，他住在马厩院子入口处的一所小屋里。她要找的正是这个人，她在马具室里寻见了他，朝他招手，把他唤到门口。

===Original===
“Mrs. Mallathorpe wants me to go over to Scaleby on an errand for her this afternoon,” she said. “Can you have the dogcart ready, at the South Garden gate at three o’clock sharp? And—without saying anything to the coachman? It’s a private errand.”

===Chinese===
“马拉索普太太要我今天下午替她跑一趟斯凯尔比，”她说。“你能不能把轻便马车备好，三点整在南花园门口候着？还有——别跟车夫提起半个字？这是桩私事。”

===Original===
Of late this particular groom had received several commissions of this sort, and being a sharp fellow he had observed that they were generally given to him when Miss Mallathorpe was out.

===Chinese===
近来到他手里的这路差使已有好几桩了；这马夫是个机灵人，早瞧出这类差使多半挑在马拉索普小姐出门的时候交下来。

===Original===
“All right,” he answered. “The young missis is going out in the carriage at half-past two. South Garden gate—three sharp. Anybody but you?”

===Chinese===
“成，”他答道。“大小姐两点半坐马车出门。南花园门——三点整。除了你还有旁人没有？”

===Original===
“Only me,” replied Esther. “Don’t say anything to anybody about where we’re going. Get the dogcart ready after the carriage has gone.”

===Chinese===
“就我一个，”埃丝特答道。“咱们去哪儿，对谁也别提。等马车走了再把轻便马车备出来。”

===Original===
The groom nodded in comprehension, and Esther went back to the house and to her own room. She ought at that time of day to have been eating her dinner with the rest of the upper servants, but she had work to do which was of much more importance than the consumption of food and drink. There was going to be a flight that afternoon—but it would not be Pratt who would undertake it. Esther Mawson had carefully calculated all her chances as soon as Pratt told her that he was going to be away for a while. She knew that Pratt would not have left Barford for any indefinite period unless something had gone seriously wrong. But she knew more—by inference and intuition. If Pratt was going away—rather, since he was going away, he would have on his person things of value—documents, money. She meant to gain possession of everything that he had; she meant to have a brief interview with Mrs. Mallathorpe; then she meant to drive to Scaleby—and to leave that part of the country just as thoroughly and completely as Pratt had meant to leave it. And now in her own room she was completing her preparations. There was little to do. She knew that if her venture came off successfully, she could easily afford to leave her personal possessions behind her, and that she would be all the more free and unrestricted in her movements if she departed without as much as a change of clothes and linen. And so by two o’clock she had arrayed herself in a neat and unobtrusive tailor-made travelling costume, had put on an equally neat and plain hat, had rolled her umbrella, and laid it, her gloves, and a cloak where they could be readily picked up, and had attached to her slim waist a handbag—by means of a steel chain which she secured by a small padlock as soon as she had arranged it to her satisfaction. She was not the sort of woman to leave a handbag lying about in a railway carriage at any time, but in this particular instance she was not going to run any risk of even a moment’s forgetfulness.

===Chinese===
马夫会意地点了点头，埃丝特便回转宅里，回到自己房中。按这个时辰，她本该同其余的上等仆役一块儿用饭，可她手头有事要办，那可比吃喝要紧得多。这天下午将有一场出逃——只是出逃的不会是普拉特。普拉特一告诉她自己要出门些时日，埃丝特·莫森便把手上所有的机会细细盘算了一遍。她知道，若不是出了大乱子，普拉特绝不会不明不白地离开巴福德。可她知道的还不止这些——凭着推断和直觉。普拉特要走——不如说，既然普拉特要走，他身上必定带着值钱的东西——文件、钱。她打算把他身上的东西一样不剩全数拿到手；打算同马拉索普太太面谈片刻；然后赶车去斯凯尔比——把这一带地方甩脱得同普拉特原先打算的那样干净彻底。此刻她正在自己房里做最后的准备。要做的其实不多。她知道，这桩买卖倘若得手，随身物件尽可弃之不顾，毫不心疼；而且倘若走时连一套换洗衣裳都不带，行动反倒愈发轻便无碍。于是到两点钟，她已穿上一身裁剪利落、不惹眼目的定做旅行女装，戴上一顶同样素净的帽子，卷好阳伞，把伞、手套和一件斗篷搁在一伸手就够得着的地方，又往自己纤细的腰间系了一只挂包——用一条钢链拴着，待收拾得妥妥帖帖，便拿一把小挂锁锁牢。她本就不是那种会把挂包遗落在火车车厢里的女人，而这一回，她更不容自己有半分疏忽——哪怕一眨眼的忘性也不行。

===Original===
Everything was in readiness by twenty minutes past two, and she took up her position in a window from which she could see the front door of the house. At half-past two the carriage and its two fine bay horses came round from the stables; a minute or two later Nesta Mallathorpe emerged from the hall; yet another minute and the carriage was whirling down the park in the direction of Barford. And then Esther moved from the window, picked up the umbrella, the cloak, the gloves, and went off in the direction of the room wherein she had left Pratt.

===Chinese===
到两点二十分，万事俱备，她便在一扇窗前占定位置，从那里望得见宅子的正门。两点半，那辆马车连同两匹神骏的枣红马从马厩那边绕了过来；过了一两分钟，妮斯塔·马拉索普从大厅里走了出来；再过一分钟，马车已顺着园中大路辚辚地朝巴福德方向驰去。于是埃丝特离开窗口，拿起阳伞、斗篷和手套，朝她安置普拉特的那间屋子走去。

===Original===
No one ever went near those old rooms except on some special errand or business, and there was a dead silence all around her as she turned the key in the lock and slipped inside the door—to lock it again as soon as she had entered. There was an equally deep silence within the room—and for a moment she glanced a little fearfully at the recumbent figure in the old, deep-backed chair. Pratt had stretched himself fully in his easy quarters—his legs lay extended across the moth-eaten hearthrug; his head and shoulders were thrown far back against the faded tapestry, and he was so still that he might have been supposed to be dead. But Esther Mawson had tried the effect of that particular drug on a good many people, and she knew that the victim in this instance was merely plunged in a sleep from which nothing whatever could wake him yet awhile. And after one searching glance at him, and one lifting of an eyelid by a practised finger, she went rapidly and thoroughly through Pratt’s pockets, and within a few minutes of entering the room had cleared them of everything they contained. The sealed packet which he had taken from his safe that morning; the banknotes which Mrs. Murgatroyd had returned in her indignant letter; another roll of notes, of considerable value, in a notecase; a purse containing notes and gold to a large amount—all those she laid one by one on a dust-covered table. And finally—and as calmly as if she were sorting linen—she swept banknotes, gold, and purse into her steel-chained bag, and tore open the sealed envelope.

===Chinese===
若不是有特别的差事，谁也不会走到那些老屋子跟前去。她把钥匙在锁眼里一转，闪身进门——随即从里面重新锁好，四下里一片死寂。屋子里面也一样静得出奇——有一瞬间，她朝那把高背老圈椅里横陈的人影投去略带怯意的一瞥。普拉特在他那安适的落脚处舒展开四肢——两条腿直直伸在虫蛀的炉边地毯上；头和肩膀深深仰靠着褪色的花毯挂饰，一动不动，简直要让人当他已经死了。可埃丝特·莫森在不少人身上试过那味药的效力，知道这一回遭算计的不过是沉入了一场酣睡，眼下天塌下来也醒不了。她对他细细打量了一眼，又用熟练的指头掀起一边眼皮看了看，随即开始迅速而彻底地搜检普拉特的衣袋，进屋不过几分钟，便把袋里的东西搜刮一空。他那天早晨从保险柜里取出的那个封好的纸包；默加特罗伊德太太随那封怒气冲冲的信退回来的钞票；皮夹里另外一卷为数可观的钞票；还有一只钱袋，装着大笔的纸币和金币——这些她一样一样摆到一张积尘的桌子上。最后——神色平静得仿佛在清点衬衣什物——她把钞票、金币连同钱袋一古脑儿扫进那只钢链挂包，撕开了那只封套。

===Original===
There were five documents in that envelope—Esther examined each with meticulous care. The first was an authority to Linford Pratt to sell certain shares standing in the name of Ann Mallathorpe. The second was a similar document relating to other shares: each was complete, save for Ann Mallathorpe’s signature. The third document was the power of attorney which Ann Mallathorpe had given to Linford Pratt: the fourth, the letter which she had written to him on the evening before the fatal accident to Harper. And the fifth was John Mallathorpe’s will.

===Chinese===
那只封套里共有五份文件——埃丝特一份一份细细验看。第一份，是授权林福德·普拉特出售记在安·马拉索普名下若干股份的文书。第二份是涉及其余股份的同类文书：两份都样样齐备，只差安·马拉索普的签字。第三份，是安·马拉索普授予林福德·普拉特的授权委托书；第四份，是她在哈珀罹祸前夜写给他的那封信。而第五份，便是约翰·马拉索普的遗嘱。

===Original===
At last she held in her hand the half-sheet of foolscap paper of which Mrs. Mallathorpe, driven to distraction, and knowing that she would get no sympathy from her own daughter, had told her. She was a woman of a quick and an understanding mind, and she had read the will through and grasped its significance as swiftly as her eyes ran over it. And those eyes turned to the unconscious Pratt with a flash of contempt—she, at any rate, would not follow his foolish example, and play for too high a stake—no, she would make hay while the sun shone its hottest! She was of the Parrawhite persuasion—better, far better one good bird in the hand than a score of possible birds in the bush.

===Chinese===
她终于把那半页大裁纸握在了手里——马拉索普太太被逼得失魂落魄、又明白从自己亲生女儿那里断然讨不到半分体恤的时候，便把这纸的事吐露给了她。埃丝特是个脑子快、悟性高的女人，遗嘱从头到尾，目光扫过一遍，便已把它的分量掂了个透。那双眼睛随即朝不省人事的普拉特一转，闪过一丝轻蔑——她无论如何可不学他那愚蠢的样儿，去下那样大的赌注——不，她要趁日头最烈的时候把草晒足！她信的是帕拉怀特那一套道理——一只稳稳到手的好鸟，强过林子里二十只没影儿的鸟，强得多。

===Original===
She presently restored the five documents to the stout envelope, picked up her other belongings, and without so much as a glance at Pratt, left the room. She turned the key in the door and took it away with her. And now she went straight to a certain sitting-room which Mrs. Mallathorpe had tenanted by day ever since her illness. The final and most important stage of Esther’s venture was at hand.

===Chinese===
她随即将五份文件放回那只厚实的封套，拾起其余随身物件，看也不看普拉特一眼，出了屋子。她把钥匙在门锁里一转，拔下来带在身上。接着便径直去到一间起居室——自打病倒以来，马拉索普太太白日里便一直栖身在那里。埃丝特这桩买卖的最后一关、也是最要紧的一关，就在眼前了。

===Original===
Mrs. Mallathorpe sat at an open window, wearily gazing out on the park. Ever since her son’s death she had remained in a more or less torpid condition, rarely talking to any person except Esther Mawson: it had been manifest from the first that her daughter’s presence distressed and irritated her, and by the doctor’s advice Nesta had gone to her as little as possible, while taking every care to guard her and see to her comfort. All day long she sat brooding—and only Esther Mawson, now for some time in her full confidence, knew that her brooding was rapidly developing into a monomania. Mrs. Mallathorpe, indeed, had but one thought in her mind—the eventual circumventing of Pratt, and the destruction of John Mallathorpe’s will.

===Chinese===
马拉索普太太坐在一扇敞开的窗前，倦怠地望着外面的园子。自打儿子死后，她便一直处在一种近乎麻木的状态里，除了埃丝特·莫森，难得同任何人说话：从一开始就明摆着，女儿在跟前只会叫她烦乱气恼，因此依着医生的嘱咐，妮斯塔尽量少到她跟前去，只是处处留心看护着她，照料她的起居安乐。她整日枯坐愁思——而只有早已博得她全心信任的埃丝特·莫森知道，这份愁思正飞快地熬成一种偏执的痴念。的确，马拉索普太太心里只剩下一个念头——终有一日要制住普拉特，毁掉约翰·马拉索普的遗嘱。

===Original===
She turned slowly as the maid came in and carefully closed the door behind her, and her voice was irritable and querulous as she at once began to complain.

===Chinese===
女仆进来，随手仔细掩上身后的房门，她这才缓缓转过身来，立刻开口抱怨，嗓音又急又带怨气。

===Original===
“You’ve never been near me for two hours!” she said. “Your dinner time was over long since! I might have been wanting all sorts of things for aught you cared!”

===Chinese===
“你足有两个钟头没上我跟前来了！”她说。“你的饭时早就过了！我说不定正缺这少那呢，你倒好，全不放在心上！”

===Original===
“I’ve had something else to do—for you!” retorted Esther, coming close to her mistress. “Listen, now!—I’ve got it!”

===Chinese===
“我是另有事情要办——为的是你！”埃丝特顶了回去，一面凑到女主人跟前。“你听着！——东西到手了！”

===Original===
Mrs. Mallathorpe’s attitude and manner suddenly changed. She caught sight of the packet of papers in the woman’s hand, and at once sprang to her feet, white and trembling. Instinctively she held out her own hands and moved a little nearer to the maid. And Esther quickly put the table between them, and shook her head.

===Chinese===
马拉索普太太的神情举止陡然一变。她一眼瞥见那女人手里的一叠文书，随即霍地站起身来，脸色发白，浑身发抖。她本能地伸出双手，朝女仆挪近了几步。埃丝特却迅速把一张桌子横在两人之间，摇了摇头。

===Original===
“No—no!” she exclaimed. “No handling of anything—yet! You keep your hands off! You were ready enough to bargain with Pratt—now you’ll have to bargain with me. But I’m not such a fool as he was—I’ll take cash down, and be done with it.”

===Chinese===
“不行——不行！”她叫道。“什么都先不许碰——先说好！你的手给我放规矩些！你跟普拉特讨价还价倒是痛快——这会儿轮到跟我讲了。可我没他那么傻——我要当场付清的现钱，付完就两清。”

===Original===
Mrs. Mallathorpe rested her trembling hands on the table and bent forward across it.

===Chinese===
马拉索普太太把颤抖的双手撑在桌上，隔着桌子俯身向前。

===Original===
“Is it—is it—really—the will?” she whispered hoarsely.

===Chinese===
“这——这——当真是——那份遗嘱？”她声音嘶哑地低声问。

===Original===
Instead of replying in words, Esther, taking care to keep at a safe distance behind the table, and with the door only a yard or two in her rear, drew out the documents one by one and held them up.

===Chinese===
埃丝特不用言语回答，只是小心地隔着桌子守住一段稳妥距离——身后离门不过一两步远——把那几份文件一份一份抽出来，举了举。

===Original===
“The will!” she said. “Your letter to Pratt. The power of attorney. Two papers that he brought for you to sign. That’s the lot! And now, as I said, we’ll bargain.”

===Chinese===
“遗嘱！”她说。“你写给普拉特的信。授权委托书。还有他要你签字的那两份文书。全在这儿了！好了，我说过，咱们来谈价钱。”

===Original===
“Where is—he?” asked Mrs. Mallathorpe. “How—how did you get them? Does he know—did he give them up?”

===Chinese===
“他在——在哪儿？”马拉索普太太问。“你——你是怎么拿到手的？他知道不知道——是他交出来的么？”

===Original===
“If you want to know, he’s safe and sound asleep in one of the rooms in the old part of the house,” answered Esther. “I drugged him. There’s something afoot—something gone wrong with his schemes—at Barford, and he came here on his way—elsewhere. And so—I took the chance. Now then—what are you going to give me?”

===Chinese===
“你要知道，就告诉你：他这会儿正在老宅那边的屋子里睡得又香又沉，”埃丝特答道。“我给他下了药。巴福德那边出了事——他的算盘砸了——他是路过这儿，往别处去。所以嘛——我逮着了这个机会。好了——你打算给我什么？”

===Original===
Mrs. Mallathorpe, whose nervous agitation was becoming more and more marked, wrung her hands.

===Chinese===
马拉索普太太的焦躁不安愈发压不住了，她绞着双手。

===Original===
“I’ve nothing to give!” she cried. “You know very well he’s had the management of everything—I don’t know how things are—”

===Chinese===
“我没什么可给的！”她叫道。“你又不是不知道，样样都攥在他手里——如今事情成了什么光景，我都不知道——”

===Original===
“Stuff!” exclaimed Esther. “I know better than that. You’ve a lot of ready money in that desk there—you know you drew a lot out of the bank some time ago, and it’s there now. You kept it for a contingency—the contingency’s here. And—you’ve your rings—the diamond and ruby rings—I know what they’re worth! Come on, now—I mean to have the whole lot, so it’s no use hesitating.”

===Chinese===
“废话！”埃丝特叫道。“这话骗不了我。你那张书桌里就放着一大笔现钱——前些日子你从银行提了一大笔款子，你自己清楚，这会儿就搁在里头。你留着它防备万一——如今万一来了。还有——你有你的戒指——钻石戒指、红宝石戒指——值多少钱我心里有数！来吧——我全都要，你还犹豫什么，犹豫也没用。”

===Original===
Mrs. Mallathorpe looked at the maid’s bold and resolute eyes—and then at the papers. And she glanced from eyes and papers to a bright fire which burned in the grate close by.

===Chinese===
马拉索普太太望了望女仆那双放肆决绝的眼睛——又望了望那叠文书。她的目光又从眼睛和文书上移开，落到近旁炉膛里烧得正旺的一炉火上。

===Original===
“You’ll give everything up?” she asked nervously.

===Chinese===
“你肯全都交出来？”她忐忑地问。

===Original===
“Put those banknotes that you’ve got in your desk, and those rings that are in your jewel-case, on the table between us,” answered Esther, “and I’ll hand over these papers on the instant! I’m not going to be such a fool as to keep them—not I! Come on, now!—isn’t this the chance you’ve wanted?”

===Chinese===
“把你书桌里的那些钞票，首饰盒里的那些戒指，搁到咱们中间这张桌上，”埃丝特答道，“我立刻把这些文书交到你手里！我可不会傻到把它们留在手上——我才不呢！来吧！——这不正是你盼着的机会么？”

===Original===
Mrs. Mallathorpe drew a small bunch of keys from her gown, and went over to the desk which Esther had pointed to. Within a minute she was back again at the table, a roll of bank notes in one hand, half a dozen magnificent rings in the other. She put both hands halfway across and unclasped them. And Esther Mawson, with a light laugh, threw the papers over the table, and hastily swept their price into her handbag.

===Chinese===
马拉索普太太从衣襟里摸出一小串钥匙，走到埃丝特指的那张书桌跟前。不到一分钟，她便又回到了桌边，一手拿着一卷钞票，一手拿着六枚光华夺目的戒指。她把双手往桌对面递到一半，摊开了手指。埃丝特·莫森轻轻一笑，把那叠文书隔着桌子抛了过去，随即慌忙把它们的身价扫进了自己的挂包。

===Original===
Mrs. Mallathorpe’s nerves suddenly became steady. With a deep sigh she caught up the various documents and looked them quickly and thoroughly over. Then she tore them into fragments and flung the fragments in the fire—and as they blazed up, she turned and looked at Esther Mawson in a way which made Esther shrink a little. But she was already at the door—and she opened it and walked out and down the stair.

===Chinese===
马拉索普太太的神经陡然安定下来。她深深叹了口气，抓起那几份文书，飞快而彻底地逐一验看。然后便把它们撕成碎片，把碎片掷进火里——碎片腾起火苗的当口，她转过身来，那样地望了埃丝特·莫森一眼，望得埃丝特不由往后缩了缩。可她人已经到了门口——她拉开门，走出去，下了楼梯。

===Original===
She was halfway across the hall beneath, where the butler and one of the footmen were idly talking, when a sharp cry from above made her then look up. Mrs. Mallathorpe, suddenly restored to life and energy, was leaning over the balustrade.

===Chinese===
她正走到下面大厅一半的地方——管家和一个男仆正在那儿闲谈——头顶上一声尖利的喊叫使她抬起了头。马拉索普太太霎时间还了魂、来了精神，正伏在栏杆上。

===Original===
“Stop that woman, you men!” she said. “Seize her! Fasten her up!—lock the door wherever you put her! She’s stolen my rings, and a lot of money out of my desk! And telephone instantly to Barford, and tell them to send the police here—at once!”

===Chinese===
“拦住那个女人，你们几个！”她说。“抓住她！把她关起来！——不管关在哪儿，都锁上门！她偷了我的戒指，还偷了我书桌里的一大笔钱！马上打电话到巴福德，叫他们派警察到这儿来——立刻！”

## XXVIII. The Woman in Black / 二十八 黑衣女人

===Original===
Nesta Mallathorpe, who had just arrived in Barford when Eldrick caught sight of her, was seriously startled as he and Collingwood came running up to her carriage. The solicitor entered it without ceremony or explanation, and turning to the coachman bade him drive back to Normandale as fast as he could make his horses go. Meanwhile Collingwood turned to Nesta. “Don’t be alarmed!” he said. “Something is happening at the Grange—your mother has just telephoned to the police here to go there at once—there they are—in front of us, in that car!”

===Chinese===
埃尔德里克望见妮斯塔·马拉索普的时候，她刚到巴福德；见他同科林伍德朝马车跑来，她着实吓了一跳。这位律师不拘礼数、也不及解释便登上了车，转身吩咐车夫，叫他扬鞭赶马，尽最快的脚程返回诺曼代尔。科林伍德则转向妮斯塔。“别慌！”他说。“庄园里出事了——你母亲刚刚打电话给这儿的警察，叫他们立刻赶去——喏，就在前头——咱们前面那辆汽车里！”

===Original===
“Did my mother say if she was in danger?” demanded Nesta.

===Chinese===
“我母亲说过她有没有危险吗？”妮斯塔急问。

===Original===
“She can’t be!” exclaimed Eldrick, turning from the coachman, as the horses were whipped round and the carriage moved off. “She evidently gave orders for the message. No—Pratt’s there! And—but of course, you don’t know—the police want Pratt. They’ve been searching for him since noon. He’s wanted for murder!”

===Chinese===
“她不可能有危险！”埃尔德里克嚷道，一面从车夫那边转过身来——马鞭一扬，马车掉头动了。“那口信分明是她吩咐发的。不——是普拉特在那儿！还有——不过你们自然还不知道——警方在缉拿普拉特。从中午起他们就四处搜他了。他是一桩命案的通缉犯！”

===Original===
“Don’t frighten Miss Mallathorpe,” said Collingwood. “The murder has nothing to do with present events,” he went on reassuringly. “It’s something that happened some time ago. Don’t be afraid about your mother—there are plenty of people round her, you know.”

===Chinese===
“别吓着马拉索普小姐，”科林伍德说。“那桩命案同眼下的事不相干，”他接着宽慰道。“是早先发生的事。别为你母亲担心——她身边有的是人，你知道的。”

===Original===
“I can’t help feeling anxious if Pratt is there,” she answered. “How did he come to be there? It’s not an hour since I left home. This is all some of Esther Mawson’s work! And we shall have to wait nearly an hour before we know what is going on!—it’s all uphill work to Normandale, and the horses can’t do it in the time.”

===Chinese===
“只要普拉特在那儿，我就没法不悬着心，”她答道。“他怎么会跑到那儿去的？我离家还不到一个钟头呢。这准是埃丝特·莫森捣的鬼！可咱们得等上差不多一个钟头才能知道出了什么事！——去诺曼代尔一路上全是上坡，马儿赶不及的。”

===Original===
“Eldrick!” said Collingwood, as the carriage came abreast of the Central Station and a long line of motorcars. “Stop the coachman! Let’s get one of those cars—we shall get to Normandale twice as quickly. The main thing is to relieve Miss Mallathorpe of anxiety. Now!” he went on, as they hastily left the carriage and transferred themselves to a car quickly scented by Eldrick as the most promising of the lot. “Tell the driver to go as fast as he can—the other car’s not very far in front—tell him to catch it up.”

===Chinese===
“埃尔德里克！”科林伍德喊道——马车这时正同中央车站和一长列汽车并齐。“叫停车夫！咱们雇一辆汽车——到诺曼代尔能快上一倍。顶要紧的是别再叫马拉索普小姐悬心。好啦！”他们匆匆下了马车，转上一辆汽车——埃尔德里克眼尖，一下就相中了那一排里最有指望的一辆——他又接着说。“叫司机尽管开快——前头那辆车没走多远——叫他追上去。”

===Original===
Eldrick leaned over and gave his orders.

===Chinese===
埃尔德里克探过身去，把话吩咐了。

===Original===
“I’ve told him not only to catch him up, but to get in front of ’em,” he said, settling down again in his seat. “This is a better car than theirs, and we shall be there first. Now, Miss Mallathorpe, don’t you bother—this is probably going to be the clearing-up point of everything. One feels certain, at any rate—Pratt has reached the end of his tether!”

===Chinese===
“我吩咐他不光要追上他们，还要赶到他们前头去，”他说着，重新在座位里坐定。“咱们这辆车比他们的好，准能先到。好了，马拉索普小姐，您甭操心——多半就要到水落石出的当口了。反正有一点是可以断定的——普拉特已经到了山穷水尽的地步！”

===Original===
“If I seem to bother,” replied Nesta, “it’s because I know that he and Esther Mawson are at Normandale—working mischief.”

===Chinese===
“我要是显得操心，”妮斯塔答道，“那是因为我知道他同埃丝特·莫森都在诺曼代尔——在那儿使坏。”

===Original===
“We shall be there in half an hour,” said Collingwood, as their own car ran past that in which the detectives and Byner were seated. “They can’t do much mischief in that time.”

===Chinese===
“半个钟头就到了，”科林伍德说——他们这辆车正从侦探们和拜纳坐的那辆旁边赶过去。“这点工夫他们使不了什么坏。”

===Original===
None of the three spoke again until the car pulled up suddenly at the gates of Normandale Park. The lodge-keeper, an old man, coming out to open them, approached the door of the car on seeing Nesta within.

===Chinese===
三个人再没开口，直到汽车在诺曼代尔园子大门口蓦地停住。看门老头出来开门，一眼看见妮斯塔坐在车里，便凑到车门前。

===Original===
“There’s a young woman just gone up to the house that wants to see you very particular, miss,” he said. “I tell’d her that you’d gone to Barford, but she said she’d come a long way, and she’d wait till you come back. She’s going across the park there—crossin’ yon path.”

===Chinese===
“小姐，方才有位年轻太太刚上宅子里去了，说是有顶要紧的事，非要见您不可，”他说。“俺跟她说您去了巴福德，可她说她打远道来的，等您回来就是。她正打那边园子里穿过去——抄着那条小道走呢。”

===Original===
